# TOOL CAMPAIGN — one tool per agent

The queue for auditing every tool against the live build. One row per tool, one
agent per tool, no agent touches a file it does not own.

**The rule.** A tool is verified only by measuring **what the user sees, and what
the file contains.** Both halves or neither:

- **browser evidence** — a real Chromium tab against a live dev server (started
  *detached*: `(nohup npx vite --port 52xx --strictPort >/tmp/xx.log 2>&1 &)`),
  a screenshot of the tool holding real content, and ink measured **off the
  canvas bitmap** — never inferred from a node count or a reported total.
- **a byte-level check of the exported file** — open the download with PyMuPDF
  and measure what the file itself draws (`get_pixmap` ink vs. the untouched
  source), not merely that an object exists in it.

A green unit run is neither half. Every assertion written around this area so far
has been about **counts and presence** — blocks extracted, marks added, objects
written — and never about output. That is the same shape as the markup bowtie,
the vacuous image compression and the zero-ink link: `edit-pdf` once shipped a
`area=0` mark with 1,400 passing tests. The symptom was real; the tests were
blind.

**Status** — `unexamined` → `defect-found` → `fixed+deployed` → `verified-good`.
A row may only reach `verified-good` with both evidence halves recorded.
`blocker:` in the last column means the true cause sits in a shared module the
tool's owner must not edit without a decision; the fix is sequenced, not
improvised.

Harness beside this file: `research/audit3-cdp.mjs` (CDP driver),
`research/audit3-sweep.mjs` (canvas ink + exported bytes),
`research/audit3-bytescan.py` (file-side measure),
`research/text-campaign-repro.mjs` (edit-pdf-text: load → open block → export →
dump the content stream), `research/text-campaign-dark.mjs` (same, dark mode),
`research/split-campaign.mjs` (split: every mode and every selection string in
one Chromium run per case — the mode chosen first and read back out of
`aria-pressed`, each other field read back before the click, every produced file
and the archive's own entries captured through `downloadBlob`'s object URL, plus
a pane and a result screenshot per case), `research/organize-campaign.mjs`
(organize: geometry, per-card thumbnail ink, a
real HTML5 drag with the gap indicator read mid-flight, the export captured from
`createObjectURL`), `research/deskew-fixture.mjs` + `research/deskew-residual.py`
(deskew: a text page turned by a known user-space angle, then what angle the
produced file carries — text lines and pixels must agree, or it exits 1),
`research/organize-campaign-bytes.py` (compares that file
with the plan the grid held — order by first line per sheet, rotation as
*source /Rotate + the plan's turn*, blanks asserted empty; exits 1 on any
mismatch, so it is a check and not a printout),
`research/rasterize-fixture.mjs` + `research/rasterize-ink.py` (any tool that
rasterises: writes the four ink cases — text, photograph, one hairline, empty —
and a `/Rotate 90` sheet whose bands land on the displayed top and right, then
measures a produced file's non-white **count**, its image XObject's pixel size,
its displayed box, its banded edges and the ink's centre of mass; with
`--compare SRC.pdf` it exits 1 unless inked sheets still carry ink, in the same
shape and aspect, and an empty one is still empty),
`research/pdf-to-image-fixture.mjs` + `research/pdf-to-image-controls.mjs` +
`research/pdf-to-image-ink.py` (pdf-to-image: five sheets that differ in **box as
well as ink**, so "identical byte counts" can be told apart from "one page N
times"; a probe that opens the rail's "N more options", clicks the format
segment, writes Resolution / JPEG quality / Pages and reads each back out of the
DOM before the run — what `rail-tool-bytes.mjs`, which clicks with defaults,
cannot reach; and a checker that compares every produced image's size with the
page box at the dpi asked for, its ink with the same source page rasterised by
PyMuPDF at that dpi, and the selection's files with each other by SHA-256,
exiting 1 on any mismatch),
`research/colour-fixture.mjs` + `research/colour-rail.mjs` +
`research/colour-ink.py` + `research/colour-measure.sh` (the colour tools: a sheet
whose regions have *known colour* — a solid bar, clean paper, three squares and two
flat greys either side of the contrast pivot, one empty control, and the same three displayed regions on a sheet carrying `/Rotate 90` — because ink
presence cannot judge an inverter; a probe that writes the rail's sliders, checkboxes
and segmented Method and reads every value back out of the DOM and `aria-pressed`
before the run; a checker that predicts each region through the CSS filter formula and
scores the answer against doing-nothing and against the reverse setting; and a driver
that runs all four consumers of `RenderOptions.filter` — including `pdf-to-greyscale`'s
re-render, whose segment it presses — and finishes by re-asserting the
same checks against files that must NOT satisfy them — five controls, and if one of
those passes, the script says `CONTROL BROKEN`; a case may also be declared `OPEN`, meaning a
defect this campaign measured in a file it does not own, whose failure is expected and whose
passing is reported as news (no case is `OPEN` as of `128a247`, and the greyscale one
carries an identity and a page-selection case beside it because a check that only asks
"is there ink" passed the reverted run's empty-sheet control for the wrong reason),
`research/ocr-fixture.mjs` + `research/ocr-cause-probe.mjs` +
`research/ocr-round-trip.mjs` + `research/ocr-check.py` +
`research/ocr-engine-blocked.mjs` (ocr-pdf: a sheet whose six lines are planted
verbatim, one with ink and no letter shapes, one empty; a probe that measures the
canvas *the tool hands the reader* — at the `renderPage` settle and again after
`finished` — plus what the engine itself says about a canvas the probe painted,
which is how a blank-read is told from a missing reader; a **cross-tool** round
trip that rasterises the planted page with the shipped `rasterize-pdf` and makes
the shipped `ocr-pdf` return the words, scored by difflib ratio and word F1 over
the exact strings and checked page by page out of the downloaded file; and a run
with the model hosts and the wasm CDN blocked, which is the only way to measure
the "engine unavailable" sentence rather than assert it in a unit test),
`research/ocr-digitize-round.mjs` (digitize-document: three or four photos of known
content — copy, a gradient, a flat grey block, bare paper — in one run, so a silent
page with ink is measured beside a blank one, with the card's own warning count read
back against its bullets), `research/ocr-faint-line-probe.mjs` (any pre-processing in
front of the reader: ten painted canvases, from a line 2 % below the paper through a
uniform sheet and a jittering blank one, each measured before and after the stretch
and then handed to the shipped engine — ten cases, one of which reads the words and
one of which must not),
`research/text-tail-rig.test.ts` + `research/text-tail.vitest.config.ts` +
`research/text-tail-measure.py` (txt-to-pdf: a text file whose characters can be
counted — 120 numbered rows with markers at 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 and the final line —
run through the shipped tool and judged by MuPDF for **which markers land inside a
page box**, not whether the content stream mentions them; the same fixtures are
written on both sides of a change and committed as `text-tail-{before,after}.txt`,
and `text-tail-deployed.txt` is the same question asked of the deployed download),
`docs/research/table-list-tail-rig.test.ts` + `table-list-tail.vitest.config.ts` + `table-list-tail-measure.py` (the branches `text-tail` left standing: the same five markers planted at 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 % and the final text of **one table row, one list item, one heading and one picture caption taller than a sheet**, on every converter that reaches them — `csv`, `json`, `markdown`, `html`, `xml`, `word` — plus a wide 12-column sheet whose text lines are counted per baseline, because a row that paginates while printing all its lines on one y is still broken. Judged by MuPDF for what lands inside a media box, written as `table-list-tail-{before,after}.txt`; `table-list-tail-deploy.py` re-measures a deployed download and exits 1 unless its characters reconcile exactly),
`research/compare-fixture.mjs` + `research/compare-rail.mjs` +
`research/compare-measure.py` + `research/compare-measure.sh` (compare-pdfs: two
documents whose only difference is **one solid 340×40 pt block planted at
40,60 on sheet 1**, a second sheet both files carry unchanged, a sheet the source
leaves empty, and a blank-only pair — because the question this tool has to answer
is *where*, not *whether*; a probe that loads TWO files (this route mounts no rail
form, so the only action is the panel's own button), reads the verdict out of the
DOM and the ink out of each canvas bitmap — `opaque`, `ink` and red-mark counts
kept apart, since a canvas nobody drew into is transparent black and a naive ink
count reads that as a full page — optionally turns a page and back (`WARM=1`, the
move that separates "this pane cannot compute a verdict" from "its first render
never reached a canvas"), and writes the download; a checker that measures the
exported overlay's bounding box **in displayed page points** against the planted
rectangle, the page's ink against its own source sheet at the same density, and
the pane's published numbers against the same rectangle, so both answers are
judged by one standard; and a driver whose last block re-runs those assertions
against files that must NOT satisfy them — five controls),
`research/encrypted-inputs-run.sh` + `research/encrypted-inputs-{fixtures.py,rig.test.ts,canvas.ts,readers.py,table.py,vitest.config.ts}`
(encrypted inputs: a rig that locks one countable document with qpdf and with
MuPDF at three strengths and in both kinds of secret, verifies each file with the
tool that wrote it, asks this app twelve questions per file through the shipped
runner, and then judges every produced byte with MuPDF, qpdf, poppler, pypdf and
Ghostscript — verdicts `preserved` / `silent-loss` / `unreadable`, never "it did
not throw"; it lives on its own vitest config because CI's `include` is `tests/**`,
and it writes `research/encrypted-inputs-{fixtures,app,readers}.log` plus
`-table.md` so a later pass re-measures the same way; the write-up is
`research/encrypted-inputs-findings.md`).

| tool | status | cause / evidence | owner seam |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `digital-sign-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The digest was already right; the visible block painted nothing, the dictionary lied about who signed, and the second signature was reported as if it were the first.** Measured first on the live build with the committed probes (`docs/research/verify-digital-sign-run.mjs`, judged by `docs/research/verify-digital-sign-read.py --tamper`), on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` with a real X.509 (`CN=BrowserSnip Triage`): the signature itself was sound — `/SubFilter /adbe.pkcs7.detached`, `/ByteRange [0 3875 20259 709]` tiling a 20 968 B file around its 16 384 B `/Contents` placeholder, `openssl cms -verify` **CMS Verification successful**, and tampering the first span, its last signed byte or the second span each gave **exit 4 verification failure** (flipping a placeholder byte stayed exit 0 — the control).<br>**Defect 1 — "Show a visible signature block" painted nothing.** The widget carried `/Rect` and no `/AP`, so a reader had nothing to draw: page-1 dark px at 100 dpi were **91 043 source and 91 043 signed (delta 0)**, the widget's own clip 62 133 / 62 133, and no name or date was in the page text. It now writes a Form-XObject appearance (`/BBox` equal to the rect, Helvetica + Helvetica-Bold resources, white ground, rule, *"Digitally signed by"*, the signer, the UTC instant, the reason and the location): after, page 2 goes **72 817 → 63 638** dark px while the unmarked page 1 stays 91 043 and the empty page 3 stays 0, MuPDF extracts the name and date as text, and the render was looked at (`/tmp/r/dig-final-mark.png`). `visible: false` still writes no `/AP` and 0 pt² of ink, so the block is a control and not a constant.<br>**Defect 2 — the dictionary named the field, not the signer.** `/Name` was `/Signature8563` (the random *field* name, which PDF 32000-1 Table 252 says is the signer) and `/Reason`, `/Location`, `/ContactInfo` were written as PDF **names** (`/I#20approve#20this#20document`, and a bare `/` for an empty box). All three are UTF-16 text strings now, `/Name` is the certificate CN, and an empty box is omitted. The widget also had no `/Parent`, so poppler read it as a second signature — `pdfsig` printed *"was asked for widget and didn't had one"*, *"Bad bounding box for annotation"*, listed **2 signatures for 1 field** and exited 2. With `/Parent` set (and `/FT`,`/T`,`/V` left to the field) `pdfsig` says **"Signature is Valid." / "Total document signed" / "Certificate issuer is unknown"** and exits 0, and `pypdf` reads one field instead of `/Signature1` + `/Signature1./Signature1`.<br>**Defect 3 — the second signature was reported as if it were the first.** Signing rewrites the file, so the signature already in it dies; the sentence still said only *"… verifies against those bytes"*. Measured: on a re-sign, `pdfsig` reads the first `Digest Mismatch`, `openssl` exits 4 on its range, and the card said nothing. The run now re-reads every signature and warns *"The signature that was already in this document no longer match these bytes …"* (measured on the screen, `/tmp/r/dig-twice.pdf`); field names are sequenced (`/Signature1`, `/Signature2`) instead of random, so two fields cannot collide.<br>**Defect 4 — a locked input was half-signed and sold as valid.** The runner re-seals an encrypted copy *after* signing, which ciphers the `/Contents` the digest was taken over: `readSignatures` answered `digestMatches: false` (`Only 8, 16, 24, or 32 bits supported: 48`), `pdfinfo` answered *"Incorrect password"*, and the card claimed it verified. Same on a permissions-only file. Encrypted inputs are now refused with the reason (*"… is encrypted, and a signed copy of an encrypted file is re-encrypted after the signature is made … Unlock it first (Remove password)"*) and the card carries the note. A sheet smaller than the block was also written off the paper (`[36,-26,256,64]` on 150×100 → two edges and the border clipped); it is shrunk and clamped now (`[4,4,146,94]`).<br>**Red without the fix: 5 failed / 3 passed** with `src/lib/pdf/core/signature.ts` and `src/lib/pdf/tools/security.ts` stashed (block paints, invisible control, earlier-signature warning, small sheet, locked refusal). Full suite 3 432 passed / 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0; pane read in dark mode (`/tmp/r/dig-final-pane.png`), no console error but a repeated 500 on an `#/tool/...` navigation in every run (also present before the fix).<br>**Left open, measured:** a true incremental update is not done — the first signature is *reported* dead, not *kept* (the fork has `takeSnapshot`/`saveIncremental`, so this is reachable, but it changes every byte-range assertion); signing could keep an encrypted file working the same way, by laying the CMS down as ciphertext; the white block is opaque, so it covers what it is placed over (page 2 lost 9 179 px of its own band — expected for a signature block, unmentioned on the card). | `src/lib/pdf/core/signature.ts` (the appearance stream, `/Parent`, text-string `/Name`·`/Reason`·`/Location`·`/ContactInfo`, the widget refactor, `nextFieldName`, the small-sheet clamp), `src/lib/pdf/tools/security.ts` (the earlier-signature warning, the encrypted-input refusal, the note), `tests/pdf/digital-sign-bytes.test.ts`, `docs/research/digital-r64-{before,after}.log` |
| `optimize-pdf` | measured-clean on one fixture, half the case list open (dev build `:5271`) | **The rebuild draws the same picture and the checker is an equality, per page, not a prose ratio.** Driven on `http://localhost:5271#/tool/optimize-pdf` through the committed probe with `LABEL='^Optimize$'` and its defaults, on `/tmp/triage/uncompressed.pdf` (the colour fixture with every stream stored raw, 3 835 B): the card read *"Losslessly tidied 3.7 KB → 2.3 KB (-38%). no uncompressed stream was large enough to be worth compressing; no unreferenced objects were found."* and handed back 2 371 B. `docs/research/optimize-lossless.py --src … --out …` at 150 dpi answered **VERDICT IDENTICAL, 0 failures**: per page equal ink pixel count and equal SHA-256 of the whole RGB raster (`1 044 940` total both sides), equal character count and equal text digest (`1 792` both), equal `rect`/`mediaBox`/`cropBox`/rotation (page 4 kept its `595×420` and `/Rotate 90`), equal `/Annots`, links, image list, and at document level equal `/Info` keys **and values** (title, creator, producer), equal outline, equal embedded files, and **zero dangling `N 0 R`** in the output. Screen half: the result card is not blank — the page-1 preview is inked, the four-thumbnail strip shows sheet 3 empty and sheet 4 turned, both as the file has them, and the sentence matches the numbers on its own card. **The one thing the message gets wrong is not the bytes:** `deflateAll` skips any stream under 512 B (`optimize.ts:678`), so on a 3.8 KB four-page file *every* stream is skipped and the tool tells the user no stream *"was large enough to be worth compressing"* while claiming a 38 % saving from the object-stream rebuild alone — true on this file, but the fixture cannot discriminate `compressStreams` from the rebuild, and the triage row records the same shape at 79.6 KB (*"Already tight"*, identical bytes). **A toggle flip on a fixture with a >512 B raw stream is the missing discriminator, and it was not driven.** Left open, measured and not fixed: (1) `compressStreams=…` and the all-four-off control were not run (runner loss), so control #2 of the round is unproven for this tool; (2) no fixture here carries an image XObject, so the `/Filter`-unchanged half of claim 2 is asserted only by the checker's code path, not by a file; (3) the 512 B floor is named on the card only as `no uncompressed stream was large enough`; (4) the growing-rebuild branch (`keepSmaller`) was not reached — every fixture pair tested shrank. | no `src/` change. Harness: `docs/research/optimize-lossless.py` (new, the equality checker) and the `optimize-pdf` label entry in `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` — measured, `FIELDS='stripMetadata=toggle'` reported **NO CONTROL** before that entry existed, so every toggle run of this tool silently went ahead on its default. Probe output `/tmp/r023/before-uncompressed.{pdf,png}`; controls (self-pair, planted text, planted image) started and are the open item above |
| `pdf-compress` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The screenshots and the size numbers were already right; three of the four decode paths threw the picture away under them.** Every image in `/tmp/triage/in/heavy.pdf` is JPEG, so the brief's fixture only exercises one decoder. The others were measured on four hand-written shapes (`docs/research/compress-decode-forms.py` builds them as PDF 1.5 and judges them: page box, ink at 72 dpi, the stored `/Width`/`/Height`/`/Filter` of each image and the two corner samples), and read back by MuPDF. **(1) Unfiltered samples were called a format.** `/Filter` is optional and the stream is then the samples; `decodeRaster` inflated it anyway, zlib refused, and the image came back `undecodable` — 5 280 738 B in, **the identical 5 280 738 B out**, under the sentence *"1 image use a format this browser cannot decode"*. The same happened to a 26 MB 300 dpi scan (`/tmp/triage/x/scan300.pdf`, 26 103 180 B retained) and to the raw `/SMask` of a transparent logo (`loadSoftMask` had the same unconditional inflate, so `hasAlpha` stayed false and the logo became its own black box). **(2) A PNG predictor was treated as pixels.** `[/FlateDecode]` with `/DecodeParms /Predictor 12` stores each row as a difference from the row above; those bytes went straight to `putImageData`, and the tool reported *"Compressed 1.4 MB → 211 KB (-85%). 1 of 1 image(s) re-encoded"* over a page of diagonal moiré — smaller, openable, wrong, with **both edge samples (190,30,30) and (30,40,190) flat black (0,0,0)**. **(3) An `/Indexed` palette was read as a grey level.** The index went into all three channels, so a palette of 24 entries produced grey 3–23: *"Compressed 699 KB → 16.3 KB (-98%)"* over a nearly black rectangle, corners `(0,200,20)` and `(20,220,0)` both black. **After** (`compress-decode-after.log`): raw 5 280 738 → **121 698**, 1600×1100 → 1222×840 (110 dpi cap), corners 190,30,30 → 193,30,33; predicted 1 487 355 → **121 698** and the same corners, i.e. the same pixels as the raw file; indexed 715 435 → **39 079**, corners `(0,199,18)` and `(19,220,0)`; alpha-raw 7 040 927 → **1 828 481** with the `/SMask` kept and the hidden half still paper. Scan 26 103 180 → **54 747** (2480×3508 → 1240×1753, ink 562 617 → 577 311, both edge bars and every text row intact). **Already right, measured and pinned on the brief's own fixture** (12 live runs, `<127.0.0.1:5271>`): all five strengths produce different files (81 531 B in → 79 756 / 49 950 / 37 484 / 29 904 / 23 912 B), each opens in MuPDF, and every page keeps its ink (page 1 text 61 727 → 61 727, pages 2–6 122 185 → 122 185–122 367 at the same 420×300 image and aspect), the text page still has its 1 812 characters, `/Info` survives, and the carry fixture keeps its `/Annots` (1 text note + 1 link), its 3-entry outline and its `/Rotate 90` on page 2. Lossless keeps the image stream byte-identical (74 458 B, sha `4884459ab145`). A file that cannot shrink is reported honestly: the already-extreme file at balanced **grew** (23 910 → rebuild 23 910+) and the tool returned the original with *"Nothing to save … the rebuild measured 23.4 KB against the original 23.3 KB, so it was larger and the original was returned unchanged"*. `colour.pdf` is not that file — 3 904 → 2 300 B is a real 41% off a vector page, with 0 ink on its blank page 3 in both files as the control. Encrypted and 0-page inputs are refused in words (*"locked.pdf is password protected…"*, *"zero.pdf has no pages"*). Card counts match the file (79.6 KB → 36.6 KB is 81 531 → 37 484 B; `PAGES 6 → 6`), and the pane, the page-2 preview and dark mode were read: the preview agrees with MuPDF's render of the same page. **Red without the fix: 4 assertions** in `tests/pdf/optimize-bytes.test.ts` (no-`/Filter`, predictor, palette, raw `/SMask`), 28 green in both states as controls; the fixture helper grew `store` and `indexedBlocks`. **Left open, measured:** a CMYK JPEG is re-encoded as RGB, so the colour moves to the browser's decode (237,28,36 → 253,1,0 on the same pixel — hue kept, the source ICC profile not); lossless prints *"None of the 1 image(s) could be re-encoded smaller"* although nothing was attempted there; a `/Filter` array (`[/FlateDecode]`) is still skipped rather than unwrapped; 1/2/4/16-bit and CCITT/JBIG2 images are skipped; the result card draws page 1 at ~240 px and upscales, so text looks softer in the preview than in the file. | `src/lib/pdf/core/compress.ts` (`filterNames`, `decodeParms`/`unpredict`, `readPalette`/`paletteLookup`, raw-sample and raw-mask decode, the one-image sentence), `tests/pdf/optimize-bytes.test.ts`, `docs/research/compress-decode-forms.py`, `compress-decode-{before,after}.log`, shots `022-compress-{pane,image-page,image-page-dark}.png` and `022-compressed-predicted-{before,after}.png` in `research/shots/` |
| `overlay-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **Overlay, its page mapping and its four controls were already right on the pixels; "Behind (underlay)" shipped the stamp and threw the base away, and a `/Rotate` sheet got the stamp fitted to the paper it is stored on rather than the paper the reader sees.** Measured first on the live `<127.0.0.1:5271>` build with `docs/research/overlay-rail.mjs` (a two-file rail driver — `colour-rail.mjs` uploads one file and cannot reach a `multiFile` tool — every control written as a user event and read back from the DOM before the click, pane and card screenshots kept) and judged by `docs/research/overlay-ink.py` on `get_pixmap` renders, whose regions are derived from the two inputs (`stamp_only` = ink only the stamp has, `base_only` the reverse), run first on paper against paper (every region 0) so a 0 is a measurement.<br>**Already right, and pinned here so it is not re-opened:** `colour.pdf` sheet 1 + `sheets.pdf` sheet 1 (same 420×595 frame) puts **68 834 stamp-only px, 68 834 of them inked in the output (100.0 %)** at the stamp's own bbox `[42,148,293,505]`, and keeps the base's **28 196 base-only px, 28 196 still inked (100.0 %)**, text 1 779 → 1 779 characters; a stamp with no ink leaves all four sheets **pixel-identical to the base** (0 px off, at the same dpi); the mapping is by index and the overflow is *dropped with a sentence, not appended* — 4 base sheets against a 5-sheet stamp gives 4 output sheets and *"The stamp has 5 pages but the base has 4 pages, so stamp page 5 was never used"*, while `same` puts sheet 3's 1 393 characters on output sheet 3 and sheet 4's image on sheet 4, `first` puts sheet 1 everywhere (text `1779, 13, 0, 0`, no image), and `last` reproduces the base pixel for pixel because sheet 5 is empty; every control reaches the bytes — `scale=0.5` leaves **16 950** extra px in the stamp's box against 68 834 at 1× (24.6 % of it, ¼ to within rounding), `opacity=0.5` moves the same box's mean level **12.7 → 115.2**, on the card `PAGES 9 → 4` over 4 sheets; the base's 3 annotations and its link come back at their **own rects** (`Text` `[200,200,216,216]`, `FreeText` `[60,120,300,150]`, `Highlight` `[56.7,89.1,263.3,104.9]`, link `[60,300,200,316]`), the embedded `note.txt` travels, and the title survives.<br>**Defect 1 — "Behind" lost the page.** `stampBehind` emptied the sheet's `/Contents` and redrew a `embedPage` snapshot of it on top; `embedPage` only *queues* the embedder and `PDFPageEmbedder.embedIntoContext` reads `/Contents` at `save()` — by then the emptied list plus the stamp. Measured on `colour.pdf` sheet 1 with `sheets.pdf` sheet 2 as the stamp: mean level inside the stamp's box **219.5** against the base's **196.8**, **0** px darker than 120 there, **0 characters**, the base's black band gone, the output *only* the stamp (`shots/overlay-1-underlay-before.png`). `stampBehind` now sets the page's own streams aside and pushes them back after the stamp (one `Do`, no XObject), so the page's words, fonts and resources are its own: after, the same box holds **17 053** dark px and 1 816 characters extract, the band is back (`shots/overlay-2-underlay-after.png`), and the order in `/Contents` is stamp-then-page. Annotations and link survive underlay too (3 and 1). **Defect 2 — the stamp was fitted to the stored frame.** The destination came from the stored crop box and nothing turned the stamp with the sheet, so a 420×595 `/Rotate 90` base (a 595×420 landscape sheet) got a portrait stamp scaled as if the paper were portrait and *sideways*: comparing the output against the stamp placed where a reader sees it (its own display, fitted to the visible 420 pt height and centred) covered **8.7 % of 5 410 expected px and its text ran vertically** (`shots/overlay-3-rot90-before.png`). The call now passes the sheet's own turn negated (`angle = -rotationOf(page)`) — the viewer applies the rotation, so the content has to be pre-turned against it — and the same comparison covers **5 410 of 5 410 px (100.0 %)**, the text horizontal, the corner mark at the display's top-left (`shots/overlay-4-rot90-after.png`); controls: the same stamp on an unrotated sheet of the same size covers 10 972 of 10 972 (100.0 %), and the unstamped base against that same expectation covers 4 413 of 10 972 (40.2 %), so 100 % is not free. Unrotated sheets are unchanged by it (sheet 1's regions still 68 834 / 28 196 / 100 %, the blank control still 0 px off). **Copy:** "stamp pages 5 were never used" → *page 5 was*; and the shared preview caption read "Page of base_overlaid.pdf" on any one-page result (`Page${of}` with `of` empty when `total` is 1) — it names the page now.<br>**Red without the fix: 3 failed / 125 passed** with `src/lib/pdf/tools/organize.ts` stashed (underlay keeps the page, `expected '' to contain 'sheet 1'`; the drawn box on a rotated base, `expected 595 to be close to 296.5`; the singular note), plus **1 failed / 97** with the shared `OutputPreview.tsx` stashed. Fixture `colour.pdf` (sheet 2 empty at ink 0, sheet 4 stored 420×595 `/Rotate 90`, displayed 595×420 at ink 274 962) and `annot.pdf` sheet 1 (5 `/Annots` entries, 1 816 characters). **Right, and kept:** the ink lands at the angle typed, clockwise as the field says — 30° gives text lines at **30.0°** in image space on a box grown 420×595 → **661.2×725.3** with ink **1.019×** the source (nothing cut), −30° gives **−30.0°**, 90° and −90° give **90.0°** and **−90.0°** on a 595×420 sheet at 0.990×, and 1 779 characters are still there as vector text (1 779 of 1 779, no `/Subtype /Image`); a `/Rotate 90` sheet composes instead of fighting — sheet 4 + 0° comes back 595×420 at `/Rotate 0` with the source's own ink **274 962 exactly**, +30° at 725.3×661.2 and 1.001×; the empty sheet turns to 661.2×725.3 at ink **0**; unpicked sheets are copied verbatim, `/Rotate 90` intact; the keep-size control really crops (45° keep: 0.908 / 0.883 / 0.934 of the source ink) and its note prints. **Four defects, all fixed:** (1) **the document's info was thrown away silently** — `pdf-lib` copies pages and nothing else, so every output came back `title=""` against the source's own title, at every angle, even for a 1-of-4 turn where three sheets were copied verbatim; `carryDocInfo` now carries title, author, subject, keywords, creator and the XMP packet, the producer is this app's, and the card says so (`PAGE_COPY_INFO_NOTE`); an untitled file invents nothing (pinned). (2) **links and annotations went with no sentence at a right angle** — `noteRebuildLoss` was gated on “the angle is not a multiple of 90”, but every angle rebuilds: `annot.pdf` sheet 1 came back **0 annots, 0 links, 1 779 of 1 816 characters** at 30°, **and at 90° and 0° the card said nothing at all**. The gate is gone; 30°, 90° and 0° all read *5 links or annotations could not come along*. (3) **0° was sold as a turn with nothing to say** — the file is re-laid (a stored `/Rotate` baked to 0, the info and 5 annots gone) while the card read only “Turned 4 pages by 0° clockwise”, where the sibling `rotate` says a 0° run is a copy and keeps all of it; this tool now says the opposite and names `rotate`, and the angle's own help points at the cheaper lossless tool for a right angle. (4) **the pane painted the source angle while the file got the typed one, and the result card painted another file's page** — at 45° the four tiles were upright with `data-turn-to` null (`rotate-custom-1-pane-before.png`) against 45.0° in the file; `pdf.js`'s `PageViewport` only honours 0/90/180/270 (any other angle falls into its identity branch, which the old comment got wrong), so `thumbnails` now rasterises at the page's own `/Rotate` and turns the bitmap on a paper-white canvas sized for the turned box, and the pages field carries `previewFrom: 'angle'` — 45° now paints 45/45/45/135 (`rotate-custom-2-pane-after-45.png`). (2) **each section's words printed twice** (`ROTATION` over `Rotation`, `PAGES` over `Pages`), because a one-field section has the field's label as its heading; a heading is suppressed when it is the only field's own label, and both sections now read once. blank count 0 clamps to 1, 300 clamps to 200 with `PAGES 4 → 204`. **Ink:** every blank is exactly **0** non-white pixels at 50 dpi while the inked sheets keep theirs byte for byte (`33011 / 24021 / 69334` in and out, sheet 3 in at 0), so the instrument was checked against a page of paper, not only against a blank one. **Furniture:** `/Info`, annots, links, outline, embedded files were the item. 3 annots + 1 link in → 3 and 1 out; The `595×420 /Rotate 90` sheet is a `595×420 /Rotate 90` page on its own (ink 83558) and inside a part; a part that is only the empty sheet 3 comes out a **1-page 420×595 file with 0 ink**, previewed as a blank sheet, so the blank is neither dropped nor replaced by pdf-lib's filler page; on `annot.pdf` the three annotations and the one link on sheet 1 survive as three and one with ink 31361 = the source. **Refusals, all true and all measured:** blank *and* whitespace-only ranges → `DISABLED` + *Still needed: Enter at least one page range, for example 1-3, or choose a different split mode.*, `4-2` → *runs backwards — page 4 comes after page 2. Write 2-4.*, `9` (alone and second in `1-2,9`) → *refers to page 9, but this document has 4 pages*, `abc` → *is not a page number or a range like 3-5*; a one-page source under every-N gives one file and under odd/even-both one file plus *This document has no even pages, so no even-pages file was made.* **Defect 1, the numbering:** page numbers in the output names had no width, so a 12-page split wrote `twelve_page-1 … twelve_page-10 … twelve_page-12` — the archive's central directory carries page order but every name-sorted listing (Explorer, Finder, `sort`) puts sheet 10 between 1 and 2, and the card's own *Download as zip* is where the entries meet a folder. | `edit-pdf-text` (font fidelity) | fixed+deployed | **Two user reports, four measured causes, and a rig that could see them.** The full cause table, the font-retention decision matrix and the open items live in `research/edit-pdf-text-findings.md`; the numbers below are that file, condensed. The reports: *"editing a text literally draws boxes on top of current text"* and *"the font is changed"*. The first cause was **not** the cover patch: a block whose words the engine could not delete was **skipped** — the file kept the old words, the new text was never written, and the on-screen box (painted in the page's own sampled colour, as a finished edit is) sat over live text. So the fix was to make the deletion work, and the box got a state test of its own. Phase 1 measured before touching behaviour — eight documents, each produced by a different real producer (`docs/research/text-edit-fixture.py`: the campaign CV, the same page through `gs pdfwrite`, the same page with every show operator rewritten into a kerned `TJ`, the same page wrapped in Form XObjects — plain and nested — a 200 dpi scan with an invisible mode-3 text layer, a hand-built Type3, a PyMuPDF subsubset Type0/Identity-H, and a tinted sheet with artwork behind the words), 734 text blocks, each deleted on its own and then the whole pipeline run, judged off the produced file by `docs/research/text-edit-measure.py` and off the produced **paint** in Chromium by `docs/research/text-edit-probe.mjs` / `text-edit-proof.mjs`:<br>**cause · fixture · blocks before → after**<br>· **text drawn inside a Form XObject** — the walker never entered one, so a Word/Distiller page read as a page with no text: `forms.pdf` **192 of 192 blocks not deleted at all** → 0<br>· **the numbers in a `TJ` array were ignored** — `[(left) -3600 (right)] TJ` paints `right` 43 pt along; measuring it beside `left` put every rect in the wrong place: `distilled.pdf` **41 not deleted + 31 partial**, and one line measured at x 177 for words printed at x 50 → 0<br>· **showing text displaced the line matrix as well as `Tm`** — every later line of a `Tj`-per-line page slid along the page: `distilled.pdf` **31 partial** → 0<br>· **a partially covered operator was dropped whole** — editing one column deleted the column beside it: **168 blocks losing 4 196 characters that were not theirs** (2 057 on `kerned.pdf` — one column of a two-column row — 2 086 on `distilled.pdf`, 53 on `sample.pdf`) → **0 characters, 0 blocks**<br>· and one cause under all of them: **standard-14 widths were indexed by position in a dense AFM list instead of by glyph name**, so a page of non-embedded Helvetica measured ~9 % too wide (262 pt for a run that paints 242.3) — the tail of a line fell outside its own deletion box, and survivors re-embarked 28 pt to the right. Now keyed by name, and cross-checked against pdf-lib's own `widthOfTextAtSize` for the same string.<br>**Font retention.** `faces.ts` resolved bundled Noto Sans for every run, so all **734** blocks changed face. In-place editing runs on the open document, so the run's own font dictionary is now read from the file (`TextSegment.fontSource`, `removeTextInRects(...).faces` keyed by block, `pageTextFaces`) and reused when it can spell the text — proven three ways: its own code↔Unicode map, a non-zero width for that code, and the glyph coverage of the embedded program. Reused for `sample`, `forms`, `kerned`, `cid`, `paper`, `scan` (**541 of 734 blocks, verified by reading `/BaseFont` and the painted spans back out of the file**). Refused, **per block, naming the font and the reason**, for 193: 192 where the Distiller subsubset has no glyphs for the typed characters (`the embedded subset has no glyphs for: # Q Z z`) and 1 Type3 face that cannot be re-encoded; a bold/italic the face does not carry is refused the same way, and a family the user picked is honoured and *not* reported as a swap. Faces are cached per style **and per text**, because spellability is a property of the text — measuring a block in a face that cannot write it lays out words the file then drops.<br>**Honest residuals.** `removeTextInRects` now verifies its own work against the bytes it wrote and returns counts plus named reasons (`type3-font`, `unmeasured-widths`, `image-behind`, `form-reused`, `form-too-deep`, `nothing-at-rect`, `form-not-copied`); `needsRasterFallback` means *measured pixels behind the block*, which is why `cid`/`paper`/`scan`/`type3` no longer claim a raster they do not have; a form shared with another page is copied rather than edited under its feet (pinned by a test that reads page 2's title back); anything that genuinely survives is covered by a patch **sized to the surviving glyphs** (`leftoverRects`, not the block box) in the colour sampled from behind them, and the result says `N words could not be deleted and were covered … hidden, not removed`. A committed block whose state says its words are still in the file wears the warning token instead of the page's colour.<br>**Final table, same rig, both builds.** before (`b6f985b`): clean 0, not-removed 233, old words still findable in the file 57, blocks losing a neighbour's characters 168 (4 196 characters), face kept 0, patches 0, placement claims 0 (the build reported none) · after (`b4e4dcf`): **clean 541, substituted-and-named 193, not-removed 0, residual 0, collateral 0, rectangles painted 0**, and every laid-out piece found in the produced paint at its own x, baseline and size (`placed` vs the rendered file — the `stillHasOldWords`/`patches`/`piecesPainted` columns are `0`/`0`/all on every file whose face was kept). Page ink in the block's own box, e.g. `sample.pdf` heading 1 814 px before → 840 px after with 3/3 pieces painted in `Helvetica-Bold`, and `paper.pdf` 19 019 → 19 019 with 5/5 pieces in `Helvetica` — a re-typed block that keeps its face keeps its ink.<br>**Re-measured on the deployed site** (`https://www.browsersnip.com`, CI `42563214198` green, the live chunk `TextEditor-vCuQZF0J.js` carrying this row's strings, driven through the real UI with `text-campaign-repro.mjs`): `sample.pdf` → the heading block exported at `48.0` (the editor's own origin, i.e. the producer's run was deleted and redrawn) in **`Helvetica-Bold`** — the document's face, not Noto — with `0` filled rectangles on the page before and after; and `forms.pdf`, the Form-XObject document where **192 of 192 blocks were undeletable**, exports the same block the same way: `Helvetica-Bold`, 0 rectangles, page ink 61 612 → 56 282 px measured by PyMuPDF at 150 dpi over the whole sheet (the drop is the re-typed block, not a patch). Dark mode re-run on the dev server: `opaque 500 395 / dark 5 407`, `body bg rgb(18, 18, 16)`, no console error.

The overlay sat mid-page, the deletion rects were turned a second time so the engine found nothing, the block was skipped and the file came back **5 438 B in, 5 438 B out, both pages untouched** while the pane showed the rewrite. Now the run's box is pushed through the viewport transform and its own `fontSize` travels with it (on a turned page the box height is the run's *length*, so the type read back as 234.5 pt). after: the block is deleted and redrawn at **5.2 pt from 10, 3 lines, 0 collisions, neighbour 2/2 kept, `fit_ok true`** (`shots/reflow-rot.png`), and `/Rotate 90` is kept.<br>**Also carried, measured:** `/Info` title and author, **2/2** annots and **1/1** link survive every export; Reproduced on the deployed site and on the dev server, no console error. Cause: `rasterizePages` (`core/render.ts:519-540`) encodes `render.canvas` straight after `renderPage()` settles, and `renderPage` settles when the task is *scheduled* — the pixels arrive on `render.finished`, which this helper never awaits, then `render.cancel()` runs. It is sequenced item 1 below, arriving by bytes rather than by screen. Adding the one missing `await render.finished` in the same environment turned the same archive into 293 KB / 818 KB per page and 6.97 % / 23.45 % ink, alternating exactly with the fixture's photo pages — the cause, not a plausible theory. **Fixed at the cause in `6b9b479`**: one `await render.finished` in `rasterizePages`, granted narrowly by the parent (`pdf-to-cbz` is its only caller). The test double was why no test saw it — its `renderPage` returned no `finished` at all, so nothing could encode too early; it now models the ordering (the canvas is unpainted until `finished` resolves, and the encoder hands out an inked or a blank PNG), and `pdf-to-cbz`'s test compares the archived bytes against the inked sheet instead of counting entries. Red without the await, green with it. **Re-measured on the deployed site after the push**: the same fixture now archives 293 373 / 817 627 bytes per page at 7.04 % / 23.68 % non-white, alternating exactly with the photo pages, where the pre-fix deploy had measured 23 690 bytes and 0.00 % six times over, and the report line reads `6 pages rendered at 200 dpi into 2.9 MB` instead of `3.4 KB`. ComicInfo.xml is **absent**, which the format allows (`readium/architecture`: CBZ has "no mandatory extra files"); recorded, not added. **Two defects, both fixed.** (1) **The strip's `none` chip reached nothing** — `selection(..., { fallback: 'all' })` turned the emptied field back into every page, so clicking "none" produced a **byte-identical** archive to "all" (337 982 B, 4 entries, 4 pages, 3/3 runs). The fallback is gone: the same click now stops at "No pages chosen — the page list is empty, so there is nothing to convert." with "Pick pages between 1 and 4", and an untouched field still means all. (2) **There was no JPEG quality control at all** — the encoder was handed a literal `0.92` and `rasterizePages`' `quality` parameter had no caller. The field now exists (`qualityField`, hidden for PNG, step 0.01, default 0.92 so the default output is unchanged at 337 982 B) and reaches the bytes: 0.4 / 0.9 / 1.0 → **154 299 / 318 339 / 495 485 B** at the same 1166×1652 per page and ink 21.46 / 18.25 / 57.02 % against the source's 18.90 / 19.50 / 56.76 (the 0.4 ringing adds ink, it does not move the page). | `deskew-pdf` | verified-good | **Two stacked defects: an unawaited paint, and a page turned the way it already leaned.** `core/measure.ts:detectSkew` read `render.ctx.getImageData` straight after `await session.renderPage(...)` — the same missing `await render.finished` that cost `edit-pdf-text` its page and `pdf-to-cbz` its archive — so the ink list came back under 32 px, the estimator answered `{ angle: 0, confidence: 0 }`, and every tilted page was reported as nothing to do. **The await alone made it worse**, which is why it was deliberately not shipped on its own: the estimate is a **bitmap** angle (y runs *down*, so positive = text running downhill to the right — PyMuPDF's `dir` convention, and this row's script reports in it), while `applySkew` (`tools/optimize.ts`) turns the page **clockwise as it is viewed** by whatever it is handed; the call site passed the estimate un-negated and doubled the lean. The one helper that spells that negation, `deskewAngleFor`, was tested in `convert.test.ts:936` and had **no caller in `src/`** at all. Angle table — four fixtures from `docs/research/deskew-fixture.mjs` (40 text lines wrapped in one `cm`, turned −6 / −3 / +3 / +6 user-space degrees), every angle measured on the *file* by `docs/research/deskew-residual.py`, which reads the text-line directions **and** a projection profile over the page's rasterised pixels and exits 1 unless the two agree:<br>· **the inputs** measure `+6.00 / +3.00 / −3.00 / −6.00°` (the sign flips at the bitmap boundary — that reversal is the whole bug in one line)<br>· **deployed before**: report “Nothing to do: no page was tilted past 10° at a confidence of 0.35”, four times → output `+6.00 / +3.00 / −3.00 / −6.00°`, a no-op byte for byte<br>· **await only, local dev server**: report `6.00° / 3.00° / -3.00° / -6.00°` → output `+12.00 / +6.00 / −6.00 / −12.00°` — confidently wrong, turned on into the tilt<br>· **both halves, re-measured on the deployed site** (`BASE=https://www.browsersnip.com`, same probe): report `1 (tilt +6.00°, turned 6.00° counterclockwise)` · `(tilt +3.00°, turned 3.00° counterclockwise)` · `(tilt -3.00°, turned 3.00° clockwise)` · `(tilt -6.00°, turned 6.00° clockwise)` → **output `0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00°` by both methods**, ink 9.40–11.32 % still on the page, no console error<br>· **0° control**: “Nothing to do”, output unchanged at `0.00°` with 13.86 % ink — no correction invented out of anti-aliasing. Fixed at the cause in `8313e2d`, CI green on `42472071987`. The report line now names the tilt it found **and** the direction it turned, because the three angles this tool touches (the page's `/Rotate` frame, the bitmap, the correction) do not share one sign, and a bare number in the sentence is what hid the defect. Nothing to fix here; it is now re-pinned.<br>· **the rotated sheets — before**: the 90/180/270 file reported “Straightened **1** page(s)” and corrected only the `/Rotate 180` page. Pages 1 and 3 (`/Rotate 90`/`270`) came back at `lines=−3.00°, profile=7.80°` — the tilt still in the file — and were **named nowhere**: the 0.133-confidence estimate was zeroed by the estimator's own 0.15 floor, which satisfied the `|angle| ≤ 0.15` half of the skip test, so the "Not straightened" line was never written. A no-op that reports success over a page it never measured is the fake success this campaign exists to catch.<br>· **cause**: `skewFromProjectionProfile` searched only `±maxAngle` around 0. The bitmap is the *displayed* page, so under `/Rotate 90` the text is painted vertically and that window aligns columns — measured on the same page, 0.133 confidence against 0.734 upright, with the right magnitude guessed by luck. It now sweeps the same window around 0/90/180/270, keeps the sharpest peak and subtracts that quarter turn, so the answer is the tilt against the page's **own** axes — the frame `applySkew` writes into. **After**: all three pages `0.00°` by both halves, `/Rotate` kept at 90/180/270/90, the 0°-under-90 control untouched at 13.86 % ink and silent.<br>· **the low-confidence skip now speaks**: a page the estimator cannot judge is named (“page 1 could not be judged (0.00° at 0.035 confidence, below the 0.35 you asked for)”, measured on a 4-dot sheet). A page measured straight stays quiet, so the 0° control is still silent.<br>· **carry, per page, before → after**: ink darkness within 0.18 % (control exactly equal), 3640 characters of text on every page, zero image XObjects (no raster rebuild on this path), `/Info` title + author, the outline and the text annot all present in the output, annots named in the warning (“2 annotation(s) kept their old coordinates…”).<br>· **the promise said “the page size it had to grow” and the line never named it** — it does now (`page 638×872 → 683×904 pt`, matching the output's box to 0.1 pt).<br>· **instrument**: `deskew-residual.py` rasterised the *displayed* page while `lines` is the *content* frame, so the two halves disagreed on a rotated sheet by construction; the raster now comes from a copy with every `/Rotate` zeroed and the page's rotation is printed. **It called a scanned page blank — and a signature, and a link.** Three defects, all in `core/blank.ts`, all closed here. The fixture's ink was counted first: `blanks.pdf` is text 1 350 px / empty 0 / full-page scan 341 960 / invisible-OCR text 0 / link only 0 / one 0.1 pt hairline 990 / white-on-white 0 / text 1 321. **Before, on the deployed site** (`docs/research/rail-tool-bytes.mjs` → the download → PyMuPDF): the run reported `Removed 5 pages (2-3,5-7), kept 3 pages`, and the 1 149-byte file it handed back holds three sheets measuring 1 350 / 0 / 1 321 with **zero image XObjects** — the photograph and the signature were deleted beside the true blank. With `Keep pages that only hold annotations` clicked off the screen reported the identical line and the identical 1 149 bytes: the toggle moved nothing. A document that is *nothing but* scans (3 pages, 5.4 MB, 341 960 px each) was refused outright — `All 3 pages look blank, and deleting every one would leave an empty PDF` — under the tool's own note that a scanned image counts as ink. Causes: pdf.js reports an image as `paintImageXObject`, which was in `IMAGE_OPS` but **not** in `PAINTING_OPS`, so the rule's `paintOps === 0` branch fired on the one page type it must not; the same rule's second branch read "no image and no visible text" as blank, which is what deleted a page whose only ink is one vector stroke; and `keepAnnotated` was a toggle over nothing because `resolveAnnotation` rebuilt the `/Annots` entry with `context.obj(num, gen)` — in this fork the *literal* helper, which answers a `PDFNumber` — while `readName`/`readNumber` then asked a `PDFDict` for a **string** key, which is keyed by `PDFName` instances, so every annotation read as absent. **After, the same driver against the same deployed URL**: `Removed 1 page (2), kept 7 pages`, screen `PAGES 8 → 7` and `1.8 MB → 1.8 MB · unchanged`, and the 1 894 510-byte download measures 1 350 / 341 960 / 0 / 0 / 990 / 0 / 1 321 with its `/Subtype /Image` still in the file. The toggle now moves exactly one page: off → `Removed 2 pages (2,5), kept 6 pages`, 1 894 390 bytes, the same seven-sheet list minus the link sheet. The scan-only file answers `No blank pages were found in 3 pages — nothing was removed` and returns all 5 679 499 bytes with three image XObjects. What the rule refuses to call blank is pinned from both sides in `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`: an empty sheet still goes; a hairline sheet survives with `20 50 m · 180 50 l · 0.1 w · S` readable in the saved content stream; a link-only sheet is kept only while the toggle is on; and `keepAnnotated` does not keep a mark flagged `Invisible` 0x01 / `Hidden` 0x02 / `NoView` 0x20 (the masks were 2, 128 and 4 — Hidden by luck, the other two never) or a `/Rect` lying wholly outside the crop box, while `Print`, `ReadOnly`, `Locked` and a rect clipped by the page edge still count. Nine reverts of the fix were each run to confirm the intended test goes red: the raster ids, the stroke guard, `context.lookup(ref)`, the `PDFName` keys, the rect test, the flag test, full-containment in place of overlap, and the double back on `paintXObject` two ways. **Left open then, and what became of it (all three answered in round 019):** (1) every download above reads `annots = 0` where the source reads `annots = 1` — keeping the sheet keeps a page whose link object the copy dropped, silently, because the tool builds its output with `collectPages` and declares no `noteRebuildLoss`; the same rebuild loss the `organize` row records for `Save`, in the one tool that promises the page *for the annotation's sake* (`tools/organize.ts` + the page copy in `core/`); (2) two of the seven kept sheets still measure 0 ink — the invisible-OCR page and the white-on-white page — because the rule reads the display list and not colour, so `Tr 3` text and a white fill count as content; the OCR note cannot fire for the first either, since `invisibleTextItems` is inferred from a zero font matrix rather than the render mode (finding (3) of the report that opened this row: `core/blank.ts`, but a decision about what "blank" promises before it is a patch); (3) the toggle run was driven by a copy of `research/rail-tool-bytes.mjs` with the checkbox clicked — worth folding into the committed probe the next time its owner touches it.

Measured on the kept sheet: the source page's `/Annots` is `('array', '[20 0 R]')`, the download's is `('array', '[15 0 R]')`, and the object behind it is unchanged (`/A /S /URI /URI (https://example.com)`, `/Rect [60 515 200 535]`, `/Subtype /Link`, `/NM`), with `get_links()` resolving `example.com`. The rule now walks the display list's **graphics state**: `setTextRenderingMode` says whether a run draws at all (modes 3 and 7 do not, `ISO 32000-1` Table 106) and the fill/stroke colour says whether what it draws is paper; `inkOps` is what a reader can see, and both predicates key on it. The colour arrives as a three-component `Uint8ClampedArray` — `Array.isArray` answers false for it, which is how the jsdom double passed while the browser kept the sheet — so the reader accepts array-likes and the double was changed to hand over the typed buffer the worker really builds (`PartialEvaluator` normalises `rg`/`g`/`k`/`sc` through `getColour`/`getRgb` to 0–255 first, so no colour space reaches the rule unresolved; a tiling pattern keeps `setFillColorN` and counts as ink). A raster, a gradient and a pattern are ink whatever their colour. **After, same driver, same fixture, same dpi**: `Removed 3 pages (2,4,7), kept 5 pages`, `PAGES 8 → 5`, 79 632 B; the file's five sheets read 107 385 / 237 333 / 0 (with its link) / 362 / 1 635, page 3 still carries one `/Subtype /Image` XObject, and the card prints both sentences — `1 page (4) held only invisible OCR text, so nothing showed on it and it was removed — the searchable text went with it.` and `1 page (7) painted white on white … any text it holds is selectable but was never visible.` The **controls** moved the other way as they should: the same text in black on a white wash is content (`No blank pages were found`), the true blank still goes, the scan and the hairline still stay, and the toggle still gates exactly one sheet — off → `Removed 4 pages (2,4-5,7), kept 4 pages`, `PAGES 8 → 4`, 79 524 B, the link sheet gone and nothing else. **(3) The toggle is driven by the committed probe now**: `colour-rail.mjs` gained a `remove-blank-pages` label entry, so `FIELDS='keepAnnotated=toggle'` finds the box, clicks it, and **reads `checked=true → checked=false` back out of the DOM before the run** rather than a scratch copy of `rail-tool-bytes.mjs`. **(4)** Screen half, looked at: `docs/research/shots/blank-2-pane-toggle-on.png` (the pane holding `blanks.pdf … 8 pages`, the toggle ticked, the new description `Detect and delete pages that show nothing: no image, no mark, no visible text`), `blank-2-pane-toggle-off.png` (same pane, box clear), `blank-2-card-after.png` (the card: `80.5 KB → 77.8 KB · 3% smaller`, `PAGES 8 → 5`, `Removed 3 pages (2,4,7), kept 5 pages.`, both notes, a five-thumbnail strip whose third tile is the blank-looking link sheet and whose fourth shows the hairline — the file's own sheet list), and `blank-2-card-dark.png` (the same in tokens, no hex literal). Card counts match the file it handed back: 8 → 5 said, 5 pages in the download.
