The **empty control** comes back at `ink 100.00 %` with **0** mid tones and channel spread 0 — solid dark paper, no structure invented out of a filter pass. Screen half: `docs/research/shots/colour-1-invert-deployed-after.png`, the result card holding the inverted page with its copy readable, `3.4 KB → 322 KB · 9400 % larger`, `PAGES 3 → 1`, no console error.<br>**The promise the tool makes is now true, and it was true-shaped before it was true**: the rail’s own summary, file size and page count all agreed with the defect. Tests: `tests/pdf/colour-tools-ink.test.ts` (33) drives the real tool through the real `core/render.ts` against a pdf.js double that models *both* facts — the paint lands in a later task, and `resetCtxToDefault` eats a `ctx.filter` set before `render()`. It requires two distinct luminance populations with the roles swapped (a uniformly black page has one, and passes `output !== input`), and carries the control that proves the assertion is not vacuous: the same check run against a page of paper and a page of solid black fails both. **Reverting the `await` turns 23 of its 33 red; reverting the `core/render.ts` filter pass turns 15 red; reverting the identity transform in that pass turns 24 red.** <br>**Re-settled at ±2, then two leftovers taken.** A second round re-measured the same sheet with the prediction tolerance narrowed from the rig's 8 to **2** per channel (`colour-ink.py --tol`): bar 0.0 → 255.0 and band 255.0 → 0.0 (Δpred **0.0** each), copy 220.4 → 33.8 against **34.6** predicted (Δpred **0.8**), whole sheet 18.90 % → **86.90 %**; the turned sheet (4) again Δpred 0.0 and box 595.0×420, and the empty control `--solid`: page mean **0.0** against a predicted 0.0, **0** mid tones, channel spread 0. `dpi` is proportional and the page is not: 96 → 560×793 px raster, 400 → 2333×3305 (**4.166×, the asked ratio**), both pages 420×595 and `len(get_text()) == 0` on every sheet, which is what the card and the run message both say. Double inversion is a round trip, not a byte compare: inverting the 200 dpi output again returns bar 0.0 / band 255.0 exactly and copy 220.4 → 222.0 (**Δ 4.3**, two JPEG generations), box unchanged. A sheet of pure black inverts to paper and a sheet of pure white to ink, each a single tone with **0** mid tones, so neither end is a loose threshold.<br>Two defects the re-measure indicted. **(1) The produced page was the `/MediaBox`, not the displayed page.** `core/pages.ts` `displayedSize` returned `getSize()`, while pdf.js rasters `page.view` — the `/CropBox` clipped to the `/MediaBox` — so a sheet cropped to a 336×71.4 band came back as a **420×595 page holding a 933×198 picture stretched over it**: 0.77:1 against the 4.71:1 the picture is. Fixed at the cause (shared, standing rule 5: `displayedSize` now returns the view box, which is also what `CropBox ∩ MediaBox` means by §14.11.2), re-measured **420×595 → 336×71.4**, picture 933×198, ink 100 % → 0 % because the whole crop is the inverted bar. Sheet 7 is the mirror case — stored 595×420 with `/Rotate 90`, displays 420×595 — and the produced box follows the display: **420×595**, Δpred 0.0 on both regions, so the fix cannot be passed by copying either box. The 107-file suite stays green (3268 passed). **(2) The document `/Info` was thrown away.** The rebuilt document is a `newPDF()` and nothing carried the title: 3 of 3 runs before the fix answered `title: undefined`, while the card's notes named only links and annotations. `rerasterise` now carries title / author / subject / creator / producer / keywords — the same field list as `helpers.ts` `rebuildDocument`, so there is one notion of the document's metadata — and the note says so. Re-measured on `annot.pdf`: title **carried**, annots **0** and links **0**, which is what the note still promises. Tests: `tests/pdf/colour-tools-ink.test.ts` now 35, the two new ones are the box-and-aspect case (a fixture page whose `/CropBox` is the bar band; asserts the produced page is 160.0×31.2 and the picture's aspect is within 2 % of it) and the `/Info` case with a no-`/Info` control so it cannot pass on pdf-lib's defaults. **Reverting both source files turns exactly those 2 red, nothing else.** Left open, measured rather than fixed: links and annotations are still dropped on a re-render (the note says so; carrying them would mean re-creating them in the rebuilt document's coordinates, a product decision per tool that re-rasterises), and text in the output is pixels — `get_text()` is 0 on every produced sheet — which the card, the notes and the run message all state. Screen half: `docs/research/shots/colour-1-invert-deployed-after.png` (light) and this round's `docs/research/shots/colour-2-invert-dark.png`, `docs/research/shots/colour-3-invert-empty-dark.png` — dark mode, tokens only, the inverted sheet legible as light type on black and the empty control a black page on the darker canvas, no clipping, no console error. | the `invert-colors` entry in `tools/edit.ts` + `core/render.ts`’s filter path; `core/pages.ts` `displayedSize` (shared, this round) |

| `adjust-colors` | verified-good (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **Five of its seven controls were wired to the form and not to the code, and the promise line sold them.** Same helper, same await (`tools/edit.ts:1991`), and the await alone was not enough: measured on the dev server with the paint awaited, `brightness=1.6`, `contrast=2`, `saturation=0`, `hue=180` and `grayscale` produced **the same measurement** — whole-sheet ink 19.88 %, 13 719 non-white px, every region mean equal to ±0.1 — i.e. five runs of “do nothing”, while the rail read back each value it had been handed and the report line said *Adjusted 1 page at 200 dpi*. Cause is not this tool: pdf.js’s `beginDrawing` calls `resetCtxToDefault`, which writes `filter = 'none'` over anything set on the context before `page.render()` (pdfjs-dist 4.10.38, `build/pdf.mjs:7610-7617`), so `RenderOptions.filter` reached only pdf.js’s own background fill. Fixed at that option (`7cf6ce2`) rather than routed around here, per the parent’s decision.<br>**Measured per region on the deployed site after the push** (CI `42498140597`, 200 dpi, sheet 2 — the fixture’s flat blocks, because a page of greyscale text is nearly a fixed point of these controls: paper at 255 and ink at 0 are what `contrast` and `whitening` leave alone). Each figure is the measured mean against the colour the **source sheet’s own mean** predicts through the CSS formula, with the distance from doing nothing and from the reverse setting beside it:<br>· `brightness=1.6` — grey 89.0 → **142.0** (predicted 142.4, Δ 0.4; identity 53.0, reverse 86.4), wash 165.0 → **255.0** (clipped as the formula says; Δidentity 90.0), red (229,25,25) → (254.9,41.0,41.0) (Δ 1.0)<br>· `contrast=2` — the two greys move **apart**: 89.0 → **50.0** and 165.0 → **204.0** (predicted 50.5 / 202.5), which is what “pushes lights lighter and darks darker” claims<br>· `saturation=0` — red/green/blue channel spread 204 / 153 / 204 → **0.0**, lightness kept (68.4 / 136.3 / 58.3 predicted, 69.0 / 136.0 / 59.0 measured)<br>· `hue=180` — red → (0.1,113.0,112.9) against (0.0,111.9,111.9) predicted, Δidentity **228.9**; `grayscale` — same achromatic result, and it wins over `saturation=2` as its help line promises<br>· `threshold=0.5` — **0** sampled px between luma 64 and 191 where the source sheet, read at the same density, carries 8 520: a real cutoff, not a contrast boost. The control run (same page, threshold off) leaves 6 743 mid tones, so the collapse is the toggle’s and not the JPEG’s<br>· **identity** — every control at its default composes an empty filter string and the produced page matches `rasterize-pdf` of the same sheet region for region, worst Δ **0.1** (bar/copy/band Δ 0.0), which is the assertion that rules out a filter applied twice.<br>The unit twin compares the encoded raster with the bitmap the page’s `finished` settled with, **sample for sample** (`tests/pdf/colour-tools-ink.test.ts`), and requires the five colour controls to write **five different** rasters — the control that fails if a slider is wired to the form only. Left open, measured here, not this row’s file: the `threshold` field is labelled “Make black and white” but sits *after* the colour filter, so a binarised page inverts its hues first and thresholds second — true to the code, worth a copy edit by whoever owns the rail next.<br>**Round 044 took that left-open item and the rest of the row.** Status raised to `verified-good`: the five controls were re-measured per region on the dev build at 200 dpi, each against the source sheet’s own colour pushed through the CSS formula, and **every control still lands where its formula says** — brightness 1.6 / contrast 2 / saturation 0 / hue 180 / grayscale all **Δ ≤ 1.5** from the prediction with Δidentity 25–229, and two controls applied together write the **composition of both**: `brightness=1.6 contrast=1.5` Δ ≤ 1.6 while brightness alone is 39.9 off and contrast alone 79.3; `saturation=2 hue=90` Δ ≤ 1.0 while each alone is 24.9–50.9 off. Identity holds: every control at default against `rasterize-pdf` of the same sheet, worst Δ **0.1** (both sheets). **The left-open label was the one real defect and the order was right**, so the order was kept and the words fixed: the cutoff demonstrably runs *after* the colour settings (`threshold=0.5 brightness=1.6` lifts the 89-luma grey over the cut — black → white, 0 mid tones against the source sheet’s 41 325 — because a cut applied first and then brightened would stop being black and white), and the help now says so instead of leaving the label to imply the page is cut from the original. The card also never named the two things it drops: it now says links and annotations are not carried and are pixels instead (measured: the note icon paints at its source position, 987 non-white px, and the copy’s `/Annots` is gone), and its geometry note now names the `/CropBox` it honours (crop sheet: 336×71.4 page, 933×198 raster, aspect 4.712 against 4.706). `/Info` carried (title, author, subject, keywords), `/Rotate` honoured both ways (595×420 and 420×595 outputs), empty control 0 non-white px. Pinned in `tests/pdf/colour-tools-ink.test.ts`: cutoff-after-the-settings (goes red — 15 failures — if the cut is moved before the filter), pair composition sample for sample, crop-box aspect, `/Info`, annots dropped-and-named, and the help/notes wording (**2 red** with the copy reverted). Harness fixed alongside: `colour-ink.py` predicted an unclamped filter chain and a 0.299-luma cut, so it called a correct composition a FAIL. Screenshots `shots/colour-4-adjust-pane-help.png`, `colour-5-adjust-empty.png`, `colour-6-adjust-dark.png`. Left open: the canvas-roundtrip drops form widgets’ *fillability* as well as their `/Annots` (same class, not measured here), and the pages-field caption collides with the thumbnail grid — a `pagesField` seam, not this tool’s. | the `adjust-colors` entry in `tools/edit.ts` (call site + card copy), `colour-ink.py` for the two prediction errors |
| `compare-pdfs` | verified-good (dev build `:5271`) | **It showed an answer it had not computed and shipped a different one it had not photographed.** The handed-over question — *which path produces the verdict the user reads, and which produces the exported report?* — has a three-part answer, and none of the three parts was the one the audit assumed.<br>**Which path is which (measured on the deployed page, `docs/research/compare-rail.mjs`, `BASE=https://www.browsersnip.com`).** The verdict comes from `CompareMain.renderPagePair`, which awaits `page.render().promise` and so was never suspected; the exported file comes from `CompareTool`'s button → `tools/legacy.ts` (`compare`) → `pdf-engine/commands.ts` `comparePDFs`, which read both canvases on the settle — **the eighth copy of the family** (`694e007`, `6b9b479`, `8313e2d`, `4ba14b6`, `53fcb85`, `38ef532`, `7cf6ce2`/`128a247`). The awaiting path was right about the pixels and still published nothing: while the pane's own loading state is up it does **not** render its two `<canvas>` elements, so the first comparison threw on `canvasARef.current` being null inside a `catch {}`. Measured on load: `mounted 3, sizes [[300,150],[300,150]]` (a canvas nobody ever sized), `opaque 0 / ink 0` on both sheets, `read` absent, strip `NO DIFFERENCES FOUND ON PAGE 1 AT THIS SENSITIVITY — THE EXPORT MARKS NOTHING.`, card `0 %`. Turn one page and back (`WARM=1`) and the same tab, the same pair, answers **`1 CHANGED AREA · 13,600 OF 249,900 PIXELS (5.4%) ON PAGE 1`** over sheets measuring `opaque 100 %`, ink 6 991 px against 20 591 px — a difference of exactly the planted 340×40 pt block — **while the card beside it still read `0 %`**, because `CompareTool` subscribed with `setSt(getCompareState())`, handing React the same mutable object, so the notification never took the panel down. So the sentence the audit recorded came from neither working half: the strip had not run, and the card could not re-render. Meanwhile the file: **23 815 bytes, two image XObjects per page, ink `0.11 %` on every page — including the one the source leaves empty — against its own source sheet at `2.25 %`, and `0` highlighted px.** (The audit's `0.13 %` is that number on its fixture: a painted page measures the label line and nothing else.)<br>**Fixes, three of them, all at the read or at the mount.** `await Promise.all([renderA.finished, renderB.finished])` in `comparePDFs`; the loading state became an overlay (`data-compare-loading`) so the sheets stay mounted, and a comparison that never ran now says `Neither page has been read yet` instead of answering 0; the panel copies the store per notification. The verdict also **localises** now, which the export already implied and nothing measured: `diffAreasOf` groups changed pixels on the coarse grid but bounds them from the pixels themselves, and the pane publishes the box in the page's own points — `1 changed area · 13,600 of 249,900 pixels (5.4%) on page 1 · largest at x 40–380 · y 60–100 pt from the page's top-left` — in the strip, in the card's list, and as `data-compare-area` for the probe. The threshold now re-answers from the pixels it already read (per-pixel distance does not depend on the threshold it is judged at), which is what the panel's own advice — "lower the sensitivity to catch smaller changes" — had been promising since before anything could honour it.<br>**Positive, negative and blank, re-measured on the deployed site after the push** (CI `42510939470`, same fixture, `compare-measure.sh`, every figure off the produced file's own overlay XObject with its `/SMask`, and the pane's numbers out of the DOM):<br>· **planted and found, at the place** — verdict area `(40, 60, 380, 100)` against the planted `(40, 60, 380, 100)`: **0.0 pt off**; the exported overlay `bbox (39.8, 60.0, 380.2, 99.8)`: **0.16 pt off**, `58 847` marked px = **13 558 pt² of the planted 13 600 pt² (99.7 %)** — the mark is the difference and not the page: 13 558 pt² of the 249 900 pt² sheet — 5.4 %, exactly the planted block's share of it;<br>· **ink arrived** — page 1 of the download reads **8.30 %** where the same page before read 0.11 % and its source measures 2.25 % (the excess is the mark itself); the file is 86 070 bytes, and the result card says `Compared 3 pages of "a.pdf" against "b.pdf" and painted the differences onto the copy`;<br>· **negative control, earned** — A against A marks **0 px on every page** *and* still carries its ink (`2.81 %` against its source's `2.25 %`), card and strip both `0 %`: the zero is a measurement, not paper;<br>· **blank control** — sheet 3, empty in both files, is marked on no page and comes back at `0.11 %` ink (the `Page 3 — comparison` label) instead of being called different; a blank-versus-blank pair likewise reads `no differences found`, so the tool has not swapped one wrong answer for the other;<br>· **the unchanged middle page** — sheet 2 is in both files and comes back with **0 marks and its own 1.76 % ink** (source 1.37 %), which is the per-page claim an ink total cannot make;<br>· **the surfaces agree** — `card and strip publish the same number: 5.4%` on the differing page and `0%` on the equal ones, in every run, and `read=true`.<br>**Tolerance ±2 pt, justified rather than chosen**: the pane's diff runs at 1 device px per pt (`DISPLAY_SCALE 1.0`) and the export at 2, so quantisation alone costs ≤ 1 pt and ≤ 0.5 pt, and the checker rasterises the produced page at 150 dpi (0.48 pt per px) through a 0.7-opacity blend; measured drift is 0.0 and 0.16 pt, so no claim here leans on the margin.<br>**A fourth defect, found by the negative control and not by the positive one**: A against A — the same bytes twice — answered **13 998 changed pixels in two areas** on the dev server, over a sheet reading `opaque 2.98 %`: two `page.render()` tasks had been handed the same canvas, and the second wiped the first's paper fill under its own ink. StrictMode's double effect is only how to *see* it; a user turns the pair over while it is still painting. Renders are queued through one promise now, with a token so a superseded pair publishes nothing (`refuses to photograph a sheet another render task is still painting`).<br>**Copy went by the same measure.** `compare-pdfs` promised *"a page-by-page report of what differs, with a picture of each changed area when you compare pixels"*. Three things contradict it: the route prints that entry's promise but its button runs `legacy.ts`'s `compare`, so the Pixels / Text content / Both segment the sentence leans on is never rendered; the entry's own `run` encodes an overlay per differing page into `images` and returns without them (pinned: `out.files` is `undefined`); and the shipped export paints one translucent band over the sheet, it does not cut out a picture per area. Its `description`, its `promise` and a new note now say what the page does (`…the changed areas counted and located in the page's own points, and a copy of the first file with those areas painted`, `The numbers beside the pages are for the page in view; the marks are painted across every page both files have…`). The folded `compare` entry's "highlight **every** difference" was contradicted by its own threshold and now names it.<br>**The unreachable path was repaired and counted, not assumed away.** `tools/security.ts`'s `visualDiff` read both canvases on the settle too; one `await` there, and its headline no longer counts rows by regexing their prose — `"no visible difference"` contains *differ*, so **A against A was reported as `3 of 3 page(s) differ`**, and a `Both`-mode text change was not counted at all; the count is now a `Set` of pages.<br>**Red without the fix.** `docs/research/compare-measure.sh` on the deployed pre-fix build: **6 of 6 cases FAIL, 0 of 5 controls failed to fail**; after the push: `every case measured as asked, and every control failed as asked` — twice, in two independent runs (`compare-deploy-after.log`, `compare-deploy-after-2.log`), the downloads 86 070 and 86 071 bytes with the same ink and the same bbox, so the claim is *measured-equal*, not byte-equal (pdf-lib writes a timestamp). Unit side (`tests/pdf/compare-diff.test.ts` (18) + the four cases added to `ux-surfaces.test.ts`; 59 green over the two files): revert the export `await` → **5 red** (`the export read a page canvas before its paint landed: expected 9 to be +0`, ink `[0,0,0]` where `[4800,4500,0]` was planted); put the placeholder back over the sheets → **9 red**; let the panel subscribe to the object itself → **2 red**; revert the rail's `await` → **1 red**; count the headline from the prose again → **2 red**; report the grouping grid's bounds instead of the pixels' → **4 red**; drop the render queue → **1 red** (`expected 2 to be less than or equal to 1`). **Which case passes in both states, and why**: the blank-versus-blank file case is green under the `await` revert — an empty sheet has nothing to mark either way, so it is the instrument's zero, which is exactly why the A-versus-A case beside it carries the ink floor and does go red; the copy pins and `throws the pictures it makes away` are green by construction, since they police wording and a dropped value, not pixels.<br>Screen half: `docs/research/shots/compare-1-deployed-after.png` (the deployed pane: A carrying the red mark exactly where B's black block is, strip and card both `5.4 %`, `· area 1: x 40–380 · y 60–100 pt from the page's top-left`), `…-2-deployed-result.png` (the download's first page drawn on the card, `4.9 KB → 84.1 KB · 1601 % larger`, `PAGES 6 → 3`), `…-3-deployed-dark.png` (dark mode, same numbers, no hex literal in either compare component — the overlay veil is `bg-cream/80`), no console error in any run. Green: 59 across the two files, 198 across the security and definition neighbours (`edge-security`, `security-bytes`, `registry`, `parity-definitions`, `ux-definitions`, `tools-e2e`), 247 across the rasterise ones (`greyscale-ink`, `colour-tools-ink`, `rasterize-ink`, `pdf-to-image`, `deskew`, `edge-convert-from`, `convert`, `pages-out`), 312 more (`edge-edit`, `edit-bytes`, `edge-organize`, `optimize-bytes`, `edge-editors`); `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0.<br>**The six items this row left open are closed, each measured before and after.** They were handed over as a list rather than a fix because none of them was one line, and the first was a layout call: <br>(1) **the route printed a promise its own button did not keep, and the segment that promise leaned on was on no page.** `#/tool/compare-pdfs` printed the `security.ts` entry's promise while the button ran `legacy.ts`'s `compare`, so neither the Pixels / Text content / Both segment nor the `resolution` box had a control: measured on the dev build with two files loaded, the panel's only controls were its file list, `Generate comparison PDF`, `◂ Prev`, `Next ▸` and the sensitivity slider. The decision went to **the report in the pane** — the smaller of the two: the panel now renders that entry's own segment and resolution box against the comparison that already lives there, and the pane answers in the mode chosen (a text answer reads the two text layers and counts changed words; a pixel answer is unchanged). Measured on the pair: pressing `Text content` returns `aria-pressed=true`, strip `Page 1: the extractable text is identical.`, card the same line, `data-compare-text=same`; pressing `Both` publishes both halves; `Pixels` restores the pixel verdict. On a same-ink, different-words pair the strip goes `identical=true` → `data-compare-text=differs` with `1 word(s) removed, 1 added`, which is a change a pixel comparison cannot see — the whole reason the segment exists. The copy says which half the marks belong to (`The exported copy marks pixels either way.`), because a reworded sentence has no pixels to paint. <br>(2) **a pair that differed only in page height got one reading from the pane and the other from the file.** The pane bottom-aligned the pair inside the union frame and the export top-aligned it, so on `a.pdf`/`b.pdf`'s 500 pt / 560 pt variant — same copy, same offsets, nothing else — the pane published `1 changed area · 7,248 of 235,200 pixels (3.1%) · largest at x 40–282 · y -29–103 pt` — **negative**, i.e. above the page — while the export of the same pair marked **0 px**. One alignment now: both halves draw from the frame's top-left (the export's own rule), so `y` is always a coordinate on the page in view. Same pair after: pane `no pixel differences`, `0 px`, export `0 px`, `1.71 %` ink (its own), page box 420×560. A control next door: with a block planted at 40,60–380,100 on the short sheet the verdict and the mark are both at that rectangle. <br>(3) **two pixel diffs painted the same change at two opacities.** `commands.ts` `diffCanvas` and the pane both use `distance × 1.5` clamped; `security.ts` `visualDiff` used a flat **200**, and it also judged a *mean* channel distance (`delta / 3 > threshold * 255`) where the other two judge the sum — three times blunter. Both are one rule now. Measured on a faint block worth a sum of 60 (20 per channel): before, the report called it `no visible difference` and would have painted it at alpha 200; after, it reports `% of pixels differ` and paints at **alpha 90** — the same 90 the export writes at that pixel. <br>(4) **a second, fully transparent image XObject on every page.** On an A-against-A download every one of the three sheets carried **2** image XObjects with **0 of 999 600** alpha bytes set: 85 247 bytes for three pages of text and no difference. `diffCanvas` now returns `null` when not one pixel cleared the threshold and the caller (which already guarded) draws no overlay. After: **1** XObject per page on all three sheets, **72 193** bytes (−15.3 %), the pages' ink unchanged at `2.81 %` against the source's `2.25 %` — the zero is still a measurement. On the positive pair sheets 2 and 3 lose the overlay and sheet 1 keeps it: `86 070 → 77 382` bytes, same `8.30 %` ink, same mark. <br>(5) **one field name, two numbers.** The definition asked 24 out of 120 and the slider 30 out of 150, so neither control could express the other's default. Both are now `30` in `5–150`, and `tests/pdf/compare-diff.test.ts` fails if they part again. <br>(6) **no localisation was ever asserted from the exported file for the pane's own scale.** The pane now renders at the resolution field's density (`144` dpi default, exactly 2 device px per point — the density the export writes at) while drawing at one CSS pixel per point, so the sheets still fit beside each other. Measured at that density: the verdict's area is `(40, 60, 380, 100)` against the planted `(40, 60, 380, 100)` — **0.0 pt** — and the exported overlay `bbox (39.8, 60.0, 380.2, 99.8)`, **0.16 pt**, `58 847` marked px = **13 558 pt²** of the planted 13 600 pt² (99.7 %). <br>**Six positives and three controls, re-measured on the dev build after the fixes** (`compare-measure.sh`, `compare-measure.py`, port 9840–9845, the downloads on disk): the verdict is exact, the export 0.16 pt, ink `8.30 %` against the source sheet's `2.25 %`; the unchanged sheet comes back **0 marks with its own 1.76 % ink and 1 XObject**; the blank sheet **0 marks, `0.11 %`** (the label); A against A **0 px with `2.81 %` ink and 1 XObject per page**; the blank-versus-blank pair `no differences`; card and strip publish the same number (`5.4 %`, `0 %`) in every run and `read=true` always; no console error in any run.
