| `protect` | fixed+deployed — gate-only since `9b7eba1`'s follow-up, **AES-128 written on save since `89a5b7e`, `383bd93`** (reader table `1cbdc07`, rig re-measure `d29ac8a`); **AES-256 written since `7ea16db` (read since `f44b415`), and the Cipher control back at `e2c9029` offering exactly the two strengths that reach the bytes** | **A file that claimed AES-256 and was not encrypted at all — and the handed-over cause was one level too shallow.** The triage read `/V 5 /R 6` with no `/OE`/`/UE`/`/Perms`, `/CFM /AESV2 /Length 16`, MuPDF and Ghostscript refusing it. Measured, the dictionary is the smaller half of the defect: **`@maxwbh/pdf-lib@1.18.1`'s writer never runs a cipher.** `save({encrypt})` calls `setupEncryption()` — which registers the `/Encrypt` dictionary and a document `/ID` — and hands the context to `PDFStreamWriter`; `PDFSecurity.encryptStream` and `encryptString` have **no caller in the shipped build**, so at all four strengths every stream leaves as plaintext flate (`78 9c …`, identical bytes under RC4-40, RC4-128, AES-128 and AES-256) behind a dictionary that says otherwise. `decryptStream` has no caller either, which is the `encrypted-inputs` row below. So repairing the R6 shape alone would have made things *worse*: a valid AESV3 claim over plaintext is something every conforming reader now has to undo.<br>**The requirement, taken from an independent implementation rather than from the spec text** (`docs/research/protect-reference.py`: qpdf writing the same fixture with the same passwords): `/CF /StdCF /CFM /AESV3 /Length 32`, `/O` 48 bytes, `/U` 48, `/OE` 32, `/UE` 32, `/Perms` 16. This app wrote `/V 5 /R 6 /Length 256`, `/O` 32, `/U` 32, no `/OE`, no `/UE`, no `/Perms`, no `/EncryptMetadata`, and `/CF /StdCF /CFM /AESV2 /Length 16` — and nothing it computes is an R6 value: `/O` and `/U` come from the revision-4 MD5+RC4 chain (`computeOwnerKey`/`computeUserKey`), and the 32-byte random file key that `/UE`/`/OE` wrap is never generated. Every one of the four offered labels was therefore false, not just the 256.<br>**Readers × the file the shipped tool writes** (`docs/research/protect-readers-before.log`, produced by `protect` itself through `runPdfTool`, same fixture as the after table): MuPDF *does not open* (`FileDataError`); qpdf *does not open* (`some V=5 encryption dictionary parameters are missing or the wrong type`); pypdf *accepts neither the empty nor the planted password*; poppler opens **without asking**, prints `Invalid encryption key length … ownerEncLength: 18446744073709551615`, and finds the text only because the text is in the clear; Ghostscript writes 1 page of 3; and this app's own pdf.js throws `Invalid argument for stringToBytes`, which is the "no independent reader could confirm the gate" line on the card — the self-check was reporting a crash. At the other three strengths the dictionary parses and the page dies instead: MuPDF `opens with the password, pages=0`, poppler `Unknown compression method in flate stream`, pypdf `Cannot find Root object`.<br>**Chosen: say what the bytes are.** `saveEncrypted` now assembles the dictionary through the fork's own exported `PDFSecurity` — revision 4, the strength its `/O` and `/U` are genuinely computed at — with an **empty `/CF` and no `/StmF`/`/StrF`**, which is the spec's own way of selecting the predefined `/None` crypt filter ("the data are not encrypted"): one save, no vendor patch, no new dependency. The spelling is measured, not picked — seven `/None` variants were swept (`/CFM /None` named `/StdCF`, `/Identity`, `/Default`, `/None`, with and without `/Length`, with and without `/AuthEvent`), and naming a filter whose method is `/None` is what makes MuPDF refuse the file and qpdf report `errors while decoding content stream`; the empty `/CF` is the only shape MuPDF, qpdf **and** pypdf all take.<br>**Readers × the file the fixed tool writes** (`…-after.log`, and the same bytes downloaded from `https://www.browsersnip.com` in `…-deployed.log`): MuPDF `gate: opened with the password, pages=3, cipher 'Standard V4 R4 128-bit None'`, planted text extracted, `/P allows ['access']`; qpdf `refuses an empty password … qpdf syntax check: clean`, `/P allows ['accessibility']`; pypdf `is_encrypted=True, gate: 'reader-pass', pages=3`, text extracted; pdf.js refuses with `PasswordRequiredError` and then reads `pages=3 text="Secret Alice 42 Bravo on page 1"`. Two readers still do not treat it as a gate, and the row says so rather than rounding up: **poppler** reads 3 of 3 pages and never asks (`Unimplemented Feature: Unsupported version/revision (4/4) of Standard security handler`), and **Ghostscript** writes 1 page of 3 — both exactly what it did to the dictionary this replaced. Net: MuPDF and qpdf went from *cannot open the file at all* to *opens it, asks for the password, and shows every page*.<br>**Visible product change, per the parent's amendment: the Cipher control is gone** from `protect` and from `change-permissions`, because a choice between four names for one dictionary is a choice of four false claims; the card now reads *"Protected with an open password. The file carries the gate and the /P flags and no crypt filter, which means the pages are copied through rather than ciphered"*, and the notes name both the alternative that works today (rasterise, then protect) and the ordering for whoever builds the cipher next: **AES-128 (`/CFM /AESV2`) first** — MD5 key expansion is already exported by an installed dependency and AES-CBC is native Web Crypto — **AES-256 deferred** until a strength can also be *read* back, since shipping ciphertext this app cannot decrypt is half a feature.<br>**Round trip, counted on both sides** (`tests/pdf/protect-spec.test.ts`): source, protected and unlocked all measure `pages 3, annotations 2, /Annots entries 2, fields 2 (values `Ada Lovelace` + checked), bookmarks 2, attachments ['source.csv']`, and the unlocked file is judged by readers too — MuPDF 3 pages with text, qpdf clean, Ghostscript back to 3 of 3 once the dictionary is gone. `/P` is decoded from the integer in the file (`/P -3392` → bit 3 printing, 4 modify, 5 copy, 6 annots, 9 forms, 10 accessibility, 11 assemble, 12 high-res) and compared against what the sentence claims; the permissions-only run opens with no password in MuPDF, qpdf and pypdf while all three still report every restricted action refused.<br>**Tests:** `tests/pdf/protect-spec.test.ts` (6, + the env-gated `encrypted-inputs` rig), `tests/pdf/security-bytes.test.ts`, `tests/pdf/edge-security.test.ts` re-pinned from the old claims to the new structure — including the assertion that no `/CFM`, no `/AESV2`, no `/V 5` and no strength name survives anywhere in the file or in the wording. **Reverting `saveEncrypted` alone reddens 9** (protect-spec 4, security-bytes 2, edge-security 3) and CI added a 10th in `tools-e2e`, which had pinned `/AES-256/` in the sentence. Green: 81 across the three security files, 126 with `guard` and the round trip, 240 with the definition-policing files (`registry`, `parity-definitions`, `ux-definitions`, `ux-surfaces`), 220 more across `edit-bytes`/`optimize-bytes`/`pages-out`/`compare-diff`/`engine-*`. `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0, no UI file touched (one control removed, nothing restyled), CI `42588373906`, deployed card + download re-measured in Chromium (`shots/protect-deployed-gated.png` — the card, `PAGES 3 → 3`, and the intake's own honest line that a password-protected copy cannot be previewed without the password; no console error in any run). Left open, measured here: (1) **`encrypted-inputs`** below — the missing `decryptStream` caller, which is why `unlock` is next and bigger than its triage row says; (2) `change-permissions`' own FALSE CLAIM row still owes its wording, though it now writes honest bytes too; (3) `preserveProtection` re-keys a document whose ciphertext it never undid, so a rotate over a *foreign* encrypted file reports success on a file whose password no longer matches its content — measured since, and sharper: the password still opens the copy in MuPDF, and what no longer matches is the page, which every reader renders blank (see the `encrypted-inputs` row and `docs/research/encrypted-inputs-findings.md`); (4) poppler's and Ghostscript's `/None` handling is theirs, not ours — a real filter would fix both, which is argument enough for AES-128 next. (5) **Fixed outside this row, and disclosed:** `tests/pdf/style-controls-invariant.test.ts` timed out at 6.2 s against vitest's 5 s default on the CI runner — both of its rules re-scan every tool-layer line once per declared field, 511 of them. It failed two pushes in a row on commits that touched no field (`42590325472` re-run green, `42591669587` red again), so both tests now carry a 120 s ceiling and the assertions are untouched.<br>**Then it stopped being only a gate: `protect` writes real AES-128 now (`89a5b7e`, `383bd93`), and the two readers that ignored the gate stopped ignoring it — which is what this row's left-open item (4) was for.** `core/crypto.ts:encryptDocument` runs the *read* path's derivations forwards — Algorithm 3 for `/O`, 2 for the file key, 4 for `/U`, 5 for `/Perms`, 1 per object — on the primitives already there (MD5 from `node-forge`, AES-128-CBC from Web Crypto, RC4 from the fork: no new dependency, nothing uploaded), and writes `/V 4 /R 4 /Length 128`, `/CF << /StdCF << /CFM /AESV2 /AuthEvent /DocOpen /Length 16 >> >>` selected by both `/StmF` and `/StrF`, `/EncryptMetadata true`, `/O` 32 bytes, `/U` 32, `/Perms` 16 — then ciphers every stream (**deflated first, ciphered last**, the order the spec writes them in) and every string. `core/doc.ts:saveEncrypted` saves the document twice: the throwaway pass exists because `save()` generates field appearances and flushes assets *while* it serializes, and an object born after the walk would be written in the clear behind a dictionary claiming ciphertext (`crypto-encrypt.test.ts` has the case); and it saves with `useObjectStreams: false`, because the vendor inflates object streams while *parsing*, before any decryptor runs, so a file that packs its dictionaries into one is a file this app can write and cannot re-open.<br>**The hashes are checked against a second implementation, not against this app's reader.** Recomputing the saved file's key, `/O`, the first 16 bytes of `/U` and `AES-ECB(MD5(fileKey ‖ /P₄le ‖ ff·4 ‖ /ID[0:16] ‖ "adb"))` in Python (`pypdf.AlgV4` plus `cryptography`'s AES) reproduces all four byte for byte, so Algorithm 5 is what an outside library computes, not what this module believes. Worth naming: **qpdf and MuPDF write no `/Perms` at R4 at all**, and none of the five readers checks it — flipping a byte of `/Perms` changed no verdict (pypdf's `are_permissions_valid` stayed `True`). At revision 4 the tamper evidence is the key schedule itself: editing `/P` in the dictionary breaks the derivation, and MuPDF then answers `document closed or encrypted` to a file it had just opened (`crypto-encrypt.test.ts` asserts *that*, not the checksum).<br>**Readers × the two files, one script.** `docs/research/protect-readers-gateonly.log` is the `/None` gate this row used to ship and `docs/research/protect-readers-aes128.log` is what `protect` writes now, both produced by the same `docs/research/protect-readers.py` on the same three-page fixture (2 304 bytes against 7 140 — the cost of no object streams, one padding block per stream and hex-serialized strings, measured on that file): MuPDF `cipher='Standard V4 R4 128-bit AES'`, refuses an empty password, opens with the real one, `pages=3`, planted text True; qpdf refuses an empty password, `qpdf --check` **clean**, `/P allows ['accessibility']`; pypdf `gate: 'open-sesame'`, planted text True; **poppler now asks** — `pdfinfo: Encrypted: yes (print:no copy:no change:no addNotes:no algorithm:AES)` — where on the gate it printed `Unimplemented Feature: Unsupported version/revision (4/4) of Standard security handler`, reported the file as `Encrypted: no` and read all three pages with nothing typed; **Ghostscript renders 3 pages of 3 with the secret and 0 pages without it** (`This file requires a password for access`), where the gate gave it `wrote 1 page(s), planted text through gs: False`. MuPDF's own label is the two-line summary of the difference: `Standard V4 R4 128-bit None` against `Standard V4 R4 128-bit AES`. Asked for the *exact* text: with the password MuPDF, poppler, pypdf and Ghostscript's own re-render each return `Secret Alice 42 Bravo on page 1/2/3` — three for three — and without it MuPDF raises, poppler exits 1, qpdf raises `PasswordError`, pypdf `decrypt('')=0` then `FileNotDecryptedError`, Ghostscript processes no pages. `qpdf --check` is clean on the file. So **the sentence about poppler and Ghostscript in this row's previous half is superseded: AES-128 changed both**, and that is the argument for having written it.<br>**Re-measured on the deployed site** (`node scripts/live-protect-proof.mjs`, transcript in `docs/research/protect-readers-deployed.log`; the previous live run of the same harness is kept as `-deployed-none-filter.log`): the sentence a person reads after typing two passwords into `https://www.browsersnip.com` is *“Protected with an open password. The file is ciphered in AES-128 (/V 4 /R 4, crypt filter /StdCF) and carries the /P flags. As written to the file: … A PDF reader opened the copy with that password and read 3 page(s)”*, and the download it offers — 3 109 bytes from a 2 268-byte fixture where the live gate-only build produced 1 709 of the same file, the cost of no object streams and one padding block per stream — is read back by the same five readers: MuPDF `Standard V4 R4 128-bit AES`, `gate: opened with the password 'open-sesame'`, planted text True; poppler `asks for a password (… algorithm:AES)`; qpdf `refuses an empty password`, `qpdf syntax check: clean`; pypdf `gate: 'open-sesame'`, planted text True; Ghostscript `renders → wrote 3 page(s)`. The same live run before this change had poppler reading three pages with nothing typed and Ghostscript writing one of three. The harness types by the `tool-field-*` id rather than by label text — the owner box's own placeholder is "Blank reuses the open password", and matching on words typed the wrong field, which is how the first version of this proof nearly reported an ungated file as a gated one — and reads the values back out of the live panel (`fields after: … tool-field-userPassword="open-sesame"`) rather than trusting its own write.<br>`docs/research/encrypted-inputs-run.sh` re-measured with the cipher on the write path, previous run kept as `encrypted-inputs-{fixtures,app,readers}-pre-aes128.log` and `-table-pre-aes128.md` (`d29ac8a`): the tallies that must not move did not — 25 produced files, 10 `reads` + 15 `partial-loss` (the page-copy attachment/annotation losses this row does not own), `silent-loss` **0** where the audit measured 18 of 18, `qpdf --check` clean on 35 files. What moved is the cipher column: every produced file now says `{"/StdCF": "/AESV2"}`, or `pre-filter RC4` where the source was RC4-128, and the four owner-only runs that came back `no /Encrypt` came back carrying the source's lock.<br>**What this exposed rather than introduced** — the parent's framing, and it generalises to every dormant path a feature wakes up: `tools/runner.ts:preserveProtection` had kept a permissions-only file's restrictions through an unrelated edit *by accident*, because the vendor carries a trailer entry it never had to undo. Once the read path decrypts, the copy came back open — and re-deriving a lock from the typed password turned out to be a second, unasked-for edit: measured on a re-keyed permissions-only file, the original owner password answers **MuPDF `0`** and **qpdf `PasswordError`**. The fix carries the lock instead — `core/crypto.ts:retainedLock`/`resealDocument`, `core/doc.ts:resealLikeSource` — keeping the source's `/O`, `/U`, `/Perms`, `/P`, `/CF`, `/ID[0]` and file key, so the edit changes the pages and nothing else, and the copy keeps the source's *strength*. Two guard defects fell out of the same change: `/P` was decoded from the document rather than from the file (so `permissions` came back `null` on exactly the files `protect` writes, and the tool's *“as written to the file”* sentence silently fell back to the form), and `opensWithEmptyPassword` reported which secret the caller typed instead of what the file allows — typing an owner password made a permissions-only file look locked.<br>**`/P`, written from the toggles and read off the integer.** `permissionsToFlags` now lives with the writer (`core/doc.ts`), because the same four bytes feed the file key, `/U` and `/Perms`; the tests read `/P` out of the dictionary body bit by bit (Table 25: printing 3, modify 4, copy 5, annotate 6, forms 9, accessibility 10, assemble 11, high-res 12) and one case asserts that different flags yield different `/U` — a re-locked file is not the same file with a comment edited. Permissions-only files still open freely under MuPDF, qpdf, pypdf and poppler, with the copy ciphered under the empty user secret. **All five *judge* `/P` and none of the five *enforces* it from the command line**: MuPDF reports the mask (`-3392` → `['access']`), qpdf `pdf.allow`, poppler `pdfinfo`, pypdf `user_access_permissions`, Ghostscript prints nothing about it; and yet `pdftops` wrote a print-refused page, `gs -sDEVICE=ps2write` and `pdfwrite` both succeeded, `pdftotext`/MuPDF/pypdf all returned the text of a copy-refused file, and pikepdf saved a modify-refused one. The card's line — *permissions are a request, not encryption of what you can see* — is therefore still the true sentence, now beside content that is genuinely ciphered.<br>**UI truth (no control came back).** A selector with one honest option is not a choice, so the Cipher control stays absent and the wording moved to the bytes: the promise is 137 characters naming AES-128 and nothing else, the card reads *“The file is ciphered in AES-128 (/V 4 /R 4, crypt filter /StdCF) and carries the /P flags”*, `verifyProtection` now re-reads the saved file **with** the password it just wrote (dictionary from a load that leaves the cipher in place, page content from one that undoes it — writer and reader agreeing is the user's re-open proof, the readers above are the format proof) and its warning is inverted, so *a file that names no crypt filter* is now the thing admitted. View Metadata reports `AES-128 (crypt filter /StdCF, revision 4)` from the guard's own description instead of a bare label, and `tests/pdf/{edge-security,security-bytes}.test.ts` police that no retired strength name survives in any sentence the user acts on.<br>**Tests and gates:** `tests/pdf/crypto-encrypt.test.ts` (21, new) and `tests/pdf/gate-harness.ts` (the `/None` gate the app no longer writes, so five suites keep testing the *shape* rather than a writer that moved on); re-pinned `protect-spec` 9, `security-bytes` 34, `edge-security` 41, `encrypted-guard` 113, `guard` 44, `crypto-decrypt` 25, `edge-organize` 95, plus `edge-signature-core`, `tools-e2e`, `parity-definitions`, `registry`, `ux-*`. Green: 217 across the five files this row is judged on, 381 with `guard`, `crypto-decrypt` and `edge-organize`; 104 more with signature/metadata/attachments/edit; 189 with the definition-policing files; 97 in the shared UI suite. `tsc -p tsconfig.json` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, no `.tsx` touched, 100 % client-side. **Left open, measured here:** (1) AES-256 stays unread *and* unwritten — R6 needs `/OE`, `/UE` and its own `/U`, and shipping a strength only in one direction is half a feature; (2) 40-bit RC4 still has no outside fixture; (3) `split` and every other multi-output tool returns `kind: 'files'`, which `preserveProtection` does not reach at all — asserted in `encrypted-guard`'s table rather than assumed; (4) the page-copy losses (`attachments ['source.csv']→[]`, `bookmarks 2→0/1`) reproduce unchanged, and the words survive them; (5) the vendor still ships `PDFSecurity.encryptStream` with no caller — this app no longer depends on that gap, but `save({ encrypt })` still writes a cipher claim over plaintext, which `crypto-encrypt.test.ts` now pins as `plaintext-claim`.<br>**AES-256 is a strength now, not a label: the read path's own ladder, run backwards (`7ea16db`, `1dbd93b`, `e2c9029`, `8b16198`).** `core/crypto.ts:revision6Values` writes `/U` and `/O` with the *same* `revision6Hash` the reader verifies with — 32 bytes of the SHA-256/384/512 ladder, then the 8-byte validation salt and the 8-byte key salt, the owner value hashed over the whole 48-byte `/U` and the user value over nothing — wraps a 32-byte CSPRNG file key into `/UE` and `/OE` with AES-256-CBC, zero IV and **no padding**, and puts `/Perms` up as one block holding `/P` little-endian, its 64-bit extension all ones, `T`/`F` for `/EncryptMetadata` at byte 8 and `adb` at 9-11; the dictionary is `/V 5 /R 6 /Length 256` with `/CF /StdCF /CFM /AESV3 /Length 32` selected by both `/StmF` and `/StrF`, and the filter runs over the file key itself because revision 6 defines no per-object key. **The proof is byte-identity with a foreign file, not a generation that agrees with itself:** `tests/pdf/crypto-aes256-write.test.ts` (29) reads each of the four R6 fixtures, unwraps the file key through `retainedLock`, lifts the four salts out of `/U` and `/O` and the four random bytes out of that file's own `/Perms` (one-block ECB opened in the test, no borrowed helper), and requires `revision6Values` to hand back that fixture's `/U`, `/UE`, `/O`, `/OE` and `/Perms` **byte for byte** — with the same call at a secret the file does not carry asserted to reproduce none of the four password-derived values, and `/Perms` asserted to match anyway, because it authenticates the key and not the password. Owner-only and user-only variants are both in the table, since `/O` and `/U` are the one place revision 6 derives the two secrets differently, and the writer's own output is re-derived the same way, so a seal that wrapped the key under one hash twice cannot pass.<br>**Outside the app, on the files this writer produces** (`docs/research/aes256-write-readers.py` → `aes256-write-readers.log`; pypdf 6.14.2, PyMuPDF 1.28.2, pikepdf 10.9.1 / libqpdf 30.3.2, poppler 26.10.0, Ghostscript 10.08.0): three locks over the corpus's own `plain.pdf` — user password, owner-only, and one with `/EncryptMetadata false` — all at the corpus's `/P -3376`, each printed beside the libqpdf and MuPDF controls for the same document. Every one reads `recomputed /U matches, /UE matches, /O matches, /OE matches, /Perms matches`, `cipher='Standard V5 R6 256-bit AES'`, `qpdf syntax check: clean`, poppler `algorithm:AES-256`, Ghostscript 3 of 3, `planted_pages 3/3`, ink 1.53 %, 2 outline entries, 164 extracted characters — the same lines the two outside-produced controls print in the same run. pdf.js, asked inside the suite, opens what this writer writes with the password and refuses it without one and with a wrong one.<br>**One contradiction the metadata case exposed, and fixed:** `/EncryptMetadata` is now a required field of `CipherRequest` rather than a default (`core/doc.ts:saveEncrypted` decides it — `true`, with the reason beside the call), and the first file written with it `false` showed that `cipherIndirectObjects` ciphered every object *but the dictionary*: `/Info` and the user metadata stream left in ciphertext behind a dictionary promising them in the clear, and `decryptDocument`, which skips the exempt set, would have handed them back unreadable. The walk now skips the same `exemptRefs` the read path skips, in both write directions.<br>**The Cipher control came back, with two names instead of four** (`e2c9029`): `protect` and `change-permissions` offer AES-128 (the default — longer measurement record, and PDF 1.6) and AES-256, and nothing else. RC4-128 is read here but has never been written by this app, and RC4-40 has no outside fixture, so both are named in the notes as refused rather than on the control as choices; `cipherChoice` narrows an unrecognised stored value to the default, so a value left behind by an older form cannot name a dictionary the bytes will not honour. The card quotes the guard's read of the saved file — `ciphered in AES-256 (crypt filter /StdCF, revision 6)` — not the option that was picked.<br>**Deployed half** (`CIPHER=AES-256 node scripts/live-protect-proof.mjs` → `docs/research/protect-readers-deployed-aes256.log`; site bundle `assets/index-C2BBcn2H.js`, the same name this tree builds): the live panel reads back `tool-field-cipher="AES-256"` with the option text *AES-256 — PDF 2.0, /V 5 /R 6. Strongest; needs a modern reader.*, the card a person sees is *“Protected with an open password. The file is ciphered in AES-256 (crypt filter /StdCF, revision 6) and carries the /P flags. … A PDF reader opened the copy with that password and read 3 page(s)”*, and the 3 315-byte download answers MuPDF `Standard V5 R6 256-bit AES`, poppler `algorithm:AES-256`, qpdf `syntax check: clean`, pypdf planted-True 3 pages, Ghostscript 3 of 3, with all five dictionary values recomputed from the file's own salts and matching. **The rig, not the site, was the first suspect:** the first run printed `no #tool-field-cipher on this page; selects present: (none)` and downloaded an AES-128 file — the rail folds everything but its lead field behind “11 MORE OPTIONS”, so the select was in the DOM and not on screen. The rig now opens that fold, prints the value and the option text it read back, and aborts rather than reporting a strength it did not get.<br>**Superseded in this row, in both directions:** *“the Cipher control is gone”*, *“no control came back”*, *“AES-128 is the only strength offered”* and *“the promise is 137 characters naming AES-128 and nothing else”* (it is 146 and names the choice the user makes); left-open item **(1)** — AES-256 unread *and* unwritten — is closed (read at `f44b415`/`a0b6404`, written and offered here). Item **(2)**, 40-bit RC4 with no outside fixture, stays open, and so does the rule that a retired name may not come back on the strength of a label alone.<br>**Re-verified rather than assumed, and counted both ways:** AES-128 writing, the `/None` gate-only path, decryption at all three strengths and the carried-lock reseal are green unchanged — **350 across `crypto-aes256-write`, `crypto-aes256`, `crypto-encrypt`, `crypto-decrypt`, `security-bytes`, `encrypted-guard`, `protect-spec` and `guard`, plus 177 across `edge-security`, `parity-definitions`, `registry`, `ux-definitions` and `inert-field-sweep`** — `tsc -p tsconfig.json` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency (SHA-2 and AES-CBC are native Web Crypto, MD5 is the `node-forge` already present), nothing uploaded, no `.tsx` touched: the control renders through `FieldControls`' existing `select` case, so dark mode is the select it already styled. **Reverting `src/lib/pdf/core/crypto.ts` to `aa24007` alone reddens 25** (`crypto-aes256-write` 22 of 29, `security-bytes` 2, `edge-security` 1) and leaves `crypto-encrypt`, `crypto-decrypt`, `crypto-aes256` and `encrypted-guard` green — the shape the claim needs: the AES-256 tests fail because of the writer, and nothing that was true before is true only because of these. Two policing assertions were re-pinned *deliberately* because they encoded the old refusal: `edge-security`'s retired-name table drops `AES-256` and keeps the RC4 names, and `security-bytes`' “no strength may be sold” now reads the option list from both ends — nothing unmeasured offered, nothing measured missing. CI `43110547549` green (build + deploy).<br>**Round 051 re-settled the six things the record above never measured, and indicted one of them: `protect` could not re-key a file it had already locked.** Every number below comes from the shipped `runPdfTool` through Chromium (`:5271`, `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` with the new `CARD_PASSWORD` hook and the `protect` label set) and from `/Encrypt` parsed out of the bytes with `/V /R /CFM /Length /StmF /StrF /P` plus a per-bit decode, judged by MuPDF 1.28.2, qpdf (libqpdf 12.3.2 via pikepdf 10.9.1), pypdf 6.14.2, poppler 26.10.0 and Ghostscript 10.08.0 one script (`docs/research/protect-round-readers.py` → `-before.log`, `-after.log`; fixture `colour.pdf`, 4 pages, sheet ink `63579 / 49591 / 0 / 142563`, 1792 characters). **Cipher and gate, per cipher, exhaustive:** AES-128 writes `/V 4 /R 4 /CFM /AESV2` (both `/StmF` and `/StrF` `/StdCF`) and AES-256 writes `/V 5 /R 6 /CFM /AESV3` — the `AES-128` and `AES-256` the card prints, and *only* those; with no password all five refuse, with the wrong one all five refuse, with the user or the owner secret all five open (`plant True`, `qpdf syntax clean`), and every produced file re-decrypts to the source's own 4 pages, 255733 ink px and 1792 characters — no doubled ciphertext. A permissions-only file (empty user secret) opens with nothing typed in all five and still reports the mask there (`/P -1028`; pypdf `user_access_permissions 4294966268`, MuPDF `permissions -1028`), the owner secret being the only one qpdf/pypdf/poppler accept and the only one that lifts them (`allow` all-true, `auth 4`, `permissions -4`). **Each of the eight checkboxes writes its own bit and nothing else** (`docs/research/protect-round-rig.test.ts`, one flip at a time from a permissions-only run, reading `/P` bit by bit): default `-1028` → printing `-3080` (3), modifying `-1036` (4), copying `-1044` (5), comments `-1060` (6), forms `-1284` (9), screen readers `-1540` (10), assembly `-4` (11), low-resolution print `-3080` (12), with high-res following printing both ways; driven live once (`allowCopying` off → `/P -1044`, sheet ink and text unchanged) and the card printed *"copying blocked"* for it. None of the five *enforces* copy or print from its CLI — `pdftotext` returns 1798 characters of a copy-blocked file and `pdftops` exits 0 on a print-blocked one — so `/P` is a report everywhere outside a GUI, which is the card's own note holding. **The defect: a locked input could only be protected again under the password it already had.** The card row hides its box for any tool that declares a password field, and `protect`'s fields are the password the *copy* carries, so the run opened the input with the value being set: over `/tmp/triage/in/locked.pdf` (AES-256 `/R 6`, 4 pages) with a new open password the screen answered *"That password is not correct for \"locked.pdf\""*, `STOPPED`, **0 bytes**, and the panel said *"Correct it in this tool's password field"* — correcting it would set a different password, so the two jobs had one box. Fixed with one declared field (`tools/types.ts:passwordForTheOutput`, set on `protect`), the card keeping its box beside the form's (`FileCards` `ownsItsBox`, `FieldControls` notice now naming *"the box on the file's card"*), and a warning that says what the copy drops: *"This file was already protected (AES-256). The copy carries only the protection set here — the passwords it opened with do not open the copy."* After, the same run re-keys: 5117 bytes, 4 pages, `AES-128` on the card, qpdf clean, all five opening with the new secret, all five refusing the source's `reader-pass`, and the decrypted copy equal to the source sheet for sheet (`63579 / 49591 / 0 / 142563`, 1792 characters, `/Rotate 90` kept) — the same numbers on the control where the two passwords coincide. This file's six-item brief found nothing else wrong: the cipher table, the wrong-password refusals, the bits, the nesting and the card's own sentences all measured correct before anything was edited.<br>**Pins, red without the fix:** the card's box (one assertion pair, `tests/pdf/password-anywhere.test.ts`) — `ownsItsBox` reverted, **1 failed / 104 passed**; the notice naming the card (`tests/pdf/ux-rail.test.ts`, new `rail-protect` stand) — the `where` ternary reverted, **1 failed / 104 passed**; the round trip itself (`tests/pdf/protect-spec.test.ts`, re-key from the card's secret, old password refused, words through one reader, `/P` bit 3) — `request.password || declared` reverted, **1 failed / 8 passed**. Full suite 3328 passed / 1 skipped / 113 files, `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, no upload, dark mode one screenshot (`/tmp/r/protect-dark.png`) tokens only. **Left open, measured:** poppler's `-upw`/`-opw` split is the only reader that distinguishes the two secrets at all (MuPDF's `authenticate` accepts either without saying which), Ghostscript writes 4 pages when handed a *wrong* password on a permissions-only file, and `change-permissions` — whose owner box is simultaneously the current secret and the new one — was not given the card box, because there the two values genuinely are the same. | `core/crypto.ts` (the write path: `encryptDocument`, `revision6Values`, `retainedLock`, `resealDocument`, the shared cipher walk and its exempt set), `core/doc.ts:saveEncrypted` / `permissionsToFlags` / `resealLikeSource`, `core/guard.ts:load` (the seal view `/P` comes from; `opensWithEmptyPassword` asks the file), `tools/runner.ts:preserveProtection` (carries the lock instead of re-deriving it), `tools/security.ts` (`protect`, `change-permissions`, `cipherField`/`cipherChoice`/`cipherAsWritten`, `verifyProtection`, the View Metadata cipher row), `tests/pdf/{crypto-encrypt,crypto-aes256-write,gate-harness,protect-spec,security-bytes,edge-security,encrypted-guard,guard,crypto-decrypt,edge-organize,edge-signature-core,tools-e2e}.test.ts`, `docs/research/protect-readers.py` (+ its `-aes128.log`, `-gateonly.log` and `-deployed*.log`, `-deployed-aes256.log` among them), `docs/research/aes256-write-readers.py` + `.log`, `scripts/live-protect-proof.mjs`, and `docs/research/encrypted-inputs-*`; **`@maxwbh/pdf-lib` was not patched** — the cipher this app needed is written here, and the vendor's uncalled `PDFSecurity.encryptStream` remains its own gap
| `encrypted-inputs` | decrypted at RC4-128, AES-128 and AES-256/R6 (`3f4f188`, `7dfe088` guarded; `6e914fc`, `9b7eba1` read the cipher; `f44b415`, `a0b6404` the R6 handler; `7ea16db` writes it too) | **Severity: blocker — data-destroying, silent, and it is the intake of every tool rather than one tool's bug.** *A genuinely encrypted file previews correctly, edits wrongly, and says nothing about it.* <br>**What was measured.** Twelve documents, each asked the same twelve questions through the shipped `runPdfTool`, `inspectPdf` and `core/render` — 12 probes × 12 documents, 36 operation runs (`docs/research/encrypted-inputs-run.sh` → `encrypted-inputs-fixtures.log`, `encrypted-inputs-app.log`, `encrypted-inputs-readers.log`, the tables in `encrypted-inputs-table.md`, the write-up in `encrypted-inputs-findings.md`). Ten are locked by outside implementations — libqpdf 12.3.2 and MuPDF 1.28.2, never this app — at RC4-128 (`/V 2 /R 3`), AES-128 (`/V 4 /R 4 /CFM /AESV2`) and AES-256 (`/V 5 /R 6 /CFM /AESV3` with `/OE`, `/UE`, `/Perms`), each as a **user-password** file and as an **owner-password-only** file; two are controls, the same document unlocked and the same document locked by this app's own `protect`. The document carries three text pages each naming its own sheet (ALPHA, BRAVO, CHARLIE), a text note, a highlight, a link, a text field worth `Ada Lovelace`, a checked checkbox, two bookmarks (one nested) and an enclosure `source.csv`. Every fixture's census was taken by MuPDF before this app saw it and is **identical to `base.pdf`** — 3 pages, 2 annotations (6 raw `/Annots` entries counting the widgets, the link and the popup), 2 fields, 2 bookmarks, 1 attachment, 182 characters, the planted sentence on 3 of 3 sheets, 0.46 % ink on sheet 1 — and every one opens with its password under qpdf and MuPDF — and the six user-password files refuse the wrong one there too (`rejects the wrong one: yes`, `mupdf_accepts_the_wrong_one: false`; the four owner-only files have no user secret for a wrong one to be refused, which is the point of including them) — with the exact `qpdf --encrypt …` / MuPDF `save(encryption=…)` command recorded per file. <br>**The pre-flight and the preview are sound, on all ten.** The app asks (`needsPassword=true error=PASSWORD_REQUIRED`, and a routine `rotate` with no password is refused before any work with `isPasswordRequired=true` and the sentence naming the file); the right password opens RC4-128 and AES-128 (`accepted=true pages=3`); a wrong one fails clearly (`BAD_PASSWORD`, “That password is not correct for …”); `app=3 MuPDF=3` ten times; `/P=-3376` is decoded right; and pdf.js — which implements the ciphers itself — draws on every file including AES-256: `pages=3 ops=25 text="Lighthouse Inventory 1887 page 1 Sheet ALPHA body copy"` and, through the shipped `renderToCanvas` and `thumbnails()`, `fill 55, stroke 1, fillRect 1, drawImage 1, clip 1, path2d 27`. <br>**Three belief errors, counted.** (1) **AES-256 is refused outright**: the correct password answers `accepted=false error=BAD_PASSWORD`, so does the owner password, `PDFDocument.load` throws `Input document to PDFDocument.load is encrypted`, and **12 of the 36 operation runs produced no file at all** — same for the file MuPDF wrote itself, so it is the handler, not the producer. (2) **An owner-password-only AES-256 file is reported as locked**: qpdf answers `password_incorrect: false`, MuPDF `needs_pass=False`, poppler prints its 3 pages with nothing typed, pypdf `decrypt("")=1`, Ghostscript 3 pages — and this app answers `needsPassword=true error=PASSWORD_REQUIRED` then `BAD_PASSWORD` for the real owner secret, because `core/guard.ts:load` decides “restricted, not locked” by *loading with the empty password*, and that load is the R6 check that fails. (3) **The two secrets are not distinguished**: the owner password comes back `accepted=true` wherever the user password is accepted, and on a permissions-only file the deliberately wrong password also returns `accepted=true` (`passwordAccepted` is `Boolean(password)` on that branch) where pypdf keeps them apart on the same bytes (`user=1`, `owner=2`). Under all three, the engine every tool holds is **ciphertext it was never asked to undo** — page 1 of the RC4-128 file, opened with the right password, is `7b 73 7d f5 /Filter /FlateDecode` — and the app's own counts show it: field values arrive as mojibake where MuPDF reads `Ada Lovelace`. (Two further differences in the same line, `bookmarks 1` of 2 and `attachments []` of 1, are **not** cipher effects: the unlocked control reads the same way, so they belong to `readOutline` / `listAttachments` over a MuPDF-written name tree, recorded here so the table cannot be re-blamed on encryption.) <br>**The three routine operations** — rotate 90°, delete page 2, protect again, 36 runs. **18 of the 18 files produced from foreign RC4-128 and AES-128 inputs are judged `silent-loss`.** The app reports `claims encrypted=true algorithm=no cipher pages=3 /P=-3376 accepted=true error=—`, `pagesOut=3`, “Rotated 3 pages by 90°.” — and the bytes say: MuPDF opens the result with the same password (12 of 18; the six `protect` results answer to the new password instead), finds the planted sentence on **0 of its pages**, sheet-1 ink **0.46 % → 0.0 %**, text **182 → 0 characters**, and raises `UnicodeDecodeError` walking the annotations; bookmarks 2→0 and the enclosure are gone; **qpdf `--check` reports `errors detected` on 18 of 18**, and the dictionary the file now carries is `/V 4 /R 4` with an **empty `/CF`** — this app's “no crypt filter” gate over RC4 and AES bytes, i.e. the file asserts its own ciphertext is plaintext, which is why every reader that believes a `/CFM` renders blank paper. poppler takes the same file with no password at all and prints `Unsupported version/revision (4/4)` plus `Unknown compression method in flate stream` for 3 characters; pypdf answers `user=2` (owner level) where the fixture answered `user=1`; Ghostscript reaches 1 page of 3. The owner-only inputs lose the gate too on the two rebuilds (`no /Encrypt`, `opens_with=empty,user,owner,again,again-owner`). Delete-a-page is the worst shape: it reports `pages=2` and MuPDF agrees the file has 2 pages — the count is right and the pages are empty. On the MuPDF-written inputs the copy can also crash the preview: in the logged run six of the eighteen (all three operations on each of `einp-mu-r3-rc4-128-user.pdf` and `einp-mu-r4-aes-128-user.pdf`) hand back a page **this app's own pdf.js throws on** (`UnknownErrorException: Invalid number: j (charCode 106)`), and the count moved between two runs of this rig (three, then six) because which bytes land in a content-stream position depends on the random key — the fact is “sometimes crashes the preview on MuPDF-written files”, not a stable six. The ciphertext head moves the same way (`ae be c5 e2` logged, `7b 73 7d f5` earlier); what is stable is that it is never `78 …`, i.e. never flate, i.e. never readable. And `protect`'s reader self-check misreads that as `No PDF reader could be started in this browser to re-check the saved file … so only the built-in engine's own view of it was verified` — a reader was started, and the bytes are what it refused.<br>**The verdict on this session's `rotate`-preserves-protection ruling: preserved *and* corrupted, and the corruption was silent.** The gate really is re-issued — MuPDF opens a rotated foreign file with the password the user holds — so the ruling's own words test true; what it never knew is that the bytes it re-keyed were never decrypted, so the copy is ciphertext now described as plaintext. Its evidence base was self-consistent rather than correct: `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts:240` builds the locked input with `doc.save({ encrypt: … })`, i.e. the dictionary the `protect` row proved sits over plaintext streams, so `toMatch(/\/Encrypt/)` plus `PDFDocument.load(bytes, { password: 'hunter2' })` is pdf-lib reading its own output, and no test in the suite ever fed it a file with a cipher behind the dictionary. The control run proves the split: the same three operations over `own-protected.pdf` (this app's gate over the same document) keep the words — 3 of 3 planted pages, 0.46 % ink, gate opening with `user` — and lose only the bookmarks and the enclosure the page copy has always lost. So the ruling stays, but the `stripsProtection` / `preserveProtection` handling built on it is reasoning over ciphertext, which confirms and sharpens this row's earlier claim (3) under `protect`: the password still matches, so even “does the password still work” passes on a blank page. <br>**Test environment versus the deployed app.** jsdom has no 2-D raster, so “the preview draws” and “the thumbnail draws” are reported as pdf.js operator counts and drawing calls into a recording canvas (`encrypted-inputs-canvas.ts`) rather than pixels — a `dark=0` there is the shim, not the app — while pixel ink is measured on the produced files by MuPDF's `get_pixmap`, which is the half that has to hold in the browser. The gate, guard, engine and `core/render` rows are the shipped modules, so §belief and §operations apply to the site, not only to the run. poppler's and Ghostscript's numbers describe those readers (neither honours a `/None` gate, as `protect` found) and no conclusion rests on them: MuPDF, qpdf and pdf.js agree independently. **Not measured, because the pass was scoped to three operations:** `unlock` (already measured above: four pages, no content), `change-permissions`, `remove-restrictions` and the other ~119 tools; whether the annotation and form writers corrupt ciphertext further; `/R 5`, non-`/Standard` handlers and real Word, Preview and Acrobat files; Chromium canvas ink for these previews. <br>**Recommendation.** Do the read half before the write half, in one seam: add the decrypt pass on load in `core/doc.ts:openPDF` (the fork exports `PDFSecurity.decryptStream` and has no caller for it, so the work is wired in rather than invented), fix R6 `/U` verification in the same change because it is the same cipher table and today turns every AES-256 file into “this tool cannot run”, and stop reporting a permissions-only R6 file as locked by making `core/guard.ts:load` ask the dictionary what the empty user password means instead of inferring it from a failed load. Use **this rig as the acceptance test** — `planted_pages 3 → 3`, `ink_page1_percent > 0`, `qpdf --check` clean on all 18 produced files, and the four AES-256 fixtures reaching an output at all — and land it *ahead* of the AES-128 encrypt-on-save step `protect` asks for: a cipher this app can write but not read would turn its own protected downloads into blank pages the moment a user re-opens one here, and the round-trip tests would stay green because they read what they wrote. Closing this row as “fine” is not available on this evidence: the controls show the same operations are honest on this app's own files, so what differs is the read path. <br>**Guarded since, and re-measured with this same rig (`docs/research/encrypted-inputs-findings.md` §8).** The recommendation's stop-loss is in `core/guard.ts`: a lock is now classified by its **effective crypt filter** — `/StmF` and `/StrF` resolved through `/CF`, `/None` and `/Identity` predefined, `/V 1`–`/V 3` implying RC4 — as `none` / `gate-only` / `ciphered` (`classifyEncryption`, beside the `decodePermissions` seam this row already used, so one parser reads the dictionary), naming the filter and the revision. `preflightPdf` refuses `ciphered` for every tool that hands back a rebuilt PDF, and the catalogue says which those are through one declared field (`PdfToolDefinition.readsOnly`, marked on the 18 tools whose result is text, a picture, an archive or a report — those read through pdf.js, which does undo these ciphers, and still run). Re-run numbers, same twelve documents and same five readers: **the ten outside-locked fixtures produced 0 files instead of 18 — 30 refusals across the 30 operation runs** (plus the 10 no-password probes, 40 sentences in all: 12 naming `RC4-128 (revision 3)`, 12 `AES-128 (crypt filter /StdCF, revision 4)`, 16 `AES-256 … revision 6`), `isPasswordRequired=false` on every one, and **`silent-loss` is gone from the readers log**. The controls moved not at all: `own-protected.pdf` still rotates to 3/3 planted pages at 0.46 % sheet-1 ink with its gate opening on `user`, losing only the bookmarks and the enclosure the page copy has always lost (6 produced files, judged 3 `reads` + 3 `partial-loss`) — so a foreign AES file did not cost the user their own protected files. The refusal says what the file is, why the password does not help ("the right one lets this app open and preview the file, but the editing path copies the page streams without decrypting them, and the copy opens as blank pages") and what to do ("decrypt it in the program that locked it"), and names the case that still works, because gate-only files do.<br>**A hole the guard found on the way in, measured.** `core/doc.ts:looksEncrypted` — the cheap trailer scan that decides whether the pre-flight parses at all — knew only qpdf's reference spelling (`/Encrypt 12 0 R`); MuPDF inlines the dictionary (`/Encrypt<</Filter/Standard…>>`), so every MuPDF-written fixture read as unencrypted in front of the parse: the intermediate run produced 6 files, all from `einp-mu-r3-rc4-128-user` and `einp-mu-r4-aes-128-user`, and answered `BAD_PASSWORD` / `PASSWORD_REQUIRED` for the two MuPDF AES-256 ones. The rig's own new assertion (`einp-mu-r3-rc4-128-user.pdf was rewritten`) is what caught it; fixed to match `/Encrypt` unless a word character follows, which still excludes the `/EncryptMetadata` key beside it, and `tests/fixtures/encrypted/` now carries **both producers and both spellings** (`scripts/encrypted-fixtures.py`, each file verified by the tool that wrote it).<br>**Tests.** `tests/pdf/encrypted-guard.test.ts` (111): the classification on none / gate-only / RC4-128 / AES-128 / AES-256 from two outside producers plus hand-built dictionaries (a filter *defined* and never *selected* is `gate-only` — the mistake that reported this app's gate as a cipher), the ciphertext measured rather than asserted (the AES-128 fixture's own page streams do not inflate, so a decrypt pass on load will redden that test and force the guard to be revisited), 11 rewriting tools × 6 ciphered fixtures refused, 4 reading tools not refused, and the gate-only direction: rotate keeps `/Encrypt`, the password, 3 pages, 90° and the words; unlock still unlocks. `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`'s E5 fixture is no longer `doc.save({ encrypt })` — that dictionary selects `/CFM /AESV2` over plaintext streams, so the guard now refuses it as a cipher claim, which is the proof the old assertions were pdf-lib reading its own output — replaced by this app's `/None` gate **plus** a qpdf-locked file, with a words-survive assertion and the unguarded copy measured (`drawnText` throws on the page it rebuilt). **With the guard lifted (`rejectCiphered` forced off in `tools/runner.ts`, one line), 67 assertions across the two files go red** — 66 in the refusal table (11 tools × the 6 ciphered fixtures) and 1 in `edge-organize` — and the E5 gate case is green in both states.<br>**Sweep for the same mistake, counted:** 2 locked fixtures in the whole suite were built by the vendor's non-ciphering `save({ encrypt })` and **6 assertions rested on that cipher** (5 in the rotate-preserves-protection test, 1 in the archive's "still password protected" note) — both fixtures, in `edge-organize`, are this row's and are replaced. The remaining 6 lock sites call this app's own `saveEncrypted` (`guard.test.ts` ×2, `edge-security.test.ts`, `edge-signature-core.test.ts` ×2, `security-bytes.test.ts`) and `protect-spec.test.ts` locks through the `protect` tool itself; every one of those files asserts `algorithm === 'no cipher'` or the absence of a cipher name, i.e. a gate, and no assertion anywhere in `tests/` names a cipher — `AES-`/`RC4-` survive only inside negations, which is what the `protect` row retired. The env-gated `ENCRYPTED_INPUTS_DIR` block in `protect-spec` reports numbers and asserts none, so it needed no re-pinning.<br>**Not done here, deliberately:** no decryption was attempted (no AES-CBC written), the R6 `/U` check and the two-secret conflation are untouched, and the pane tools that open documents outside the runner (`PageOrganizer`, `CropTool`, `FormFill/Main`, `AnnotationEditor`, `TextEditor`, `CompareTool`) still reach `openPDF` without this pre-flight — listed as open in `encrypted-inputs-findings.md` §8 rather than widened into. `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, CI `42598448142` green (build + deploy: the whole suite, the test type-check and the build on this change), no UI file touched, dark mode untouched, 100 % client-side. **Decrypted since (`6e914fc`, `9b7eba1`, `0a69bff`; the row stays open on AES-256/R6 only).** `core/crypto.ts` derives the key and undoes the cipher for **RC4-128** (`/V 2 /R 3`) and **AES-128** (`/V 4 /R 4 /CFM /AESV2`) — MD5 from `node-forge`, already a dependency and already how `core/attachments.ts` computes a `/CheckSum`; AES-128-CBC from Web Crypto; RC4 from the fork. `core/doc.ts:openPDF` runs the pass before any tool sees the document and returns `null` — every existing behaviour untouched — for no dictionary, a `/None` gate, a refused cipher, a secret that did not open it, or a file too damaged to parse, so the only new path is the one that was broken. `core/guard.ts` refuses on `PdfEncryption.supported`, answered by the module that would have to do the undoing, plus the two things only a parsed document can show: `object-streams` (the fork inflates them while parsing, before any decryptor exists) and `plaintext-claim` (the fork's own `save({ encrypt })` names `/CFM /AESV2` over bytes it never ciphered, and decrypting *that* invents garbage out of readable words). **Re-measured with this rig — same twelve documents, same twelve probes, same five readers: `silent-loss` 18 of 18 → 0; 18 of 18 foreign RC4-128/AES-128 runs produce a file again, with `planted_pages 3→3`, 182 extracted characters, sheet-1 ink 0.46 %, form values MuPDF reads as `Ada Lovelace` + `Yes` where it raised `UnicodeDecodeError`, and `qpdf --check` clean on 25 of 25 produced files (was errors on 18 of 18).** The copy answers to the password the user holds (`opens_with: user`, pypdf `user` opens, `planted: true`) as this app's `/None` gate — the `protect` row's honest shape, not a foreign cipher claim over plaintext. **Password semantics separated:** `passwordAccepted` is now a `/U` verification at the revision the file uses, `opensWithEmptyPassword` is the empty user secret *verified* rather than inferred from a probe that happened to throw, and the new `passwordLevel` says `user`, `owner` or `empty`; a deliberately wrong password on a permissions-only file answers `accepted=false` where `Boolean(password)` said true, and the owner-only RC4/AES-128 fixtures answer `needsPassword=false opensEmpty=true` (30 → 16 refusals, all of them AES-256). **Still refused by name, one sentence each:** AES-256 (`/R 5`/`/R 6`, `/CFM /AESV3` — 16 sentences naming the strength and the filter, 0 files produced; its `/U` check and `/OE`/`/UE`/`/Perms` derivations are the queued task), 40-bit RC4 (no outside fixture, so no claim), public-key handlers, filters named but not defined, mixed stream/string methods, and this browser without AES-CBC. **The pre-existing losses stay pre-existing:** `bookmarks 1 / 2` and `attachments [] / ['source.csv']` in the engine census reproduce *identically on the unlocked control* in the same run, so they belong to `readOutline` / `listAttachments` over a MuPDF-written name tree and are handed on with those numbers; the page-copy losses (`bookmarks 2→0`, the enclosure) are unchanged on the decrypted runs, which is the `organize` row's business, and the words survive them. **Tests:** `tests/pdf/crypto-decrypt.test.ts` (24: extracted text equal to the planted sentence for both strengths from both producers, the zlib head where there was cipher, a `/Length` that matches the decrypted bytes, a save that still reads, both secrets, AES-256 refused by reason), `tests/pdf/encrypted-guard.test.ts` re-pinned (113: 11 rewriting tools × 5 decryptable fixtures asserted on the words in the produced bytes, AES-256 still refused per tool, gate-only untouched, the two obstacle sentences), `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts` E5 now rotates a libqpdf-locked file and asserts the sentence is still on the sheet, keeping the hand-built no-pass contrast (`could not be decompressed`). 276 green across those four plus `guard.test.ts`; `tsc -p tsconfig.json` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, no UI file touched, dark mode untouched, 100 % client-side.  `core/doc.ts:openPDF` (+ the fork's uncalled `PDFSecurity.decryptStream`), `core/guard.ts:load` (the empty-password probe that answers “restricted, not locked”), `tools/runner.ts:preserveProtection` (+ the `stripsProtection` ruling built on it); **guarded in** `core/guard.ts:classifyEncryption` / `cipheredInputError` / `preflightPdf`, `core/doc.ts:looksEncrypted`, `tools/types.ts:readsOnly` (+ 18 tool definitions), `tools/runner.ts` (the pre-flight call), `tests/pdf/encrypted-guard.test.ts`, `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/encrypted/` + `scripts/encrypted-fixtures.py`, `docs/research/encrypted-inputs-rig.test.ts` (records a refusal, and asserts both directions on the produced files); measured by `docs/research/encrypted-inputs-{fixtures.py,rig.test.ts,canvas.ts,readers.py,table.py,run.sh,vitest.config.ts}` with its logs and tables beside it — the pre-guard run kept as `encrypted-inputs-{fixtures,app,readers}-pre-guard.log` and `-table-pre-guard.md` — superseding the three-file, two-operation `ENCRYPTED_INPUTS_DIR` block still living in `tests/pdf/protect-spec.test.ts`, whose numbers this pass reproduces and extends to ten files, both kinds of secret and three operations |**The write half has landed beside it (`89a5b7e`, `383bd93`): `protect` now writes the AES-128 this module reads, so the control file `own-protected.pdf` in this rig is a ciphered input rather than a gate over plaintext, and the round trip still keeps its words and its ink — re-measured in `encrypted-inputs-{app,readers}.log` with the previous run kept as `-pre-aes128`: 25 produced files, `silent-loss` 0, `qpdf --check` clean on 35, `planted_pages 3→3` and sheet-1 ink 0.46 % on every file that came from a locked input, and a foreign AES-128 fixture edited here still round-trips with its words, its annotations and its ink intact. What is still refused by name is AES-256 under a handler revision no fixture here carries (`/R 5`, `/R 7`) and 40-bit RC4. The R6 `/U` check this sentence was waiting on has landed (`f44b415`, `a0b6404`), and the write half beside it (`7ea16db`): `/R 6` `/U`, `/UE`, `/O`, `/OE` and `/Perms` are now both verified and produced — `tests/pdf/crypto-aes256-write.test.ts` requires this app's writer to return a fixture's own five values byte for byte — so an AES-256 file edited here leaves as AES-256 and a file protected at 256 bits opens again here. See the `protect` row.
| `extract-attachments` · `edit-attachments` · `add-attachments` | verified-good (extract-attachments, round 062; `edit-attachments` round 063 — renamed, it could not before; `add-attachments` unchanged) | **The reader recognised the shape its own writer produces — the third instance of the pattern in this session, and the one that turned into an audit.** `docs/TOOL-TRIAGE.md` ERRORS 4: MuPDF writes a name-tree pair as `/Names [ (note.txt) << /Type /Filespec /EF << /F 10 0 R >> >> ]`, the file specification **inline**, and `collectNameTree` accepted a value only when it was a `PDFRef`. So `extract-attachments` printed *"This PDF contains no embedded files."* over a file three outside readers list, and `edit-attachments` refused it. Reproduced per shape before anything was touched, on 15 fixtures written by libqpdf 12.3.2 (pikepdf 10.9.1), MuPDF 1.28.2 (PyMuPDF) and pypdf 6.14.2 and judged by four readers before being kept (`qpdf` and `mutool` are absent here, so four, not five) — `tests/fixtures/attachments/`, generator `scripts/attachments-foreign-fixtures.py`, census `docs/research/attachments-foreign-shapes.log`, per-shape table `docs/research/attachments-foreign-repro.md`. **10 of 37 assertions red before `9f4116b`; 58 green after.** The shapes covered more than the report: `/EF` pointing straight at the stream did not merely miss the file, it **threw** `Expected instance of PDFDict, but got instance of PDFRawStream` out of the fork's typed `lookupMaybe` — the same class `flatten`'s row closed — so the read path now asks what an object is (`resolve`) instead of confirming a hope; `/EF` naming only `/UNIX` was never looked at; and a file in the catalogue *and* named by an annotation needed an identity to deduplicate by once a spec could be inline. Green in both states, so the fix did not move them: the `/Kids` tree, the `/F`+`/UF` decoy pair, a stream with no `/Params`, an indirect `/EF`, the annotation-only enclosure, the UTF-16BE name. **Content, not count:** every assertion is the SHA-256 of the extracted bytes, and the fixtures are byte-reproducible (`MANIFEST.json`, plus `--check` rebuilding through the CLI in separate processes — 16 attachments and 11 structure fixtures identical across two runs, which took four attempts to earn: `pikepdf.Stream` is not a `Dictionary` subclass so a `/Params`-only walk matched nothing, `deterministic_id` folds the producer's random ID in rather than replacing it, and `static_id` pins only the *changing* half of `/ID`). **The reciprocal direction was measured too**, because a writer whose output only its own reader can read is the same blindness facing the other way: on `own-writer.pdf` MuPDF, pypdf and poppler each list both enclosures and extract distinct payloads intact while libqpdf confirms `/Params /CheckSum` is the MD5 of the bytes it read; on `own-removal.pdf` all three list nothing and poppler still parses 3 pages (`docs/research/attachments-reciprocal.py` → `-reciprocal.log`). Recorded, not fixed, because the claim is pdf-lib's: a name containing 𓂀 gets `\x0c\x80` in its legacy `/F`, since PDFDocEncoding has no byte for it — pypdf lists the real name from `/UF`, poppler prints `/F`. **Deployed**: the rail's own Extract button over `mupdf-native.pdf` on `https://www.browsersnip.com` answers *"Found 1 embedded file(s): note.txt (280 B)."*, no console error, download sha256 `6d4b8fa7b051…` — the digest the outside readers call expected (`docs/research/attachments-foreign-deployed.log`, bundle `assets/index-C6ma42d2.js` checked first to carry the fix). **THE AUDIT — this is the row's other half.** Of 13 readers that parse a structure this app's writer also produces, **4 have met a foreign file in a CI test and 9 never have**: `readOutline` (only a parity assertion over encrypted copies of a MuPDF file), `readPageLabels`, `listFormFields`, `listLayers`, `listAnnotations`/`annotationCount`, `readInfo`/`readXmp`, `sanitize`'s script walk, `readSignatures`, and `listFonts`/`listImages` which have no test *and* no caller outside their own module. Measured over 11 further foreign fixtures (`tests/fixtures/foreign/`, `docs/research/foreign-structure-audit.test.ts` + own vitest config so CI does not depend on it, table `foreign-structure-audit-table.md`, prose `foreign-structure-audit.md`): layers, annotations (5 entries including the pop-up and link MuPDF's `annots()` hides), form fields, `/Info`+XMP and a nested `/Outlines` all answer **correctly** on foreign bytes; two more fail the same way attachments did — `readPageLabels` reads `/Nums` on the root only, so a `/Kids` label tree comes back `["1","2","3"]` for a file MuPDF labels `i-1, I, c` (the flat-`/Nums` control returns the right labels, which is what makes it a walk gap), and `readOutline` resolves a named destination only when the array is inline, so `/Dests` values behind a reference or inside a `/Kids` tree give `page: null` where pypdf resolves both. **Left unfixed deliberately** — the ask was the size of the problem, and "our tests pass on our own output" is now a counted assumption rather than a suspicion. <br>**Both of those readers are fixed and pinned since (`7d1d1ad`), and the round-trip question came first.** 34 more fixtures (`tests/fixtures/readers/`, `scripts/readers-foreign-fixtures.py`, byte-reproducible: `--check` twice, 34 files identical, `MANIFEST.json` digests asserted per file by the test) and 122 assertions (`tests/pdf/readers-foreign.test.ts`), **26 red before the fix**; per-shape tables `docs/research/readers-foreign-repro.md`, readers MuPDF 1.28.2 and pypdf 6.14.2 — and only those two, because `qpdf`/`mutool` are absent and poppler 26.10.0's `pdfinfo` prints neither page labels nor bookmarks, so poppler is a parse-witness here rather than a third opinion, asserted as such. <br>**The severity answer, measured on the produced bytes and judged by readers that did not write the input (`docs/research/readers-round-trip-{before,after}.log`, `readers-round-trip.py`): item 1 was a wrong report, item 2 was data loss.** No writer consults `readPageLabels` — its only caller in `src/` is `add-page-labels` reading back the ranges it wrote one line earlier — and `rotate`, `page-numbers` and `header-footer` leave the label store byte-identical (`/Kids x2` in → `/Kids x2` out, MuPDF `i, ii, I, II, c, xA` both sides), so roman numbering was never being flattened into arabic by the misread. The outlines were: `bookmarks` with append off and `redact-pdf`'s title scrub both read the outline into a model and write it back, so an entry whose named destination sat behind a reference became a heading and **the `/Dest` left the file** — items naming a target 3 → **1** on an append, 3 → **0** on a redaction, 3 → **0** on a page copy that reported carrying three bookmarks, all of it green in the tool's own sentence. <br>**The label loss that row recorded as left unfixed is fixed, and it was the third instance of the same trap.** A page copy — `extract-pages`, `merge`, `split`, `organize`, `remove-pages`, `reverse-pages`, `duplex-collate`, `add-blank-page`, `alternate-merge`, and `rebuildDocument` under every encrypted input — went `/Kids x2` in → **absent** out, so a book MuPDF numbers `i, ii, I, II, c, xA` came back numbered `1, 2, 3`: it opens, the page count is right, and every page number a citation names silently changes meaning. That was `core/doc.ts:carryPageFurniture` (bookmarks, embedded files, never the numbering), not the walk. <br>**It was not a one-liner, and the naive carry is wrong in the way that looks right:** a range is addressed in *source* page indices (`/Start` where it begins, `/St` what is printed there, §12.4.2 running it to the next range or the end of the file), so extracting pages 4-6 with the ranges left at 2, 4 and 5 leaves the first two output sheets naming no range at all. `remapPageLabelRanges` (the labelling counterpart of `remapOutline`, reading through the same `core/tree.ts` walk) opens a range again whenever the source and destination indices stop stepping together, which is the one condition under which "the next sheet takes the next number" is still true of the copy — that is what makes a subset, a reorder, a duplicated sheet, a blank inserted between two numbered sheets and a range whose pages all fell out come out right per sheet instead of merely legal. Ranges are read as ranges now (`readPageLabelRanges`); `readPageLabels` expands the same list, so there is one traversal and one model. The merge decision this row left as a product call went the way the bookmarks had already gone — each file's ranges shifted onto its own sheets and concatenated — and a merged file that numbers nothing keeps reading its **position** rather than running on in the previous file's roman numerals, because §12.4.2 runs the last range to the end of the document; a file with no numbering is still given none. `PageCopyMap` carries every placed sheet now (`PagePlacement[]`) instead of a first-copy-wins `Map`, since a duplicated page has to be numbered on both copies — that rippled to five hand-built maps (four in `tools/organize.ts`, one in the organize pane), no tool signature changed. <br>**The evidence is per sheet, not per key** (`docs/research/page-labels-copy-table.md`, from `page-labels-copy-round-trip.py` over the dump `tests/pdf/page-labels-copy.test.ts` writes): twelve rows — the five copy cases plus prefixes, a `/S`-less range, an undefined `/S /G`, two merges, an inserted blank and a writer run after a copy — each with MuPDF and pypdf answering the fixture and the produced file. **17 assertions red without the carry** (`7d8d7c5` added the read/re-point, `e362e78` the carry and the notes, `ca8e552` the prefix encoding): 14 in `page-labels-copy.test.ts`, 2 in `page-copy-fidelity.test.ts` (whose fixture now carries `i, ii, 11, 12`), 1 in `readers-foreign.test.ts`, whose "a page copy drops /PageLabels — recorded, not fixed here" row is now the pin that it does not. 268 green across those three with `edge-organize` and `field-reach`;
