| `repair` | fixed+deployed | **A file that three readers open as 4 pages came back as 1 sheet of paper, reported as a success.** `docs/TOOL-TRIAGE.md` §2 measured it; this row re-measures it on a corpus rather than one file. **The corpus** (`tests/pdf/repair-damage-corpus.ts`, written to disk by `docs/research/repair-damage-corpus.mjs`): one known-good 4-sheet document — a planted word, a line of copy and a solid bar per sheet, so "4 pages" can be false in three distinguishable ways — damaged one structural way at a time by **byte edit**, never by a library re-serialising it: 45 % of the tail gone, a `/xref` whose every offset is three bytes past its object, no `startxref`, an `/ObjStm` whose internal offsets are all four past their objects, a duplicated `endobj`, a missing `endobj`, a half-written dictionary inside sheet 2's content stream, the §2 shape on a compressed base and on a plaintext base, plus the §2 input itself rebuilt from `colour-fixture.mjs` (`ledger-32-input`). Two bases carry the two index shapes, because a file cannot hold both. **What it measured before the fix** (`repair-damage-{app,readers,pdfjs}-before.log`): `truncated-tail`, `triage-report` and `objstm-wrong-offsets` produced **no file at all** — MuPDF opens `truncated-tail` and `triage-report` as 4 sheets each (two of the truncated one at 0 % ink and 0 characters) and 0 for `objstm-wrong-offsets`, qpdf gives 2, 4 and a refusal, and poppler alone still reads 4 of the objstm file — and `ledger-32-input` answered **1 sheet, 0.00 % ink, 0 characters, 1 658 bytes** with the sentence *"Repaired ledger-32-input.pdf: 1 page(s) readable."* over it: the row's own number, reproduced from a committed script. `objstm-wrong-offsets`'s refusal quoted `this.catalog.Pages is not a function` inside the user's sentence. **Two causes, both in `repairPDF`.** (1) It never rebuilt the cross-reference its own docstring advertised: `openPDF`, and on throw a regex harvest of `stream…endstream` payloads. A file whose objects are whole and whose *index* lies — a truncated download, a hand-patched tail — fell straight to that harvest. Now a strategy scans the objects (stepping over each payload by its `/Length`, because binary payload data contains the token `endobj`) and appends a fresh classic table and trailer over the file's own bytes: nothing is retyped, so a recovered sheet keeps its filters, fonts and resources. All three candidates are scored on sheets that hold a drawing operator and the best wins, which is the "never fewer pages than it could have preserved" rule as code. (2) The harvest pasted `/FlateDecode` ciphertext into a page whose dictionary claimed plaintext, and round-tripped every byte through `TextDecoder('latin1')` — **which is windows-1252 in Node and in the browser**, so 0x80-0x9F come back as different bytes — then re-encoded it as UTF-8. That is the blank sheet: the payload survives as garbage operators, the count is right, the paper is empty. Payloads are now decoded through the engine (not `Blob.stream()`, which jsdom does not implement, so the last resort could never be measured where it is written), non-page streams are skipped, and a sheet takes the `/MediaBox` of the page object that names it. **The report follows the bytes.** `repairPDF` returns its facts — path, sheets written, sheets the input claims (`/Count`, else the page objects in the bytes), and sheets whose content stream holds no drawing operator, counted on the saved output re-opened rather than on the document `save()` has already rewritten — and the tool speaks them: `Repaired truncated-tail.pdf: 2 of 4 page(s) recovered: 1 still holds content, 1 is blank.` with `2 page(s) could not be recovered` and `1 of the 2 page(s) in the output is blank: its content stream holds no drawing operator, so the sheet opens and nothing appears on it.` beside it. **"readable" is printed only when no recovered sheet is empty**; where a page tree could not be read at all the warning says the sheets were rebuilt around content streams and their size and order are a guess, not the document's. **Outsiders' verdicts, both sides** (`repair-damage-readers.log`, MuPDF + libqpdf/pikepdf + poppler + pypdf + Ghostscript; `repair-damage-pdfjs.log`, the engine this app renders with): after the fix every damaged case opens as 4 sheets under all four readers that would open it at all, at its own ink (5.3 % per sheet; `ledger-32-input` 24.5/19.8/0.0/57.0 — the input's own profile) with its planted word on each sheet; the one case whose page objects are physically gone (`truncated-tail`) returns 2 sheets and **says** 2-of-4 with one of them blank. The harness still scores that row as `lost-pages 4->2`, and that is the honest reading of the disagreement: MuPDF counts 4 sheets in the truncated input, two of which it rasterises at 0.0 % ink and 0 characters — placeholders for objects the bytes no longer hold — which is exactly the count-with-no-content trap this row is about, so the tool returns the two real sheets and names the other two. Before the same table read `refused a 4-page file` three times and `lost-pages 4->1` once. `objstm-wrong-offsets` is the case where this app now beats a reference reader: MuPDF reads 0 pages and qpdf errors on the input, and the rebuild hands back 4 sheets with their words, which is what poppler manages. **Tests**: `tests/pdf/repair-damage.test.ts` (14) reads every produced file back with **pdf.js** — page count, planted words, and the display list pdf.js hands the renderer, so a blank sheet fails rather than merely differing — and asserts the sentence's count equals the bytes', that lost sheets are named, that no user sentence quotes an engine internal, and that Ghostscript was never reached. **Re-run at `045db17` with the final test code: 8 are red without the fix** — 5 in the new file (`truncated-tail`, `triage-report`, `objstm-wrong-offsets` and the two named shapes) and 3 in the two files this touched (`edge-optimize`, `optimize-bytes`); 82 of those 90 pass there, and 201 green across `repair-damage`, `edge-optimize`, `optimize-bytes`, `pages-out`, `ux-definitions`, `parity-definitions`, `registry`. `plain-content-tailcut` is green in both states, and saying so matters: with plaintext streams the old harvest already produced 4 sheets, so the corpus needed both a compressed and a plaintext base to separate "no file" from "a file nobody can read". One existing fixture's own bug fell out of the same measure: `optimize-bytes.test.ts` built its lying-`/Count` file through `TextDecoder('latin1')` + `Buffer.from(…, 'latin1')`, which silently corrupted every stream in it — the three pages it repaired really were blank, and the old assertion had been printing "readable" over them. `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, 100 % client-side, no UI file touched so dark mode is unchanged. **Left open, measured here, none of it this row's file:** (1) the `/ObjStm` case survives only because its page content happens to live outside the object stream — a producer that puts content streams *in* the object stream too would still fall to the harvest, with no `/Resources` and so with substituted fonts (pdf.js logs `Font "Helvetica-7098480789" is not available — attempting to fallback to a default font` on exactly those sheets); (2) `repair` rebuilds pages by copying, so the `organize`/`remove-blank-pages` rebuild losses (outline, annotations, form fields) apply here too and were not probed by this row; (3) the deployed check covers one damaged file, not the corpus. **Re-measured on
`https://www.browsersnip.com`** (CI `42731152435` green, `docs/research/rail-tool-bytes.mjs`
with `TOOL=repair PDF=<corpus>/ledger-32-input.pdf`): the screen reads
*“Repaired ledger-32-input.pdf: 4 page(s) recovered: 3 still hold content, 1 is blank.
3.8 KB → 2.1 KB (-44%)”* with `The file's own index could not be read: a fresh one was
written over the 4 page object(s) still in the bytes` and
`1 of the 4 page(s) in the output is blank: its content stream holds no drawing operator,
so the sheet opens and nothing appears on it`, 0 console errors — and the 2 173-byte download
judges out as **4 pages under MuPDF, qpdf, poppler, pypdf and Ghostscript (rc 0)** with ink
`24.47 / 19.84 / 0.0 / 57.05 %` and `1778 / 12 / 0 / 0` characters, i.e. the *input's own*
MuPDF profile restored, where §2 measured 1 sheet at 0.00 % ink and 0 characters. The corpus
cases themselves are measured locally (five readers, both sides); only this one file was driven
through the site. `truncated-tail` was driven through the site as the second sample:
*“Repaired truncated-tail.pdf: 2 of 4 page(s) recovered: 1 still holds content, 1 is blank.”*
with `2 page(s) could not be recovered` beside it, and its 1 079-byte download reads **2 pages
under MuPDF, qpdf, poppler and pypdf** at ink `5.26 / 0.0 %` and `78 / 0` characters — the two
sheets whose bytes are still there, and the empty one named rather than counted. Both downloads
are `check clean` under libqpdf, which is the check the last commit (`f5cb4ec`) added to the rig:
`repair` now writes an index of its own, so “it opens” is a weaker claim than “it is well
formed”, and one blemish the earlier run had — a copied page still pointing at a content stream
the cut had taken, which qpdf reported as `ignoring non-stream item` — is gone. **One behaviour
this row changed on its own initiative, since the rule demanded
it:** a file whose pages are all blank after repair is no longer described as “N page(s)
readable” — the sentence now splits count from content, which is a user-visible wording change
beyond a pure bug fix.
**Re-settled out of order at `b85cd6a` (`docs/research/repair-damage-run.sh`, five readers, both sides, full corpus).** Nothing the earlier pass fixed had slipped: `truncated-tail` still returns 2 of 4 with the blank one named, the three index shapes (`triage-report`, `plain-content-tailcut`, `objstm-wrong-offsets`) still come back at 4 sheets with their planted words, the `ledger-32-input` profile is still the input's own, and every produced file is `check clean`. Re-measuring also meant **adding the three damage classes the corpus had no case for** — a `/Length` that lies by a third (`wrong-length`), a `/Kids` entry deleted under a whole page object (`orphaned-page-object`), sheet 2's content stream blanked in place (`dangling-contents`), and sheet 2's picture plus its link annotation blanked, on a new `base-rich` whose sheet 2 inks 7.3 % with the picture and 5.3 % without it (`dangling-image-and-annots`) — and two of those were wrong without a word: (1) `orphaned-page-object` came back **3 sheets under all four readers where MuPDF and poppler open the input at 4**, under *“3 of 4 page(s) readable”* and `1 page(s) could not be recovered (4 were listed in the page tree)` — about an object, its `/Contents` and its `/Resources` that the cut never touched. `repairPDF` now collects the page objects no `/Kids` array names and writes them back into the tree before rebuilding the index (`orphanPageObjects`, `rehookOrphans`; inserted before the first sibling with a greater object number, `/Count` moved with it), reports `path: 'rehooked'` and **4 of 4 with the fourth named**, so the loss claim is gone and the guess (position, not the sheet) is what the warning names. `wrong-length` needed nothing — a lying `/Length` already returns 4 sheets at their own 5.3 % ink under every reader with the output `check clean`; it is pinned as a control. (2) `dangling-image-and-annots` shipped **both references into the copy**: `refs {'annots': 1, 'images': 1}` in and the same out, the picture gone (7.3 % → 5.3 % on that sheet) while the sentence said *“4 page(s) readable”*, and eight MuPDF errors (`cannot find object in xref`, `no XObject subtype specified`) that no user sentence carried — `qpdf --check` called the file **clean**, which is why the rig now counts references as well as checking syntax (`references()` in `repair-damage-readers.py`, `danglingReferences()` in the test). `dropDanglingContent` became `dropDanglingReferences`: `/Annots` entries and `/Resources /XObject` entries that do not resolve are dropped beside `/Contents`, counted, and named — *“2 reference(s) on the recovered sheet(s) named objects the cut took (1 annotation(s), 1 image(s)) and were removed, so the file points at nothing it does not hold — those annotations and pictures are not in the copy”*. After: refs `0/0`, `check clean`, four readers at 4 sheets, and the one remaining MuPDF line is `cannot find XObject resource 'Image-…'` — the `/Contents` still says `Do`, which is left open and named here. **The app's own renderer, both sides** (`repair-damage-pdfjs.mjs`): `orphaned-page-object` reads `3p [PW PW PW]` on the input and `4p [PW PW PW PW]` on the output; `dangling-contents` reads `4p [PW .- PW PW]` on both, the second sheet named as paintless by the reader as well as by the sentence. The rig also needed one guard to get there: pdf.js's node worker throws `Bad (uncompressed) XRef entry` **out of a read that has already settled** on a sheet whose `/Contents` was taken, which killed the process mid-corpus and left every later case unmeasured — both `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection` are printed beside the case now, and the input read is guarded like the output read already was. **Controls that must not satisfy the pass:** the intact `base-rich` keeps its 1 annotation and 1 image and prints no removal, and a page whose `/Contents` was itself taken (`dangling-contents`) is still 1 blank sheet, named, with its reference dropped. **Tests:** `tests/pdf/repair-damage.test.ts` grew the four cases plus `danglingReferences`; **reverting `src/lib/pdf-engine/commands.ts` alone reddens 4** (the orphan corpus case, the orphan rule, the dangling image/annots rule, the dangling-contents rule) and the other 18 stay green; 22 green with it. Full suite 3 187 pass / 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0, no new dependency. Screen half on the fixed dev build: the orphan run reads `PAGES 3 → 4` with *“4 page(s) recovered, 1 of them re-hooked…”*, four inked thumbnails, 0 console errors; the dangling run (`DARK=1`, tokens only) reads `4 → 4` with both warnings and 0 console errors; both downloads re-judged out of the browser, `check clean`, refs 0, all four planted words. | `src/lib/pdf-engine/commands.ts` (`repairPDF` + the index rebuild, the harvest, the content count, `orphanPageObjects`/`rehookOrphans`/`dropDanglingReferences`) and the `repair` entry of `src/lib/pdf/tools/optimize.ts`; `tests/pdf/{repair-damage,repair-damage-corpus,edge-optimize,optimize-bytes}.test.ts`, the corpus now carrying `wrong-length`/`orphaned-page-object`/`dangling-contents`/`dangling-image-and-annots` on a fourth `base-rich`; measured by `docs/research/repair-damage-{corpus.mjs,measure.mjs,entry.ts,internals.ts,readers.py,pdfjs.mjs,run.sh}` with its logs (the pre-fix set kept as `repair-damage-*-resettle-before.log`, the earlier round's as `repair-damage-*-before.log`); `core/{guard,doc,crypto,measure}.ts` untouched |

| `txt-to-pdf` | fixed+deployed | **968 of 2 861 characters, stopping mid-line, under a page count that looked fine.** `docs/TOOL-TRIAGE.md` §4 measured it; this row reproduces it on a source built to be counted and fixes the cause. **The cause, with its number:** not a size cap. `core/layout.ts` `renderBlocks` tests a paragraph/quote block against the space left on the sheet **once** — `const height = lines.length * lineHeight; if (needs(height)) await turn();` — and then paints every wrapped line from wherever the block began, so a block taller than one page continues below `y = 0`, where the content stream holds the words and no reader shows them. That is the whole of it: at the line where the sheet ends the file is amputated, and the only visible evidence is a page count that is still a plausible number for a document it did not print. **Marker rig** (`docs/research/text-tail-rig.test.ts` + `text-tail.vitest.config.ts`, judged by `text-tail-measure.py` with MuPDF, tables committed as `text-tail-{before,after}.txt`): 120 numbered rows, 8 800 bytes, `MKSTART`/`MK25`/`MK50`/`MK75`/`MKEND` at 0/25/50/75/100 %, written by the shipped `runPdfTool` and measured out of the download. (The first instrument, `7d4af0c`, gave the same answer one clause lower — 507 stream runs, 51 of them in a box, 739 characters, last visible row 17 — because its rows were a slightly longer sentence; the fix commit `c2226b5` quotes those, and the figures below are this rig's.) Before → after, same fixtures: **txt one tall paragraph 724 chars on 2 sheets with 51 of 482 runs inside a page box → 7 131 chars on 12 sheets, 492 of 492**; CRLF identical to LF in both builds (724 → 7 131); markdown 724 → 7 131; html `<p>` 818 → 7 116; xml one text node 715 → 7 125; word one `<w:p>` 818 → 7 116; **a 298 921-byte source 724 chars on 2 sheets → 243 856 on 349**, so there was no page-count ceiling to find, only a missing turn. Unaffected before and after, which is what makes the cut attributable: one-paragraph-per-line (7 131 on 12), monospace (7 119 on 3), `csv` (7 144 on 7), `json` as a list (14 962 on 23), `xml` as items (7 840 on 13), `word` as paragraphs (7 116 on 12). **The acceptance bar is the last marker**, not a non-empty file: `tests/pdf/text-tail.test.ts` (18) asserts each marker sits inside a page box, that the page count grows with the text and equals the sentence's number and the file's sheets, and that nothing before the cut moved — the compacted on-sheet text is *equal* to the compacted source, in order, once. Also covered because this is where loops bite: no trailing newline, a partial final line, tabs, accents/CJK/RTL, a 4 000-line source, a 7 980-character unbreakable word, and a quote whose bar must not stretch across the break (every painted path point is inside its own page). **10 of the 18 are red without `c2226b5`, green with it**; `convert-to-bytes` and `edge-convert-to` (151, incl. its "spills onto a second sheet without dropping or duplicating a line" pagination test) are green in both states; `tables`, `convert`, `pages-out` and `tools-e2e` add 118 over the same seam and stay green; the five files that cover this change — `text-tail`, `convert-to-bytes`, `edge-convert-to`, `ux-definitions`, `parity-definitions` — are 249 green. **Second defect found by the same fixture, one line:** a single `\t` in a proportional run threw `WinAnsi cannot encode "\t"` and produced **no file at all** — 0 of 9 040 bytes, no page count to distrust because there was no run. `textToBlocks` now expands tabs to spaces, the expansion the code branch already applied at draw time. **The claim, checked:** the sentence stays *"Typeset 1 text source(s) into 12 page(s)."* and is now true in both halves — 8 691 characters a reader extracts from the deployed download = 8 676 non-whitespace characters of the source + 15 footer digits (one per sheet, `1`…`12`), nothing lost and none invented, and the last sheet ends on row 120. The one limit that survives is stated in the tool rather than discovered by the user: `TEXT_NOTES` now says a single word longer than the column is not broken, which is what the bytes do (painted as one run from `x = 56` — 8 010 characters in the stream for the test's word, and 99 characters of a word of that size are inside the page box when MuPDF reads one back). **Re-measured on the deployed site** (`BASE=https://www.browsersnip.com`, CI `42737518850` green, `docs/research/rail-tool-bytes.mjs` with `TOOL=txt-to-pdf`, `doc.txt` as above, `LABEL=Typeset`; log `docs/research/text-tail-deployed.txt`): the card reads *"Typeset 1 text source(s) into 12 page(s)."* with `PAGES 12` and 0 console errors, the download is 65 637 bytes — the same length as the dev server's, different SHA only because pdf-lib writes a timestamp — and MuPDF finds **every marker on it, MKEND included, on sheet 12**. The pre-fix deployed figure is the triage row's own: 968 of 2 861. **Left open by that pass, closed since by the `csv-to-pdf` row below (`c5a2394`, `f509734`)**: (1) `renderList`, (2) `renderTable`, (3) Markdown's tab and (4) the heading branch — all four were the same missing per-line turn, and all four now continue across the sheet; the `OPEN` block in `tests/pdf/text-tail.test.ts` is retired to the opposite assertion, with the pre-fix numbers quoted beside it (`md-item` 52 of 426 runs, `json-cell` 16 characters of 7 365, `md-heading` 25 of 723, a new fourth instance `md-image`'s caption 56 of 424). `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, no UI file touched so dark mode is unchanged, 100 % client-side: the source is read in the tab and never uploaded. | `src/lib/pdf/core/layout.ts` (`renderBlocks`'s paragraph/quote branch and `textToBlocks`; `renderList`/`renderTable` left as the OPEN rows above), `src/lib/pdf/tools/convert-to.ts` (`TEXT_NOTES` only), `tests/pdf/text-tail.test.ts`, `docs/research/text-tail-*` |

Unaffected before and after, which is what makes the cut attributable: `csv-rows` 870/870, `csv-wide` 2 977/2 977, `md-items` 611/611, `html-items` 600/600, `xml-tree` 603/603, `json-item` 2 047 (was 1 673 in a box). <br>**One correction to the framing this pass was handed:** `csv-to-pdf` did *not* emit a nearly empty document for any table. No clipping was chosen because the cell is the user's data and no note in the tool said it would be cut. <br>**The sweep, item 4:** the heading branch (25 of 723) and the image-caption loop (56 of 424) were the same shape and are fixed here; the `code`, `rule`, `paragraph`/`quote` branches already turned per line and were re-measured unchanged. **Found by the sweep and left, with its number:** `wrapInlines` adds the width of every candidate *prefix* to its running total instead of the width of the word just appended, so a line breaks at about **22 characters of a 469-point column** (the face's own metrics allow 106) — nothing is lost, but every text route prints a page that is three quarters blank and a page count several times what the text needs. Pinned as `OPEN: the wrapper spends a sixth of the column it is given` in `tests/pdf/table-list-tail.test.ts`, because fixing it re-figures every count in this table and belongs to the fonts seam, not this row. The same CSV through the pre-fix build gave 25 sheets and **143 characters**, 0 of 12 row endings, no tail. <br>**Tests:** `tests/pdf/table-list-tail.test.ts` (30: markers judged inside the media box per renderer and per converter, header once per sheet, one baseline per wrapped line, a bullet per item, nothing printed twice, the page count equal to the sheets and to the sentence, the parser→renderer mapping asserted rather than assumed, the tab in prose/list/table, plus the `OPEN` wrapper row), the three retired `OPEN` rows in `tests/pdf/text-tail.test.ts`, and `steps a wrapping cell down its own baselines` in `tests/pdf/convert-to-bytes.test.ts`. **24 of the 54 assertions-that-matter are red without `f509734`, green with it**; `table-list-tail` + `text-tail` + `convert-to-bytes` + `edge-convert-to` are 199 green, and `tables`, `convert`, `tools-e2e`, `text-paint`, `ux-definitions`, `parity-definitions`, `registry`, `edge-convert-from`, `page-copy-fidelity` add 307 over the same seam. `TEXT_NOTES` now says in every affected tool what the bytes do. `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, no UI file touched so dark mode is unchanged, 100 % client-side: the CSV is read in the tab and never uploaded. | `src/lib/pdf/core/layout.ts` (`renderTable` rewritten band-wise, `renderList`, the heading and image-caption loops, `markdownToBlocks`' tab expansion), `src/lib/pdf/tools/convert-to.ts` (`TEXT_NOTES`), `tests/pdf/table-list-tail.test.ts`, `tests/pdf/text-tail.test.ts`, `tests/pdf/convert-to-bytes.test.ts`, `docs/research/table-list-tail-*` (rig, config, measure, before/after/deployed tables) |

| `image-to-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **Two images per sheet shipped a page that drew nothing, and two of the five controls moved nothing on the default sheet.** *Both found by looking at a produced page, not by a count.* <br>**(1) The pair-up wrote `Do` operators for objects it never wrote.** `twoUp` embeds pages out of the `pdfFromImages` document that was never serialised; `embedPage` copies a page's objects into the target and keeps the ref it was handed for the copied form, so on an unwritten source the copier takes that number for its own objects and the form's ref is never assigned. Measured on the shipped build (`docs/research/images-to-pdf-round.mjs`, `photo.png` + `wide.png`, A4, toggle on, dev `:5271`): a **1 878-byte** file whose `/Index [ 0 5 6 3 10 4 ]` omits objects 5 and 9 while the page's two `/Subtype /Form` resources name them — 6 × `format error: cannot find object in xref`, `page.get_images()` **`[]`**, **0 ink px of 223 114** rendered, and `placedImages` answering `Unknown [0,0]` for both sides, which is why the existing two-per-sheet test stayed green (it asserts `page.length === 2` and the boxes, never the resolution). After (`openPDF(await savePDF(source))` before the embed loop): both Forms resolve with their own boxes (`[3.0, 0.0, 592.3, 420.9]` at aspect 1.400 and `[0.0, 538.4, 595.3, 724.4]` at 3.200, pixel ratios 1.400/3.200, inside the 595.3 × 841.9 sheet), **360 263 ink px**, the card's own page preview drawing both pictures, `PAGES 1` = the file's 1 sheet and *“2 image(s) placed two per sheet on 1 page(s).”* (`docs/research/shots/image-to-pdf-2-sheet.png`, `image-to-pdf-4-produced-page.png`). <br>**(2) Fit and Orientation were live-looking controls over nothing at the default “Hug each image”.** `placeImage` sizes a hugged sheet from the pixels alone (`usable = natural`, scale 1), so `fit` and `orientation` changed no geometry there: `Fit=Stretch` at the default page size returned a **byte-identical 8 809-byte file and the same 640 × 200 page**, and `Orientation=Portrait` on the same 640 × 200 picture still produced a landscape page. Both were also inert under the toggle, whose sheet is A4 portrait by construction (`twoUp` passes `false` to `pageSize` and never applies `fit`). They are now `when:`-hidden exactly where they cannot act (`v.pageSize !== 'fit' && v.onePerSheet !== true`) and still live where shown: the fixed-sheet control yields `841.89 × 595.28` under Stretch + Landscape, so the fix hid a dead pair and not a working one; the page-size help says so in words. <br>**Unchanged before and after, which is what makes the two defects the only difference:** the hug page *is* the picture (`wide.png` → 640 × 200 page, embedded PNG 640 × 200 placed full-bleed, decoded pixels **byte-identical to the source**, 128 000 ink px, 0 text, 0-byte and note.txt refused in words — `"zero.png" is empty (0 bytes) — there is nothing to read.` / `"note.txt" is a text file, not a picture.`), a 3-file batch keeps intake order and per-file size (24 × 24, 640 × 200, 420 × 300), `margin=6` gives a 652 × 212 sheet with the picture at (6,6), `cover` overflows (`bbox [-531.5, 0, 1373.4, 595.3]`), and the screen says the document has no text layer. <br>**Tests:** `tests/pdf/convert-to-bytes.test.ts` — `danglingXObjects` resolves every XObject through the file's own xref (page → Form resources, one level), run against the unpaired route first as a control (0 dangling), then against the pair-up; and the visibility test asserts the predicate per state plus that the pair still moves the bytes where it is shown. **Reverting the reopen: 1 failed / 67 passed. Reverting the two `when`s: 1 failed / 67 passed.** The full suite is 3 443 passed / 1 skipped across 119 files, `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0. Dark mode photographed, tokens only (`docs/research/shots/image-to-pdf-3-dark.png`). **Left open, measured:** `/Info` title is empty (the card promises no title and no fixture carries one), and the hug page is 72 dpi by construction — one point per pixel, matching the note that the pixels are kept at their decoded resolution, with no `dpi` control to move it. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/convert-to.ts` (`twoUp`'s reopen, the two `when`s and the page-size help; `core/doc.ts` not edited — `openPDF`/`savePDF` reused), `tests/pdf/convert-to-bytes.test.ts` (`danglingXObjects`, both tests), `docs/research/images-to-pdf-round.mjs`, `docs/research/shots/image-to-pdf-{1-card,2-sheet,3-dark,4-produced-page}.png` |

1. **A test double that omits a contract cannot catch a bug in it.** The
   `renderPage` double in `edge-organize.test.ts` returned no `finished` property
   at all, so a helper that never awaited the paint looked fine and 1,875 tests
   stayed green over a CBZ of blank pages. A double has to model the *ordering* —
   nothing inked until `finished` resolves, inked after — not just the shape, and
   `finished: Promise.resolve()` is the same blind spot with better manners.
   Where it still stands: `convert-to-bytes.test.ts:99`, `edge-convert-to.test.ts:81`,
   `edge-edit.test.ts:104`, `edit-bytes.test.ts:117` and `ux-text.test.ts:97`
   return no `finished`; `pages-out.test.ts:77` returns one already resolved;
   `optimize-bytes.test.ts:136` returns none and paints **synchronously**, so its
   `rasterize-pdf` structural checks are safe today but could never see a paint
   race. Repaired with the `rasterize-pdf` row (`4ba14b6`): its own double
   (`tests/pdf/rasterize-ink.test.ts`) lands the ink in a later task and counts
   every read of a page canvas that happens before it.
   `edge-optimize.test.ts` was repaired with the `deskew-pdf` row (`8313e2d`): its
   double now hands out background-fill pixels until `finished` resolves and inked
   ones after, in a later task — a `finished: Promise.resolve()` lands the paint in
   a microtask the awaiting caller cannot lose to, which is how a double can be
   generous and still blind. Each belongs to its own tool's row, where the
   bytes can say whether it matters. The mirror-image mistake is just as live:
   the same `edge-organize.test.ts` double answered every `Do` with a
   `paintXObject` operator that real pdf.js emits only for a *form* XObject, so
   an image page scored as painted in jsdom and blank in the browser — a double
   can hide a bug by being **more generous** than the API, not only by omitting
   it. Repaired with that row (`a216d03`): the double now reads the page's
   `/Resources`, answers an image with `paintImageXObject`, and **throws** on a
   `Do` it cannot resolve rather than guessing an id, so generosity cannot
   silently come back. Model a double from a captured real response (an operator
   list, a text content item) when the rule reads those ids, and make the wrong
   vocabulary cost a test rather than pass one.
2. **Blank output is silent.** No console error, no exception, a plausible file
   size, and a report line true about everything except the pixels — six
   identical 23 690-byte JPEGs passed an “is this a PNG” check. So an ink
   assertion belongs in every rasterising tool's test: compare the archived
   bytes (`organize-campaign-bytes.py`, `cbz-bytes.mjs` measure non-white
   coverage out of the real download), never the byte count.
3. **A helper is a defect only where a tool reaches it.** `rasterizePages` had
   one caller and it was a shipped tool, which is what made this user-facing.
   Re-audited for `deskew-pdf` (`grep -rn "\b<name>\b" src/`, per helper):
   `isBlankPage`, `rasterizeWithOptions` and `scannerEffect` still have **no
   caller anywhere in `src/`** — dead with a known flaw, left alone — while
   `detectSkew`'s canvas read is awaited now (`8313e2d`), `applyColorFilter` is
   reached by `pdf-to-greyscale`'s **raster method — a choice the user makes, not a
   fallback reached after the operator path**, corrected by the caller audit below,
   and `deskewAngleFor` — dead, correct, and tested —
   is the bridge the `deskew-pdf` call site should have used from the start.
   A broken helper nobody reaches is a maintenance note, not a defect; a
   *correct* helper nobody reaches is the same note with a bug waiting in the
   caller that ignored it.
4. **Prove two tools against each other.** The `ocr-pdf` cache collision — one
   page answered with another page's words, a fabricated result rather than a
   weak one — was found only because the row was asked for an end-to-end proof:
   plant text, rasterise it with `rasterize-pdf`, make `ocr-pdf` return it. Every
   per-tool test in this repo recognised **one** page, so a bug whose signature
   is "the second page reads like the first" is invisible to them *by
   construction*: each test's fixture never contains the pair the defect needs.
   A cross-tool chain has a second virtue — it fails loudly if **either** link
   regresses, which is why `ocr-check.py` scores the planted strings rather than
   checking the output is non-empty. Where a tool's input is another tool's
   output, test the pair.

5. **An option that is accepted, plumbed and never observable is not a dead
   parameter, it is a silent one.** `RenderOptions.filter` had a type, a docstring, a
   call site in four places and an assignment to `ctx.filter` — and had never done what
   it says, because pdf.js's `beginDrawing` calls `resetCtxToDefault`, which writes
   `filter = 'none'` over the caller's value before its first operator
   (pdfjs-dist 4.10.38, `build/pdf.mjs:7610-7617`). Grepping for *readers* of an option
   finds this class never; the only test is a measurement that changes when the option
   changes. `docs/research/colour-measure.sh` is that measurement for `filter`, per
   region, for all four consumers. The same question, asked of the rest of
   `RenderOptions` and of the pdf.js render call it forwards to — **none of these has a
   measurement proving it does anything**, listed rather than fixed, because a claim
   about them belongs to a row with bytes behind it:
   · `background` — assigned to `ctx.fillStyle` and filled, then overwritten by
     pdf.js's own `background || '#ffffff'` fill in `beginDrawing` (`:7686-7687`), which
     this module never passes a value to. Every caller in `src/` asks for `'#ffffff'` or
     `undefined`, so nothing can show the difference today; a coloured paper request
     would be silently dropped.
   · `rotation` — read, and forwarded to `page.getViewport({rotation})`, but **no caller
     in `src/` passes it** (`grep -rn "renderPage(" -A 4 src/ | grep rotation:` → the one
     hit is this module's own default), so the "render at an angle other than /Rotate"
     path has never been driven.
   · `RenderOptions.scale` — declared on the interface and never read: `renderPage` takes
     scale as its second argument, and every caller passes it there. The field is a trap
     for the next caller that sets it.
   · the pdf.js task options this module does *not* forward — `intent`,
     `annotationMode`, `pageColors`, `transform`, `printAnnotationStorage`. Fine as
     choices, but `annotationMode` and `pageColors` are the two that would change what a
     page looks like, and nothing here says which one the app wants by decision rather
     than by omission.
   The mirror of standing rule 3: a *correct* thing nobody reaches waits for a caller; a
   *broken* thing everybody believes in has already shipped four tools.

## Caller audit of the unawaited `renderPage` reads — done for `rasterize-pdf`

real browser run of *that* tool on the deployed site wherever its output can be
measured). **Order the rest of the campaign by this table, not by file order.**

The type is now clipped to the mark's own box on screen: **11 798 px outside → 0**, ink 16 382 → 4564, the file unchanged (8013 px, `Alpha\nbeta`) — it was already right. `layoutMarkText` reports the height the words need (it returned the 8 pt block height), the export names the clip in points and the pane warns before the export; 4 of 6 tests in `tests/pdf/annotation-text-fit.test.tsx` go red without it. Dark mode: paper stays white, so a `#FFEB3B` stroke is 2040 px of exactly its colour on screen and 3395 px in the file (visible, low contrast on paper, clear on the black band) and a 28 pt box is legible at 7399/11 277 px. | `edit-pdf` + `edit-pdf-text` + `header-footer` + `page-numbers` + `watermark` + `add-stamps` (WYSIWYG text styling) | fixed+deployed | The seven text properties now live in one model (`core/text-style.ts`: family, weight, slant, size, colour, alignment, line height) with one family registry that states how each face reaches the file and which postures it holds as **real faces**. Bold and italic are never synthesised: `carriesPosture` answers for the face that will *actually* write the run, so italic is refused (button disabled, reason in its title) for the bundled Noto and every one-weight script face, and for a Latin-only family asked to slant a run past WinAnsi. **Refused outright, with the reason in the UI:** all type styling on the annotation editor's **Note** — its body is a plain `/Contents` string and readers paint the popup, so the `Text size` slider there was formally populated and practically dead, and it is deleted (colour and opacity stay because `/C`/`/CA` carry them); alignment on the four rail marks (the anchor *is* the alignment); line height on any mark that draws one run; `justify` anywhere the block editor is not. One layout path: `editor/textLayout.ts` runs `prepareTextObject` → `layoutBlock` with the face the file carries, and **both** the overlay (SVG `<text>` on the returned baselines) and `addAnnotation` (the same `LaidOutLine`s in the appearance stream) consume it — no CSS wrap anywhere. CJK survived: a Japanese text box used to export `?????` through `toWinAnsi`; the face is now embedded and the run is not folded. Re-measured on `https://www.browsersnip.com` after CI `42581590836`/`42580577194` (same rig, same checker):
`every produced file says what its surface claimed`, with the dev build's numbers exactly. Measured per surface in `research/wysiwyg-text-style-findings.md` — after: text box `NotoSans-Bold` 22 pt fill `(0.545 0.361 0.965)` on baselines **44.0 pt** apart with 4 600 ink px and the *screen* claiming the same 22 pt/44 pt/weight 700; stamp `Times-Bold` 30 pt, 7 496 ink px; note `/C` and no `/DA`/`/DR`; page numbers in **`Times-Italic`** 22 pt; watermark `Courier-Bold` 48 pt. Before (`81385c1`, same rig, same checker): header/footer drew **helvetica** and the watermark drew **helveticabold** — there was no typeface control to move them. The four *editor* surfaces have no before column: the rig addresses the new control set by its accessible names, which did not exist at `81385c1`. Machine-checked from here on by `tests/pdf/style-controls-invariant.test.ts`: a declared field must be named by code that reads it, every style field of a text-drawing tool must change the produced bytes (17 byte-diff cases), and the control set must render only what its writer honours (it is mounted and its buttons counted). Census: **0** registry fields named nowhere in the tool layer; **33 of 511** not named in the module that declares them — 26 generated aliases (`jpg-to-pdf`, `pdf-to-png`, …) and 7 read by a helper in another module, ratcheted so a rise needs a review. Proposal for its own row, not a TODO: give the sticky note a *baked* styled appearance — it would make note type real and changes what a note is on the page for every existing document. | `editor/**`, `text-editor/**`, `core/text-style.ts`, `core/annotations.ts`, `tools/edit.ts` + `tools/textStyleFields.ts` |

| `add-stamps` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **On a turned sheet the stamp was drawn turned, and its opacity reached only the dictionary.** Measured first, on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` (four sheets, sheet 4 a `595×420 /Rotate 90`), dev `:5271`, `research/stamps-round.mjs` (arms the tool, picks the stamp, clicks the page, writes the inspector's own opacity/size controls and reads them back out of the DOM before the click) judged by `research/stamps-check.py` (ink bbox of the label's own colour off the page bitmap, inside/edge distances, w/h, `/AP` text, `/S`, `/CA`, annot/link/image counts against the source). **What was already right, both halves:** the corner the user clicks, on upright and turned sheets (before: 6 pt from the right, 26 pt from the top on the upright sheets; the anchor's nine segments each land in their own ninth of the displayed sheet, turned sheet included, and a box wider than the paper shrinks inside it), the card counts as the file (`PAGES 4`/clicks 4 → four `/Stamp` annots, `Placed 4 stamps on 4 pages`), page selection, `/Info`, the pre-existing annot, link and 2-entry outline survive (`annots.pdf`: 1 `Text` annot + 1 link in, 2 annots + 1 link out, title kept), and each stamp re-draws in MuPDF and poppler. **Found and fixed:** (a) a stamp on a `Rotate 90` sheet was laid out in the *unrotated* box, so a 140 pt width arrived as 140 pt of **height** and the label ran down the 44 pt edge, 9 pt of it crossing the border — before `ink bbox 43x140 w/h 0.31` flush to the top edge, after `139x44 w/h 3.16` 12 pt in; the click default is now set in displayed axes (`specs.rectFromDrag`) and the appearance is built in the displayed box with the page turn cancelled by its own `cm`, so the label is upright and the width is the width seen; the editor's preview moves with it (the box in the screen shot is 140×44 and the words in it are the file's). (b) the opacity slider wrote `/CA` alone: at 0.4 and at 1 the rendered pages were **byte-identical** under BOTH MuPDF and poppler (`md5 d12437b4…` twice) — both paint a Form `/AP` opaque and ignore the annotation alpha — so the alpha now rides the appearance as `/GS1 gs` (`/CA .4 /ca .4` in an `/ExtGState`) and the dictionary `/CA` stays 1 so no reader doubles it: after, the label pixel is `(221,154,154)` at 40 % against `(170,0,0)` at 100 % in both readers, monotone. (c) `/S` was the picker's own spacing (`/NOT APPROVED` folded to `/NOTAPPROVED`, `/ASIS`) which is not a Table 265 name, so no reader could recognise the standard stamp the card promises; the canonical token is written (`/NotApproved`, `/AsIs`) while the drawn words are the spaced label ("Not approved"), with a non-standard name (`EXPRESS`) left as given. The opaque `contents` now carries the label. | `flatten-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **Annotation flattening was a no-op that reported success, and the cause was two class assertions in the ten lines around the appearance lookup.** Re-measured before touching it (`0c1df4c`), because the ledger row is ten commits old and `core/flatten.ts` had not been edited since the feature landed: `drawAppearance` asked `context.lookup(appearanceRef, PDFDict)` for the object `/AP /N` names, an appearance is a **stream** (`PDFRawStream extends PDFStream extends PDFObject`), and the fork throws `UnexpectedObjectTypeError` on a class mismatch instead of answering undefined — so the first annotation carrying an appearance ended the whole pass, the tool's `catch` swallowed it, and the file left with every mark exactly where it was while the card printed a page count. The `Expected instance of me, but got instance of undefined` variant the deployed build printed is the second cause: a page with no `/Annots` key, which is what a blank sheet looks like in PDF, hit `lookup(undefined, PDFArray)`. The third: `doc.getForm()` on the last line *creates* an `AcroForm` when the document has none, so a comment bake added form furniture. **Fixed in `cc19e56`** — the appearance is resolved through the three shapes PDF writes it in, its operators are copied into the page by `inlineFormXObject` (the seam `flattenForm` already uses, so a baked stamp or signature stays searchable and redactable rather than becoming a picture), `/Rotate` `/CA` `/BM` are applied through the page's own graphics state because they belong to the annotation and not to its stream, and only then does the object leave `/Annots` — its `/Popup` with it, since an orphan popup is still a comment to every reader. **Both halves, same fixture either side of the fix, every figure read out of the file by `docs/research/flatten-readers.py`:** ink at each mark's own box measured with MuPDF twice — once as displayed, once with `annots=False` so only page content is painted — gives note `0.0 → 98.67`, highlight `0.0 → 96.64`, ink stroke `0.0 → 6.01`, square `0.0 → 99.99`, the 40 % square on the `/Rotate 90` sheet `0.0 → 100.0`, and the four sheets' page ink unchanged at `1.3 / 2.34 / 0 / 1.53 %`: the same picture with nothing left to click. Annotations `5 → 0`, widgets `3 → 0`, `AcroForm /Fields` `3 → 0`, `/NeedAppearances` gone; pypdf lists `Signature1`, `answer`, `tick` before and **no fields at all** after, and the flattened file opens in MuPDF, poppler and pypdf with its words and the answer's words in the page text. Before the fix the same boxes measured 0.0 content-only. Left live on purpose, counted and named, because inventing bytes is the other way to lie: a mark with no appearance to copy, one flagged `Invisible`/`Hidden`/`NoView` (a viewer never paints it), a `Link` the toggle keeps clickable, and every `Widget` — widgets belong to `flattenForm`, and baking one here left `/AcroForm /Fields` naming a field whose box had vanished. The card's copy went by the same measure: `liveMarks` now keeps `/Widget` and `/Popup` apart from comments (a form box and a note's bubble are not comments), the contradictory *"This PDF had nothing of the selected kind to flatten"* is compared against what the **input** held instead of against two zero counters, the library's exception sentence is out of the user's warning, and the dead `Resolution` slider is gone — nothing read `dpi`, the path rasterises nothing, and its help promised re-drawing the bytes never did. **Tests:** `tests/pdf/flatten-truth.test.ts` (15) asserts the after state per kind off the saved bytes, with the content streams inflated by `node:zlib` rather than by the module that wrote them; **nine reverts, each run: the `PDFDict` lookup restored turns 10 red, the missing-`/Annots` guard 5, the unpainted-flag guard 2, and the popup carry, the `/CA` state, the widget guard and the `getForm()` side effect 1 each.** Green: 556 across the fifteen files around this seam (`edge-security`, `security-bytes`, `tools-e2e`, `edge-optimize`, `edge-edit`, `edge-organize`, `edit-bytes`, `pages-out`, `greyscale-ink`, `rasterize-ink`, `registry`, `parity-definitions`, `ux-definitions`, `style-controls-invariant`); `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0; no UI file touched, so dark mode is unchanged. **Re-measured on the deployed site** after CI `42762230426` (`BASE=https://www.browsersnip.com`, `rail-tool-bytes.mjs` pressing the rail's own `Flatten` with the default toggles): the card reads *"5 annotation(s) drawn into the content of 3 page(s) and 3 form field(s) removed from the form; nothing of the selected kind is left live."* with no console error, and the download — read back outside this app — answers annotations `5 → 0`, widgets `3 → 0`, pypdf fields `['Signature1','answer','tick'] → []`, `is_encrypted False`, 4 pages, page ink unchanged at `1.3 / 2.34 / 0 / 1.53 %`, each mark's box measuring its displayed ink with `annots=False` too (`98.67 / 96.64 / 6.01 / 99.99 / 100.0`), and `Ada Lovelace` still extractable from sheet 1. pypdf's per-page array count is `[0, 1, 0, 0]`: the one surviving entry is the **`/Link` the toggle was asked to keep clickable** — MuPDF does not list links among `annots()`, which is the whole difference between the two readers, and the sentence's "of the selected kind" means comments and fields. **Round 055 re-measured the whole row rather than trusting it, and found two live defects.** Measured on `/tmp/triage/in/annot.pdf` (note + its `/Popup`, `FreeText`, `Highlight`, one `/Link`) and `/tmp/triage/in/form.pdf` (text field, filled dropdown, ticked box) through `docs/research/flatten-round.mjs` — upload, every toggle clicked by its printed sentence, opened out of the "2 MORE OPTIONS" fold and **read back out of the DOM before the click** (`Keep clickable links=off`) — and judged outside the app by MuPDF (`annots=False`, so only page content counts), pypdf `get_fields()`, pikepdf, Ghostscript and `docs/research/flatten-pdfjs-colour.mjs` (pdf.js, the reader this app previews with). **Defect 1 — the mark's `/CA` and `/BM` were set and popped before the mark was drawn.** `drawAppearance` pushed `q`+`gs` / `Q` around `inlineFormXObject`, but `page.pushOperators` writes into one stream the page keeps *ahead of* the appearance's stream, so both ops landed in an empty `q /Flat… gs Q` block and the paint ran with the default state — a 40 % wash came back solid and a Multiply highlight came back opaque, while `pageAlphas`-style checks still saw the `/ExtGState` sitting in the resources. Ghostscript on the produced file: red wash **`(255,0,0)` → `(255,153,153)`**, 50 % yellow over green **`(255,255,0)` → `(51,153,25)`**, yellow alone **`(255,255,0)` → `(255,255,127)`**. The state now travels as an option of the drawing call and is emitted inside the same `q` that wraps the placement (`content.ts inlineFormXObject` gained `state`; the widget path passes none, and both default-toggle outputs stayed **byte-identical**, md5 `bceff5c8…`/`dc847c47…`, so nothing else moved). **Defect 2 — a clickable link was counted as a live comment whenever links were meant to be baked.** `commentCount` subtracted `/Link` only when `keepLinks` was on, so the same entry was named twice: on `annot.pdf` with the link toggle off the card read *"… 1 comment(s) still live and 1 link(s) still clickable"* with **two** no-appearance warnings (the link **and** the note's `/Popup`, which has no `/AP` and was being swept as a mark once links stopped being skipped up front) — and `/Annots` held exactly one entry, the link. After: *"1 link(s) still clickable"*, one warning (*"1 link(s) carry no appearance of their own"*), no comment claim; the popup leaves with its baked parent as before. Links are now always excluded from the comment count and added to the asked-for total only when the toggle says so, and a `/Popup` is no longer a bake target at all — its parent's fate decides. **Already right, measured, pinned:** the three real marks are painted into the page and out of `/Annots` (content-only ink at the note's box **17.82 → 100.0**, at the widget boxes **0.0 → 9.03** and **0.0 → 1.96**, page content-only ink equal to the displayed ink after `24.44` and `24.66`); the baked page is the displayed page to one antialias level (**50 px of 584 220** differ, all 1-level, on the variants sheet — and the only ink outside a mark's own box is its 0.5 pt stroke bleed, 48 px); `/Annots` gone where it emptied, `AcroForm` and `/Fields` gone on the form, pypdf `['q1.name','q1.on','q1.pick'] → []`, no ref reachable from `/Root` or `/Info` dangling, pikepdf round-trip clean; the answer `Alpha` and the tick are page text; page count 4, every `/MediaBox`/`/CropBox`, the `/Rotate 90` sheet, `/Info /Title` and the outline kept; a mark with no `/AP`, a `Hidden` highlight and a `NoView` circle stay live and are named; a link with an appearance bakes when the toggle is off. **Red without the fixes: 3 failed / 15 passed** (`opens the wash’s transparency around the paint, not after it` — `f ran at 1, not 0.4`; `does not call a clickable link a live comment…`; `does not count a popup as a mark that failed to bake when links are off`), 18 green with them. Full suite 3 345 passed | 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0, no console error in any run. **Left open, measured:** (1) the result card's `OutputPreview` has no zoom control (no `aria-label="Zoom in"`, ctrl-wheel does not reach it), so a flattened sheet can only be inspected here by rendering the download — a shared pane, not this tool; (2) a `Text` note bakes its icon, so its comment text is still a comment nowhere — that is what the appearance draws, and no reader was asked to re-flow it; (3) with links off a link with no `/AP` is still left live and named rather than replaced by a guessed box.  | `core/flatten.ts` (the appearance resolve, the bake, the removal) + the `flatten-pdf` entry in `tools/security.ts`; `core/annotations.ts`, `core/doc.ts` and `core/content.ts` needed no change — `inlineFormXObject` was already there and correct, the caller just asked it the wrong question + round 055: `core/flatten.ts` (the state handed to the drawing call, the Popup guard, the `commentCount` doc), `core/content.ts` (`inlineFormXObject`'s optional `state`), `tools/security.ts` (`commentCount`, `askedFor`), `tests/pdf/flatten-truth.test.ts` (3 pins), `docs/research/flatten-round.mjs`, `docs/research/flatten-pdfjs-colour.mjs` |

Write 2-4.* **Four defects, all fixed:** (1) the one-file-per-page branch printed `PAGES 4 in · out not counted` while it held every document it saved — `filesOut` now carries the sheet count the run already chose (`4 → 4`, `4 → 2`, `12 → 12`, cross-checked against the sum of the re-opened files' page counts); (2) those file names had no width, so a 12-page run wrote `page-1 … page-10 … page-12` and the archive behind the card's own *Download as zip* sorted sheet 10 between 1 and 2 — the numbers are padded to the document's own width now (`page-01 … page-12`; the zip's name-sorted order equals its central order; nine pages or fewer are unchanged, which is the control, and each file still holds the sheet it names); (3) the whole-selection note said *the new file holds 4 pages* over four separate files and now says *one file per page, 4 pages of them*; (4) the selection collapsed duplicates, so `1-2,2-3` wrote 3 sheets where the user typed 4 — `selection` takes `keepDuplicates` for the tools whose output is a list of sheets (`split`'s overlapping files already wrote 4), the repeat is copied and named (`page-2`, `page-2-2`) and counted in a note (*Page 2 appears more than once in the selection…*). **And the pane contradicted the refusal:** the page picker resolved the cleared field through `tryResolvePages`, which reads blank as every page, so pressing the strip's own `none` chip left *ALL 4 PAGES PICKED*, four ringed thumbnails and *4 of 4 selected* on screen over a disabled Extract button asking for a selection — it marks nothing now and says *pick pages below* (`FieldControls.tsx`, `PageThumbs.tsx`, a shared seam named for that reason). **Red without the fix: 5 failed, 0 with it** (`tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts` ×4 — repeated selection, `expected [ 'sheet 1', 'sheet 2', 'sheet 3' ] to deeply equal [ 'sheet 1', 'sheet 2', 'sheet 2', 'sheet 3' ]`; the per-page count, `expected null to be 2`; the width, `expected 'dozen_page-1.pdf…' to deeply equal 'dozen_page-01.pdf…'`; the note shape, `expected 'You selected every page, so the new f…' to match /one file per page/i` — and `tests/pdf/page-picker-empty.test.ts` ×1). Controls green in both states: `1-3` gets no repeat note, `all` still says *new file*, a 4-page run keeps `page-1`, `all` still rings all four thumbnails and `2` rings one. 3085 tests green, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0, dark mode one screenshot, tokens only. **Left open, measured:** a reversed range is a refusal rather than the descending read `core/pages.resolvePages` can already do (the same open item as `split`'s row); the picker paints a *set* — page numbers in numeric order — so the typed order exists in the file and nowhere on screen; a repeated page in the per-page mode is named `page-2-2`, which is unique but reads like a duplicate copy rather than a typed repeat. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/organize.ts` (the extract entry, `selection`'s `keepDuplicates`, `noteRepeatedSheets`), `src/components/tools/pdf/shared/FieldControls.tsx` and `PageThumbs.tsx` (shared seams, pinned), `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`, `tests/pdf/page-picker-empty.test.ts`, `docs/research/extract-pages-round.mjs` |

Two conclusions that change how the campaign runs. **(a) The await belongs at the
call site, not in a helper**: `core/render.ts` was already honest about `finished`
and `core/raster.ts` only encodes what it is handed, so no shared module needed
editing for `rasterize-pdf` — the same answer `deskew-pdf` reached. **(b) Five
shipped tools were measured or traced blank by this audit** (six including
`rasterize-pdf`), which is the same defect with five more owners: `pdf-to-image`
is closed (`38ef532`), the OCR pair is closed (`53fcb85`, which also fixes
`digitize-document`'s OCR half), and the three `rerasterise` tools are closed
(`7cf6ce2`) — the last **measuring** that fixing the await was not enough, which is
what opened rule 5, and `pdf-to-greyscale` is closed (`128a247`) — the seventh copy, and
the one whose control no probe had ever pressed. `compare-pdfs` is closed too (`7d59ec9`, `4fe3535`) — the **eighth** copy, and
the first where the await was only one of four defects: the verdict path was a *different* class (a render that never reached its canvases, and a panel that could
not re-render), the negative control found a torn bitmap from two `page.render()` tasks on one canvas, and the unreachable rail report was counting pages by
regexing its own prose. Take the editor's rotated redaction next (`AnnotationEditor.tsx:2766`). `pdf-to-greyscale` was
taken off that queue by its own row: its raster method was
**driven — measured at 0.00 % ink against its own source sheet at 19.50 % on the deployed site, and
re-measured at 19.53 % after the fix** — because `applyColorFilter` is a second rasteriser that never
awaited `finished`, so repairing the render option could not reach it and one tool's agent had to close
its own row. **(c) The await was not the whole defect here**: an option the caller
passes beside it can be equally unevidenced — see rule 5. **(d) `rail-tool-bytes.mjs`
clicking with defaults is not “driven”**: the greyscale number existed for a week as
“reached, not yet driven” and took ten minutes with a probe that can press a segmented
control.

## Sequenced from the `edit-pdf-text` row — not fixed there

1. **13 `renderPage` call sites outside `core/render.ts`, 12 of them reading the
   canvas before `finished`** — audited site by site in the table above for
   `rasterize-pdf`, which closed `tools/optimize.ts` and re-drew this item, and by
   `pdf-to-image`, which closed `tools/convert-from.ts`: the
   queue is now *reached, not yet driven* (`AnnotationEditor.tsx:2766` for a
   rotated redaction — the one site left with a canvas read the campaign has not
   measured) → *closed* (`core/ocr.ts`, both its callers, `53fcb85`;
   `invert-colors` / `adjust-colors` / `scanner-effect`, `7cf6ce2`;
   `applyColorFilter`, `128a247`; `pdf-engine/commands.ts` `comparePDFs`, `7d59ec9`;
   `tools/security.ts:817`, `4fe3535` — measured unreachable from the route and
   closed as an unreachable path, not driven from a page) → *dead with a flaw* (`isBlankPage`,
   `rasterizeWithOptions`, `scannerEffect`) → *healthy* (`thumbnails()`, which
   awaits `page.render().promise` itself, and the two form panes, which mount the
   live canvas instead of copying it). They race the same paint the text editor
   lost; several looked healthy only because image encoding yields to the event
   loop long enough for pdf.js to catch up.
2. **`shared/PreviewPane.tsx` is imported by nothing in `src/`**
   (`grep -rln PreviewPane src/` → only itself; its only consumers are
   `tests/pdf/ux-rail.test.ts` and `tests/app/shared-ui.test.tsx`). Dead code with
   a live-looking name: agents keep reasoning about the UI as though it were
   mounted. Cleanup candidate for a layout pass — **do not delete it here**, since
   whether something is still expected to mount it is a layout question.
3. **A rewritten block lands ~6 pt above the run it replaced** — original span
   bbox top `81.6`, the file's rewritten top `75.6` at the same 20 pt size. Screen
   and file agree (both come from `model.layoutBlock`), so it is placement
   accuracy, not invisibility. P2, text-editor's own seam.
4. **The substituted face painted nothing past its second glyph** — left open by
   the `edit-pdf-text` row, measured per face and per reader in
   `docs/research/font-subset-findings.md`, and *closed* here: `alignTrueTypeGlyphs`
   long-word-aligns a face before fontkit's subsetter halves its `loca`, the face's
   flavour is read from `font['CFF ']` instead of the `font.cff` that build never
   sets, CFF programs go to `/FontFile3 /CIDFontType0C` under a `/CIDFontType0` with
   the one header byte fontkit mislabels repaired, and `embedUnicodeFace` now backs
   every raw-bytes caller (`aae5110`, `0e748f5`, guard in `tests/pdf/fonts.test.ts`
   and `docs/research/font-subset-revert.py`).

The `595×420 /Rotate 90` sheet goes into its cell upright at aspect **1.651 vs the source's 1.656**, panel boxes `841.9×595.3` with `/Rotate 0`, out-of-cell ink 0. Annots (5 links/annotations on `annot.pdf`) are dropped and said. **The defects:** (1) `box()` read the margin/gutter pair as (`inset`, `rightInset`) so the right-hand page carried the **fold allowance on its trim edge and the trim margin against the fold** — at gutter 40 / margin 10 the right cell's content sat 40 pt from the outer edge and 10 pt from the fold, the mirror of the left page's 10/40, 30 pt off its cell centre; lengths to the fold and to the trim now both land at 10/40 with the centre within 2 pt; (2) `/Info` and the XMP packet sit above the page tree and this tool builds a new one, so the title, author, subject, keywords, creator, packet and the producer left in silence — `carryDocInfo` + `setProducer('BrowserSnip')` as in `n-up`, with `PAGE_COPY_INFO_NOTE` on the card (`shots/booklet-pane-notes.png`); (2) the `landscape` help claims a portrait sheet “folds the wrong way” — it folds along the long edge instead and the run now says so, but the imposition itself is left/right either way; (3) 3 of the 12 fixture sheets carry only a title block, so the aspect claim is made on `colour.pdf`'s rotated sheet, not on them. | booklet's own `run`, `box()` and `notes` in `tools/organize.ts`; the `595×420 /Rotate 90` sheet is cut **in its displayed frame** — its two halves are 297.5×420 and match the display crop at **identity 1.000 / 0.995** (control 0.769), with the `SHEET 06 ROT` line on the display-right piece, and in the grid run its text lands in the display's top-right cell (piece 22) at identity 1.000; each piece of a rotated sheet is written `/Rotate 0` with the content pre-turned, so the displayed pixels are the source's to the pixel. Card counts equal the file on every case (10 → 20, 10 → 30, 10 → 40, 1 → 2), the output names are `ten_divided.pdf` / `blank_divided.pdf`, and pane and card were read at both ends and in dark mode (tokens only, count and note agreeing with the file). **Control that the field is the divisor it says:** `Number of parts` is *pieces per sheet*, not a document divisor — the document divisor lives on `split`'s `Pages per file` (*max pages per file*), re-measured here on the same 10-sheet file so the two cannot be confused: chunks 3 → 4 files `…-01-03, …-04-06, …-07-09, page-10` (the remainder short, not widened), 1 → 10 files, 10 → 1 file, 20 → 1 file, the last two with *The chunk size (10|20) is at least the document's length, so all 10 pages land in one file.* **Refusals, all true:** parts `0` → *Number of parts must be between 2 and 9: 0 became 2*, `−4` and `1` the same sentence, `20` → *20 became 9* with 90 pieces; `abc` empties the box, disables the verb and reads *Number of parts must be a number.* plus *Still needed: …*; a 1-page file divides to 2 pieces; grid 1×1 prints *One column and one row copies each page instead of dividing it*. **Defect 1 — the blank pieces were counted as inked, so both blank sentences were dead.** `stamp()` returns “this region had an area”, and since the blank-page fix it also hands a sheet with no `/Contents` the empty one the embedder needs, so a 1-page 0-ink file came back **“Divided 1 page into 2 pages.” with no warning at all** and the card's *Divided these pages produced nothing but blank sheets.* refusal was unreachable; a blank sheet inside a 10-sheet run was equally silent. The sheet's content state is now read **once, before the first copy** (`pageContentsState(page) === 'absent'`) and its pieces are counted: the mixed run says *A page had no drawable content, so 2 of the 20 pieces are blank sheets in the output.* (4 of 40 in the grid run, on the card under WHAT YOU SHOULD KNOW), and a selection of nothing else is refused with *Dividing these pages produced nothing but blank sheets.* + hint *try "Extract Pages" instead.* **Defect 2 — `/Info` and the XMP packet were thrown away in silence.** `meta.pdf` came back title `""` against *Triage metadata title* and producer `pdf-lib` on all 8 pieces. `carryDocInfo` + `setProducer('BrowserSnip')` now run as in `posterize`/`n-up`/`merge` with `PAGE_COPY_INFO_NOTE` on the card: after, the title is carried, the packet is present and the producer is ours, and a bare file invents nothing (control). **Red without the fix: 4 failed / 5 passed** with `organize.ts` at `HEAD` (2 blank-piece in `tests/pdf/blank-page-chain.test.ts`, `expected 'A page had no drawable content…'`; 2 in `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`, `expected '' to be 'Divided round'` and the no-invention control on its producer line), 0 failed with it, 9 green. Full suite 3 145 passed | 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no console error in any run. **Left open, measured:** (1) the pieces are fractions of the source sheet, not paper — a cut of an A5 is 105×595 pt and the card gives no sheet size; (2) annotations, links, form fields and the outline are still dropped, which the family's flatten note and `noteRebuildLoss`'s counted sentence say (measured on `annot.pdf`: *5 links or annotations could not come along*, 5 `/Annots` entries and 1 link at the source → 0 in the file); (3) `/Rotate` is not carried on a piece but baked into the content, so a 90° source yields `/Rotate 0` pieces — the displayed pixels are the source's and this is the family norm, recorded rather than changed; (4) a `parts` of 1 is only reachable through a clamp warning, and the note that explains the copy case (*set at least two parts*) can only be reached from the grid fields; (5) a sheet narrower than the part count gets sub-point tiles (`595/3` → 198.3333 pt boxes), which no reader has complained about here. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/organize.ts` (the `divide-pages` run: the blank-sheet read, the `carryDocInfo`/producer call, `PAGE_COPY_INFO_NOTE`), `tests/pdf/{blank-page-chain,edge-organize}.test.ts`, `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (shared probe: the three field labels, and a screenshot on a refusal instead of a silent exit), `docs/research/divide-{round.py,fixture.py}`, `docs/research/shots/divide-*.png` |
| `fix-page-size` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The box, the orientation, the rotated sheet, the crop box, the aspect and the blank sheet were already exact; "Stretch" did not stretch, the crop percentage was an area ratio that fired on pages it never cropped, a shrunk page was silent, and the document's identity was thrown away.** Measured on the live `<127.0.0.1:5271>` build with `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (16 runs; every field written as a user event and **read back out of the DOM before the click** — `Target size` a4/largest, `Orientation` keep/portrait/landscape, `Scaling` contain/cover/stretch, `Pages 1`) on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf`, a built crop/large fixture (sheet 1 carries `/CropBox [100 100 320 495]`, 4 annots and an outline, sheet 2 is 1190×1684) and a 200×500 page with a 100×100 square, judged by `docs/research/fix-page-size-bytes.py` at 72 dpi with annotations off: box, `/CropBox`, `/Rotate`, ink bbox and that bbox's aspect against the same source page's own, plus `/Info` and `/Annots` — the blank sheet reads `ink None` in both states, which is the instrument's control.<br>**Already right, and pinned here:** every output sheet is the chosen paper and **the displayed box is that paper** — the `595×420 /Rotate 90` sheet comes back as a 842×595 sheet at `/Rotate 0` with its content upright (aspect 1.648 vs the source's 1.653), so the turn is baked and the reader sees the paper asked for; the orientation control reaches the bytes (landscape → all four sheets 841.9×595.3); a source `/CropBox` is honoured and baked (220×395 visible → ink aspect 1.199 vs 1.196, cropped content gone, no `/CropBox` left); "Fit inside" keeps the aspect (0.782 / 0.724 against the source's 0.780 / 0.721) and centres; the empty sheet is 0 ink and nothing is invented; the text sheet keeps all 1 779 characters as vector text; unselected pages are copied through at their own size with `/Rotate 90` intact and the mix is named; annots and the outline are dropped **and said** ("4 links or annotations could not come along", the bookmarks sentence); card counts equal the file on every case (4 → 4).<br>**Three defects, all fixed.** (1) **"Stretch to exactly fit" was contain in disguise** — the sheet was handed to `stamp` pre-scaled and `stamp`'s own default is `contain`, so a 200×500 page onto A4 came out at ink aspect **0.474 → 0.474** (a square stayed a square) while the card said "circles become ovals"; the full sheet is now the destination with `stamp({ fit: 'stretch' })`, **0.474 → 0.834** (0.838 owed by the raw scale). (2) **The crop note quoted an area ratio, not the crop** — `1 − pageArea/sheetArea` ignores the scale: on the crop fixture it said "up to **83% of the sheet**" for a page that loses **21%**, and it counted pages cropped by a tenth of a point (`on 4 pages` where one loses anything). It now divides the *sheet* by the *drawn* area and says "of the page": the same run reads **on 1 page — up to 50% of the page is cropped**, the 595×420 sheet being the one that really loses half; a half-A4 page at A4's aspect (cropped by nothing, reported 75% before) is now silent. (3) **A page larger than the paper was scaled down without a word** — the 1190×1684 sheet went to A4 at 50% under a card saying only "Normalised 2 pages"; it now reads "Fit inside scaled 1 page down to 50% of its own size, so the type on it prints smaller", and the A4 sheet in the same run is not counted. **The identity was thrown away too:** the title and author left and the producer stayed the source's `pdf-lib` on every run; `carryDocInfo` + `setProducer('BrowserSnip')` now run as for `n-up`/`divide-pages` and the output carries the title and author (control: a bare sheet invents none). **Red without the fix: 5 failed / 136 passed** with `organize.ts` stashed (`tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`: the stretch `cm` chain and its contain control, the cropped share and its no-crop control, the shrink count and its control, the info carry); **0 failed / 141 passed** with it, full suite 3 149 passed | 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no console error in any run, dark mode on tokens only (`shots/fix-page-size-dark.png`).<br>**Left open, measured:** (1) annots, links, form fields, attachments and the outline are still dropped — warned with counts, not carried, as in every rebuild tool; (2) the source `/CropBox` is baked and the output has none, so the crop cannot be re-opened afterwards — the displayed pixels are identical, which is why it was not changed; (3) `orientation: keep` with a `most-common` sheet shrinks a landscape sheet onto the portrait paper (0.502 of its area) rather than turning the paper for it — that is what "one size and orientation" means, but a per-page `keep` size is not offered. | fix-page-size's own `run` in `tools/organize.ts`;
