`docs/research/fix-page-size-bytes.py`, `docs/research/fix-page-size-round-{before,after}.log`, `docs/research/shots/fix-page-size-*`; the five pins in `tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts` |
| `pdfs-to-zip` | verified-good (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The container itself was already right, on both halves; the entry *names* and the intake in front of it were not.** Measured on the live `<127.0.0.1:5271>` build with `docs/research/zip-campaign.mjs` (a synthetic drop, because `DOM.setFileInputFiles` hands the page an **empty** `FileList` for a non-ASCII path on this Chromium — proven by `docs/research/zip-unicode-probe.mjs`, `{"count":0}`, so the unicode cases cannot be driven through the picker) — 20 runs, every produced archive kept, every pane and card screenshot read — and judged on the download by `unzip -v` (central directory: names, method, sizes), `unzip` + SHA-256 against the input, and PyMuPDF on every extracted entry (page count, box, `/Rotate`, characters, ink at 72 dpi). **Already right, and now pinned:** every entry is **byte-identical** to its input (SHA-256 of `colour.pdf`, `sheets.pdf`, `locked.pdf`, `locked128.pdf`, `naïve.pdf`, `日本語.pdf` equal in and out on every run) and still opens to the source's own census — `colour.pdf` sheet 1 59 467 ink px, sheet 3 0, sheet 4 `595×420 /Rotate 90` at 142 563, identical to the source file; the entry names are the input names in intake order; `../evil.pdf` → `evil.pdf` and `sub/dir/deep.pdf` → `deep.pdf` with nothing climbing out; unicode and spaces survive; the same file twice → `colour.pdf` + `colour-2.pdf` with the note; one file runs ("You packed a single PDF — the archive is the same file plus a manifest."); the action is not offered at zero files ("Accepts PDF · any number of files"); the manifest toggle off leaves 5 158 B / 2 entries and no `manifest.txt`; **locked inputs go in as-is and stay locked** — both entries read `needs_pass 1`, 4 pages and 1 835 characters after `authenticate("reader-pass")`, and the card says so; the card's entry list and sizes match the central directory exactly (3 entries, `manifest.txt` 175 B, archive 5 399 B = the card's `5.3 KB`), and dark mode is tokens only. **Three defects, all fixed.** (1) **A drop that landed in the page's first frames lost all but one file, silently.** `useToolDefinition` imports the registry in an effect, so `api.tool` is undefined for a frame; `ToolSurface` read `multiple` from it, so the drop zone said *"one file at a time"* and `acceptFiles` kept `list[0]` — measured **3/3 runs, four files dropped at the frame the file input appears → one row, three gone**, with the wrong notice *This tool works on one file at a time, so it is using "one.pdf"*; after, the same probe reads `"Select PDF \| or drop PDFs here \| any number of files"` on the first frame and **4 rows for 4 files, 3/3**. (2) **Two 202-character names silently became one entry plus an unannounced `-2`** — the tool handed raw leaves to `createZip`, which cleans (leading dots off, 180-char clip) and only *then* de-duplicates, so the clash the clip created never reached the tool's own note: 4 files in → `…a*172.pdf` and `…a*172-2.pdf`, no sentence; cleaning now happens first (`entryNameFor`), the clash note fires and names both, and entries stay inside 180. (3) **`..pdf` was stored as `pdf`** — an entry no double-click opens, because the dot rule ate the whole stem; it is `pdf.pdf` with a note "\"..pdf\" is not a name an unzip tool can keep", and every renamed entry is now said out loud (the manifest, which previously printed the queue's names under an archive holding different ones, prints the entry names). Copy: *"Packed 1 PDFs"* → `Packed 1 PDF`; and the note *"this is a container, not a compressor"* was false — every entry measures `Defl:N` (deflate level 6) and the tool's own manifest and card say "before compression"/"42% smaller" — it now reads *"Files go in byte-for-byte — the archive around them is deflated, but no PDF inside it is recompressed — and a password-protected PDF stays protected inside it."* **Red without the fix:** `ToolSurface`'s one-line lookup reverted → **1 failed / 59 passed** (`tests/pdf/ux-task-shell.test.tsx`, the four-file early drop); `entryNameFor` reverted → **2 failed / 146 passed** (`tests/pdf/edge-organize.test.ts`, `expected [ 'pdf', … ] to deeply equal [ 'pdf.pdf', … ]` and the clash note absent); the `PDF/PDFs` ternary reverted → **1 failed / 147 passed** (same file). **Left open, measured:** (1) `createZip` still de-duplicates silently for its other callers (`pdf-to-cbz`, `extract-images`, `extract-attachments`, `convert-from`) — only this tool cleans first; (2) an incompressible PDF pays deflate for nothing — `noise.pdf` (2 919 898 B of DCT noise) came back 2 916 261 B, i.e. 0.1 % for the CPU of compressing 2.8 MB — and the "no smaller than the files" sentence only fires when the archive really is not smaller (it did not on any corpus fixture); (3) the fixed `manifest.txt` name could collide with an input only through a constructed `File` (inputs must be PDFs) and would be renumbered by the writer without a note; (4) the empty-state footnote is clipped to one line with an ellipsis, so the second half of the note is only readable in the notes disclosure; (5) the unicode cases are drop-only here — a *picker* pick of a non-ASCII name was not driven, because the driver cannot hand one over. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/organize.ts` (the `pdfs-to-zip` run and its note), shared seams `src/components/tools/pdf/shared/ToolSurface.tsx` (the definition lookup that keeps the first frame honest) and `src/hooks/usePdfTool.ts` (`toolId` on the api, because the id is known before the definition is), `tests/pdf/{edge-organize,ux-task-shell}.test.ts`, `docs/research/zip-campaign.mjs`, `docs/research/zip-unicode-probe.mjs` |
| `linearize-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The screen already said the truth; the file never was a linearized PDF, and the tool could not make one with the engine it named.** `linearize-pdf` declares CoherentPDF, whose writer stopped linearizing at v1.8 — the CLI shells out to an external `cpdflin` binary a browser tab has not got — so `toMemory(pdf, true, …)` threw `ERROR: toFileMemory: Pdf.PDFError("Linearization not supported since v1.8. Use an external linearizer.")` on **every** file, and the run fell through to the local rebuild every time. The card compounded it: the throw was reported as *"CoherentPDF could not be reached"*, which sends the user to look at their connection for a capability the library does not have. **Before, measured** (`docs/research/linearize-round-before.log`, `LABEL='^Linearize$'`, `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf`, `<127.0.0.1:5271>`, 3 904 B): 2 293 B out, `/Linearized` occurrences **0**, first object at byte 20 an object stream, **one** xref stream at the end (offset 2 069), `pikepdf.is_linearized` **False**, `check_linearization()` → *"called readLinearizationData for file that is not linearized"*, card **"ENGINE local path · cpdf not used"** + *"Rebuilt with object streams and 0 newly compressed stream(s) … This is not a conforming linearized PDF."* **Fix — the cause, not the sentence:** qpdf (12.2.0, Apache-2.0, `qpdf-wasm@0.1.0`, a 2.2 MB CDN-fetched WASM module; new fourth engine in `engines/provider.ts` + `engines/qpdf.ts`) writes the parameter dictionary, the two cross-reference sections and the hint tables itself. Two things the loader had to solve, both measured: Chromium refuses a *cross-origin* worker script (`Script at … cannot be accessed from origin …`, classic and module, `linearize-round-after-crossworker.log`), so its pthread workers are built from a same-origin blob URL; and the Emscripten build binds `console.log` at start-up, so the console bridge is installed **before** the factory (a sink attached after it captured an empty verdict). The seam then checks its own output with `qpdf --check-linearization` and throws unless the checker says *"no linearization errors"*; the tool credits a file only when that passed **and** the two structural facts a reader acts on hold. **After, measured** (same probe, same fixture, `linearize-round-after.log`): 4 518 B, `/Linearized` at byte **26** (**inside** the first 1024), `/L 4518` **equal to the file length to the byte**, `/H [562 136]` naming a real object carrying `/S`, first-page object `/O 11` at **698**, `/E 2454`, `/T 4240`, **two** xref sections, `pikepdf.is_linearized` **True**, `check_linearization()` **ok**, card **"ENGINE qpdf"** + *"Linearized with qpdf: … page 1 first, and its own checker found no errors."*, **4 in · out not counted** (bytes, not a document). Ink identical to the source on all four pages — **61 153 / 49 571 / 0 / 142 563** px, page 3's **0** the instrument's control — with text (1 779 / 13 / 0 / 0), boxes, `/Rotate 90` on page 4 and the title equal. A second run on a built carry fixture (`/tmp/triage/annots.pdf`, annot + link + 2-entry outline + author + `/Rotate 90`, `linearize-round-annots.log`, `linearize-check-annots.py` log): every one of those survives at the same rects and pages, and it too passes the checker. **The probe was the second defect:** `isLinearized` searched 64 KB for the *name* and ignored `/L`, so a dictionary a page into the file, or one whose `/L` disagrees with the size (which qpdf treats as "not linearized, ignore the hints"), read as success. It now requires the dictionary inside the first 1024 bytes **and** `/L` equal to the length. **Red without the fix: 3 failed / 88 passed** with the old probe restored (`linearize-pdf.test.ts`: the `/L` mismatch, the >1024 offset, and the tool being driven by it); **1 failed / 9** with the attribution split reverted (an engine that *ran and refused* reported as "could not be reached"). **Left open, measured:** (1) Fast Web View also needs the host to answer byte ranges with `206` — the card says so, and no server here was measured serving a linearized file over HTTP; (2) the fallback on an unreachable engine is unchanged and is still a plain rebuild: 2 371 B from `/tmp/triage/uncompressed.pdf`, *"Rebuilt with object streams and 0 newly compressed stream(s) … not a conforming linearized PDF"*, ink identical, `is_linearized` False — and the count is honest, because both corpus fixtures are already Flate (a synthesized unfiltered 4 KB stream is the pin that it rises above 0); (3) a file that grows is not kept, so a linearized output can be 16–18 % larger and the card only warns; (4) qpdf's dialog is bilingual only in English here and its exit code is 0 for `--check-linearization` on a non-linearized file, so the verdict is read from its sentence, not its status; (5) `runQpdf`'s check gate can only be exercised in a browser — the WASM build is pthread-based and Node has no `Worker`, so the unit tests double the seam and the real binary is judged by this round's probes. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/optimize.ts` (`linearize-pdf` run, `isLinearized`, notes/promise), new `src/lib/pdf/engines/qpdf.ts`, `src/lib/pdf/core/engine-ops.ts` (`linearizeWithQpdf`, `enginePurpose`), shared registrations `src/lib/pdf/engines/{provider,auto,index}.ts`, `src/lib/pdf/tools/types.ts`, `src/components/tools/pdf/shared/ResultCard.tsx`; `tests/pdf/linearize-pdf.test.ts` (new), `tests/pdf/{edge-optimize,optimize-bytes,pages-out,ux-surfaces,engine-attribution,engine-auto,engines-provider,registry}.test.ts`; `docs/research/linearize-check.py`, `docs/research/linearize-round-*.log`, `docs/research/shots/linearize-*.png` |
| `pdf-to-pdfa` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) — engine path **verified-good by veraPDF**, local path fixed | **A real PDF/A validator was fetched into this sandbox and run for the first time, and it splits the tool in two: the Ghostscript path is compliant, the offline path named an identification no validator could read.** veraPDF 1.28.2 (greenfield, PDFA-2B profile, OpenJDK 27; `docs/research/pdfa-validator.sh` rebuilds the jar from `software.verapdf.org`, one command per verdict in `docs/research/pdfa-validator-round.log`) — **the ledger's and the tool's note "no veraPDF here" is superseded.** <br>**Engine path, `colour.pdf` through the rail's own `Convert to PDF/A` (default 2b) on `<127.0.0.1:5271>`, download read back outside the app:** `isCompliant="true"`, **144 passed rules, 0 failed, 882 passed checks**, card `PAGES 4 → 4`, `ENGINE Ghostscript`, 7 872 B, no console error, pane and card in `docs/research/shots/pdfa-pane-engine.png`. The file is what the card says and nothing is repainted: text **1 779 / 13 / 0 / 0 characters** and ink **61 153 / 49 569 / 0 / 142 563** non-white px at 72 dpi, equal to the source page for page (sheet 3's 0 is the instrument's control), the `/Rotate 90` sheet keeping its 595×420 display as a swapped box with `/Rotate 0`. Identification `pdfaid:part=2 conformance=B`, `/OutputIntents` → `/S /GTS_PDFA1` with a **476-byte `/N 3` profile whose `acsp` signature and RGB space lcms2 parses** (`docs/research/pdfa-output-intent.icc`), `/MarkInfo /Marked true`, XMP well-formed (lxml), no `/Encrypt`, no `/JS`, no `/URI`, no `/AF`, one subset face `LVNTJP+Helvetica` with `/FontFile3` — **1 of 1 embedded**, as printed. Controls: the untouched source judged 2b **fails 4 rules** (6.6.2.1, 6.1.3, 6.2.4.3, 6.2.11.4.1, 75 checks), and the same 2b output judged against **1b fails 2** — the profile is being applied, not assumed.<br>**The defect, local path (engine refused, which is the offline tab):** 4 failed rules, and **three were this app's own, not the font it already warns about** — §6.1.3 *"The file trailer dictionary shall contain the ID keyword"*, §6.6.2.1 test 5 *"The XMP package must be encoded as UTF-8"* and, because the packet could not be parsed, §6.6.4 *"The document metadata stream doesn't contain PDF/A Identification Schema"*, all under a card reading *"Wrote PDF/A-2b identification (pdfaid:part=2, conformance=B)"*. Causes, measured: the packet went through pdf-lib's **latin1** string encoder, so the `\uFEFF` BOM left as one `0xFF` byte (`readXmp` read it anyway — the app's reader was less strict than a validator), pdf-lib's writer emits **no trailer `/ID`**, and the packet mirrored only title, author and producer where §6.6.2.2 wants every `/Info` entry that has an analogue. Fixed at the writer (`buildPdfAExtension` now emits the toolkit's packet shape with the dates, `xmp:CreatorTool`, the `xmpMM` ids and `dc:format`; the stream is UTF-8 bytes and uncompressed; two 16-byte `/ID` strings are written when the trailer has none). **After, same command: 4 failed rules → 1**, the remaining one the un-embedded font the card already names *"a validator will reject this file as it stands"* — and it is now `isCompliant` on the identification itself. Red without the fix: **1 failed / 15 passed** with `optimize.ts` stashed (`pdfa-structure.test.ts`, *"store the packet as UTF-8 and mirror every /Info entry"* — fatal UTF-8 decode, no `/Filter`, each `/Info` entry present in the packet, two 32-hex `/ID` values), and one neighbour re-pinned because it inflated the stream (`optimize-bytes`). 3 196 tests green, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0, no new dependency, no `.tsx` touched. **Left open, measured:** (1) the engine path silently drops annotations, links, form fields, the outline and page labels — colour.pdf has none, so this row states it from the source's catalogue and does not measure it; (2) `/Lang` is still absent, which is a **level-A** duty (§6.8.4, "full conformance") and not a 2b one, so the b levels pass without it and the tool offers no A level; (3) the engine normalises `/Rotate 90` into the page box instead of keeping both — the display is identical and veraPDF accepts it; (4) veraPDF 1.28.2 runs only here: it is 12 MB of jar and cannot enter a browser tab, so the card's "no validator ran" stays true. |
| `sanitize-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The switch that exists to take a payload took the pointer and left the payload, and on a PDF/A-3 file it took nothing and said there was nothing.** The `CONTROL INERT` rows (`ff66110`) proved the JavaScript switch reaches the bytes; this round attacked the other side — over-removal and under-removal. Seven switches off on a rich file: the output matches the source page for page, ink diff **0 px on all four sheets**, `/Annots`, `/AcroForm`+`/Rect`+`/AS`, `/PageLabels`, `/Outlines`, `/OCProperties`, `/Info` and `/Lang` all byte-identical, and the card says *"Nothing to remove: no switch was on."* — that half was already right and is now pinned. The hidden-layer switch removes exactly the named layer's span (`docs/research/colour-rail.mjs`, `layers-on`): with both layers forced on, the difference is **one text line, 1 158 px at bbox (61,643)-(257,660)**, the visible line's band **4 274 px identical both sides**, the ON array kept, one definition deleted; the off default changes nothing. **The defect — `Remove embedded files`:** it emptied the `/Names → /EmbeddedFiles` tree and stopped. (1) The `/Type /EmbeddedFile` stream stayed in the output — `attach.pdf`, 4 216 B in → 2 614 B out, the payload still decompressible — while the field's own help promises the deletion is final in the copy. (2) A catalogue `/AF` associated file was never looked at and the card printed **"Nothing to remove: no embedded files were present"** over a file whose XML was still named by `/AF` and still readable, a shape this app's own `listAttachments` reads. (3) The switch **off** said nothing about a payload that stays. **Fix, the cause:** `core/attachments.ts:stripAllAttachments` reuses the one walk `filespecSites` (name tree, `/AF`, `/FileAttachment` `/FS`) and `removeSites` — so a file named twice counts once — deletes the file specification and its `/EF` streams once nothing else refers to them, drops an emptied `/AF`, `/EmbeddedFiles` tree and `/Names` holder, and clears `/PageMode /UseAttachments`. `content.ts:stripEmbeddedFiles` is now a delegate. **After, measured** (`docs/research/sanitize-scope-after.json`, checker `sanitize-scope-measure.py`): `/AF` false, `/Names` false, no `/Filespec`, no `/FS`, **payload gone** from every decompressed stream, page count and each page's content bytes and ink unchanged (**0 px**), card **"Sanitized: 1 embedded file(s) removed."**; the off control keeps all three and says *"1 embedded file(s) left in place"* + *"stay in the output and are still extractable"*. Copy: "metadata" → "XMP metadata" (the default leaves `/Info`), and the JS help now says a `/OpenAction` that only names a view stays — measured: a `/GoTo` `/OpenAction`, a `/WS` `/AA` trigger and a link `/URI` `/A` all survive the script switch, the `/WC` script and the JS link do not. Absent subjects say "no annotations were present" instead of "0 annotation(s) removed". **Red without the fix: 6 failed / 2 passed** (`tests/pdf/sanitize-scope.test.ts`; the two green in both states are the payload-free control and the page-content identity), 8 green with it, 202 green across the six files that touch the shared module. **Left open, measured:** (1) removing an annotation leaves its dictionary and `/AP` stream in the file as unreferenced objects — the FreeText note's text is still extractable after "5 annotation(s) removed"; the card's note discloses non-redaction, and the same sweep would close it; (2) deleting an object leaves a hole in the xref MuPDF answers `cannot find object in xref (12 0 R)` for — poppler, pypdf (strict) and pdf.js all open the file, so it is the fork's writer, recorded not fixed; (3) `/Thumb` and unused resources are untouched and unclaimed; (4) the `view-metadata` card's `attachmentCount` is still name-tree-only, so it under-reports an `/AF` file the same way this switch did. | `src/lib/pdf/core/attachments.ts` (`stripAllAttachments`, `embeddedFileCount`, `removeSites`, `tidyEmbeddedFileTrees`), `src/lib/pdf/core/content.ts` (delegate), `src/lib/pdf/tools/optimize.ts` (attachments branch, off-switch sentence, annotations-absent sentence, description, JS help); `tests/pdf/sanitize-scope.test.ts` (new); `docs/research/sanitize-scope-{fixtures.py,measure.py,before.json,after.json,before.log,after.log}` |
| `page-numbers` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The folio landed right and the words did not: the count was padded with a hard-coded three digits and only the first token of a suffix was filled in, and the margin was measured to the em box so `margin: 0` cut the descenders off.** Measured first, on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` (4 sheets, sheet 3 empty, sheet 4 a `595×420 /Rotate 90`), dev `:5271`, `research/page-numbers-before-a.json`/`-before-b.json` through `research/colour-rail.mjs`, `research/page-numbers-round.mjs` for the big-font pane, judged by `research/page-numbers-place.py` — a source-vs-output bitmap diff whose ink bbox is reported as a fraction of the *displayed* page, so the fixture's own colour squares cannot pass for the number, and a 1200 dpi strip that counts columns of ink on the crop edge. **What was already right, both halves:** all nine anchors land where their label says on an upright sheet and on the turned one (`ink_x` 0.042–0.134 left / 0.437–0.567 centre / 0.867–0.960 right; `ink_y_from_top` 0.046–0.064 top / 0.496–0.514 middle / 0.923–0.964 bottom), the run stays upright — `w/h_ratio` 4.8–5.2 whatever `/Rotate` is — and inside the page box; `skip`/`step`/`pages` number exactly the pages chosen and restart the count where told (skip 1 + every 2nd → `Page 1 of 2` on sheet 2 only, `pages=2-4` → `of 3`, sheet 3's empty page gets its number); `/Info`, 1/1 annot, 1/1 link and the 2-entry and 3-entry outlines survive byte-identical (`annots.pdf`, `bookmarked.pdf`); the card counts match the file (`PAGES 4 → 4`); a 72 pt over-long folio is centred, runs past both edges and is admitted in those words on the *displayed* box (`595×420`). **Found and fixed:** `padded` wrote `Page 001 of 004` on a four-sheet file while its own option reads "01, 02, 03" (now the width of the last number, never fewer than two: `Page 01 of 04`, `098–100` when the count needs three); `.replace()` filled the **first** `{total}` only, so a suffix of `X{total}/{total}` shipped a literal `{total}` into the page — `Page 001X002/{total}` → `Page 1X2/2/1` (`split().join()` for both tokens, `{page}` included); and at `margin: 0` the baseline sat on the crop edge, putting the descenders outside it — bbox bottom **−3.29 pt** on an upright sheet and **423.29 of 420** on the turned one, **259 columns of ink on the crop edge** → **0**, bbox bottom −0.81 pt (line-gap slack, no ink), after placing the run by its ink box rather than its em box (the face's own declared lower bound, `/FontBBox` or the outline's bbox). The same seam is in `stamp`, so the fix is shared with `watermark`/`header-footer`/`add-stamps` and their two position tests moved with it. 6 red without it (4 new, 2 re-pinned: the rotated-corner and header/footer rows now assert the baseline clears the margin by a descender); 3222 green after, full suite. Dark mode shot taken and looked at; the card's copy (position, margin, style and token help) is true to the bytes as written. Left open, measured: the preview always opens on page 1, which a `pages=4` run did not number (the round's probe presses the strip's own chip); `padded` at `startAt: 0` still gives `00`. | `tools/edit.ts` (`formatNumber`, the token fill, `stamp`), `tests/pdf/{edit-bytes,edge-edit}.test.ts` |
| `watermark` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The pattern was not the one chosen and it walked off the page: the mark’s own width was measured only when tiling was *off* (one inverted `!tile`), so the column pitch fell to the 24 pt floor for any size or spacing, and the grid stopped on the mark’s origin while a turned run keeps going past it.** Measured first, on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` (4 sheets, sheet 3 empty, sheet 4 a `420×595 /Rotate 90`), dev `:5271`, `research/watermark-round-before.json` via `research/colour-rail.mjs`, judged by `research/watermark-mark.py` — a source-vs-output bitmap diff whose added ink is reported as a fraction of the *displayed* page (so the fixture’s colour squares cannot pass for the mark), plus the `Tm` grid read out of the appended content stream. **Before:** a 217 pt word at 64 pt repeated every **24 pt** in x (Tm x-step 24, 72 marks on a 420×595 sheet, 16 542 B), the ink reached the crop on both axes (`ink_x` 0.002–1.000, `ink_y_from_top` 0.001–0.993, **1 083** mark px in the last two pixel columns and rows); a tiled image at 45° laid its first mark at (0,0) and its last 156 pt past the crop — 28 marks, edge 632 px, ink 0.002–1.000 / 0.001–0.999. **After:** the pitch is the chosen multiple of the mark (`CONFIDENTIAL` 40 pt at gap 1.5 → 169.98 pt = 1× the face’s own advance × 1.5, gap 3 → double), the grid is bounded by the mark’s *turned* footprint and anchored by its lower corner, so no mark crosses an edge — 3 marks/page, ink `ink_x` 0.298–0.707 / 0.031–0.933, **edge 0 px**; the tiled image likewise 18 marks, 0.080–0.920 / 0.037–0.963, edge 0 px. **Already right, both halves:** one mark lands inside the box at the angle asked on the upright and the turned sheet (ink 0.300–0.707 / 0.358–0.647, `w/h_ratio` 0.94–0.95 both, edge 0); opacity is monotone and survives its floor — the blank sheet’s darkest mark pixel **136** → **195** → **243** on 255 paper at 1.0/0.5/0.1; face, size and colour reach the file (`Helvetica-Bold` 64 pt `#888888`, `/rg 0.5333`); an image keeps its 1.4 aspect at 35 % of the page and a transparent PNG keeps its alpha (`/SMask`, **75.1 %** of the image footprint bit-identical to the paper); `pages=2-4` leaves page 1 at **0** added ink while `/Info` title, 1/1 annot, 1/1 link and the 2-entry outline come out identical; behind-content loses **0** of 72 042 source text px against **1 188** when over. **Fixed in the same seam:** the over-long note named an 18-character prefix as if it were the mark (“CONFIDENTIAL DRAFT” fits a 420 pt page) — it now says “the first 18 of 58 characters”. 4 red without the fix (all four new: pitch, text footprint, image footprint, excerpt wording), 3 226 green after, full suite. Dark-mode shot at 0.3 opacity taken and looked at. Left open, measured: the preview still opens on page 1, so a `pages=2-4` run previews an unmarked sheet; a mark *wider* than the page still overflows (centred and reported); each tile registers its own font object (72 objects for 72 tiles). The evidence driver `research/colour-rail.mjs` needed two repairs to reach an image at all — a `file` field resolved to its wrapper div and the picker tag went stale under a re-render — both fixed there. | `tools/edit.ts` (`tilePoints`, the tile branch, the over-long note), `tests/pdf/edge-edit.test.ts`, `research/{colour-rail.mjs,watermark-mark.py,watermark-round-*} ` |
| `header-footer` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The bands landed right; the two hairlines were measured to the em box and the two bands never consulted each other.** Measured first on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` (4 sheets, sheet 3 empty, sheet 4 a `420×595 /Rotate 90` showing `595×420`) and on a built `400×260` sheet (`/tmp/triage/hf-short.pdf`), dev `:5271`, `research/header-footer-before.json` through `research/colour-rail.mjs`, judged by `research/header-footer-bands.py` — a source-vs-output ink diff per page whose bands, gap, edge-row ink and off-band content loss are numbers, plus the spans' font/size/colour. **Defect 1, the rule through the descenders:** `margin + size + 4` put the header hairline 44 pt from the top under a 36 pt `gypsy Łódź` whose ink reaches 53 — the line was drawn through the `g`, `y` and `p` (`shots/header-footer-before-big-font-zoom.png`); it now hangs 4 pt off the run's **ink box** (58.3–59.0 on the same page, 7.9 pt clear), measured from the box `stamp` returns. **Defect 2, the bands printed over each other:** on 400×260 at 36 pt with the 120 pt margin `placeRun` halved the margin for each band *separately* (108.5), so header ink 110.4–159.8 and footer 108.5–151.5 shared the same 43 pt of paper and nothing was said (`shots/header-footer-before-short-zoom.png`); the two now take one margin computed from both heights and the gap is reported — **1 failed → bands 90.0–123.8 and 134.6–168.5, gap 10.8 pt, rules 128.7 and 131.4 between them, card “A 260 pt page cannot hold a 33 pt header and a 33 pt footer 120 pt from both edges, so the margin becomes 90 pt.”** **Defect 3, nothing said a band was taller than the margin:** at 36 pt the band is 49 pt and the margin 4 — now the card says so. A rule with no band beside it is no longer drawn (the old code drew a stray footer line for an empty footer). **Already right, both halves, re-measured after:** every header/footer run lands inside the page box with **0** ink on all four crop edges — default margin, `margin 0` → 4 (clamped and admitted) with `gypsy Łódź gy`/`accént`/`gjpqy`/`Página 1 — Łódź gy`, and on the turned sheet, where the header sits at the displayed top and stays upright (`shots/header-footer-after-margin0.png`, `-after-big-font-zoom.png`); **content outside the bands unchanged, `content_lost_outside_bands=0`, `other_offband_change=0` on all four sheets × seven runs**; size 20, `#C00000` and `Times-Roman` reach both bands (`color=#C00000 font=Times-Roman size=20` via PyMuPDF), not one band; `{page}` `{total}` `{title}` `{author}` `{date}` `{file}` fill per page and `{nope}` stays literal; `pages=4` leaves sheets 1–3 at **0** added ink and stamps the turned sheet; the **empty sheet 3 gets both bands** (5 188 added px, source ink 0); `/Info` title+author, 1/1 annot, 1/1 link and the 2-entry outline come out byte-identical on `annots.pdf`; counts `PAGES 4 → 4` and `2 → 2`. **Red without the fix: 3 failed / 4 passed** with `tools/edit.ts` stashed (descender clearance, the two-band clamp, the taller-than-margin admission), 7 green with it, 3 229 green in the full suite; `tsc -b`, `typecheck:tests` and `vite build` clean. **Left open, measured:** (1) one style panel for both bands — the card does not promise a face, size or colour *per band*, so the size, colour and face are shared (verified to reach both); separating them is a fields change, not a defect; (2) `margin 0` is a clamp, not a zero — the field's own minimum is 4, and page-numbers' 0 is not offered here; (3) dark mode was shot on the result card (`shots/header-footer-after-dark.png`): warnings and counts read, but the shared `ResultCard` buttons “Download”/“Keep original” are pale-on-pale there — a shared component, not moved; (4) `size` is clamped to 36, so a two-band overlap on a *full-size* sheet needs a page shorter than ~326 pt and is exercised by the built fixture, not by the corpus. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/edit.ts` (`stamp` ink box, the header-footer band margin, rule placement, copy), `tests/pdf/edge-edit.test.ts` (3 rows); | `background-color` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The fill landed behind and the content stayed on top; a page whose `/Contents` was an indirect array lost every stream it had, and the artifact property list carried a `Type` no consumer recognises.** Measured on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` (4 sheets: copy, colour squares, **an empty sheet**, a `595×420 /Rotate 90`) plus `/tmp/triage/in/annot.pdf` (3 annots + `/Info`) and `/tmp/triage/in/ink.pdf` (an image XObject), dev `:5271`, `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (every field written as a user event and read back out of the DOM before the click; the download taken through `downloadBlob`'s own URL), judged by the new `docs/research/background-ink.py` — per region the fill fraction, the letter-white fraction and the dark-glyph fraction, each against the same measurement of the untouched source, **the instrument verified first**: source against itself FAILs (`white only fell 0.0 pp`) and a solid block reports `VACUOUS` rather than a pass. **Already right, both halves, and now with numbers.** `/Artifact … BDC` is the first thing in `/Contents` on all 4 sheets and the fill is written with `re f`, so the page's own drawing paints over it: on the copy region the source mean 220.7 becomes the fill and the glyphs stay dark (dark 10.8 → 13.7 %), the band goes 255 → **100.0 % fill**, the turned sheet is covered across the **displayed** 595×420 (white 42.9 → 8.7 %, fill 0 → 33.9 %, `/Rotate 90` and its blocks untouched), the empty sheet becomes the fill (white 100 % → 100 % fill, not paper), and nothing outside the fill moves: the original content streams are byte-identical, text stays 1779 chars and extractable, `/Info`, `/CropBox` and `/Rotate` survive, 3 annots stay 3, the image XObject stream's SHA-1 is unchanged. **Defect 1, silent and user-visible.** `contentsArray` only recognised a *direct* `/Contents` array, and `pageContentStreams` only read one: given `/Contents 12 0 R` (12 an array), both treated the reference as junk, so the page read as empty and the fill became `/Contents`'s only entry with the page's own array nested behind it — the flat blue page the app's own preview drew. Measured (source `edges.pdf` = sheet 1 of `colour.pdf` with its `/Contents` made indirect, rendered 100 dpi): **text 1779 → 0, dark px 14.81 % → 0.00 %**, MuPDF `format error: object is not a stream`; after the fix, 1779 chars and 10.8 → 17.5 % dark, `/Contents` still `('xref', '12 0 R')` with the fill first inside the array. **Defect 2, format.** The property list said `/Type /Artifact /Subtype /Background`; the marked-content *tag* is `/Artifact`, the `Type` must be one of `Pagination`/`Layout`/`Page`/`Background` (ISO 32000-1 §14.8.2.2.1 Table 331) and `/Subtype` appears only with `Pagination` (ISO 32000-2 Table 363) — so it now writes `/Type /Background` and no `/Subtype`, `/BBox` and `/Attached` kept. **Opacity, measured because the card had none:** `opacity=0.25` writes `/ca` in an ExtGState referenced by `gs` inside the fill's own `q Q`; the band mean 255 → (200.0,214.0,234.0) against 0.25·fill + 0.75·source predicted at (198.8,213.8,233.3), and the words stay on top (dark 10.9 → 12.3 %). **Red without the fix: 3 failed / 5** in the `background-color` block of `tests/pdf/edit-bytes.test.ts` (the artifact type, the indirect `/Contents`, the opacity state; the two controls stay green by construction), 0 failed with it, 77 green in the file, full suite green. Left open, measured: a **tiled fill** does not exist on the card (one flat `re` per page); a dark fill also raises the `dark%` a glyph check counts, so `background-ink.py` judges ink by the source's own figure rather than an absolute; a page that paints its own white rectangle shows that rectangle over the fill (measured on the turned sheet's white band, 8.7 % of the sheet), which is the page's choice and not repainted here. | `src/lib/pdf/core/content.ts` (`contentsArray` and `pageContentStreams` resolve an indirect `/Contents` array — shared with every tool that reads or prepends content — and `prependBackground`'s property list and optional `/ca`), `src/lib/pdf/tools/edit.ts` (the opacity field, its copy, the report line), `tests/pdf/edit-bytes.test.ts`, `docs/research/background-ink.py`, `docs/PDF-ENGINE-CONTRACT.md` |
| `text-color` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **Two shapes of run were left black while the card said they had been recoloured, and the count was of operators, not runs.** The helper looked for a colour operator with a text-show before the next path paint, so (1) a page that sets a colour, paints a rule and then draws a run *inheriting* it — what Word and every print driver writes for a ruled line — had the rule's `f` break the search, and (2) a run coloured through `/Pattern cs /P1 scn` or an `/ICCBased` space was skipped because `scn` is not a `rg`. It now walks the graphics state (`q`/`Q`, `cs`/`CS`, `g`/`rg`/`k`, `G`/`RG`/`K`, `sc`/`scn`/`SC`/`SCN`, `Tr`) and **inserts** the colour in front of each run instead of overwriting the operator that precedes it, so no rule is repainted; in a non-device space the run is wrapped in `q … /DeviceRGB cs … scn … Q` and the page's space is restored after it. Instrument first: the probe sheet `docs/research/text-color-probe.py` (one page, eight rows — black run, already-target run, rule then inheriting run, pattern run, ICC run, Form XObject run, image) driven by `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (`to=#ff0000 from=any`), read out of the produced stream and its 100 dpi pixels. **Before:** the card said `Recoloured 1 text run`; run A went 186 dark → 173 red, and C stayed 224 dark, D 206 dark, E 237 dark, the rule 1664 dark, F 153 dark, the image pixels byte-for-byte the same. **After:** `Recoloured 5 text runs`; A 173 red, C 203 red / 0 dark, D 195 red / 0 dark, E all red under a 200 dpi glyph crop, while the rule stays 1664 dark / 0 red, F stays 153 dark and the image is unchanged — the answer the card's own note gives ("text carried inside a Form XObject stays in that XObject"). **Count:** one `rg` in front of two runs used to report 1 for 2 changed runs; it reports runs now. **Already right and pinned, both halves, on `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf`** (sheet 1 copy, sheet 2 colour squares, sheet 3 empty, sheet 4 `595x420 /Rotate 90`): 26 + 1 = 27 runs, 1779 and 13 chars, unchanged by the fix — text 0x0 → 0xff0000 on both sheets, the bar stays 98.8 % dark, the copy region 12.61 % dark → 11.56 % red, pages 3 and 4 named as untouched, `/Contents [6 0 R]` (the indirect array of `2c7604a`) read and rewritten without loss, and `/Info`, page count, `/Rotate` (90 kept), `/CropBox` and the annot (1 `/Text`) all survive on `/tmp/triage/annots.pdf`; the turned text sheet `/tmp/triage/in/rot90.pdf` recolours 10 runs with its `/Rotate 90` intact; the sheet-level screenshots show the app's own preview drawing the red copy under the black bar, agreeing with the file. **Red without the fix: 4 failed / 8** in the new `text-color recolours the run, and nothing else` block of `tests/pdf/edit-bytes.test.ts` (the inherited run, the pattern space, the run count, the faux-bold stroke colour; the other four are controls and stay green), 0 failed with it, 11 green in the block. Left open, measured: text inside a Form XObject is not recoloured (documented, and pinned so a future change has to mean it); text set in DeviceGray space with a non-grey target, and a `/Separation` or `/Indexed` space, take the same `cs`+`scn` wrapper but were only exercised through `/ICCBased` and `/Pattern`; an invisible `3 Tr` run is counted as changed though no pixel moves. | `src/lib/pdf/core/content.ts` (`recolourText`; the state walk is local to it), `src/lib/pdf/tools/edit.ts` (promise, field help, note and the untouched-page wording), `tests/pdf/edit-bytes.test.ts`, `docs/research/text-color-probe.py`, `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (`text-color` labels), `docs/PDF-ENGINE-CONTRACT.md` |
| `form-filler` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **An answer typed over a filled dropdown shipped the old value, and the two surfaces that render the pane's own controls disagreed with each other.** Measured first, on `/tmp/triage/in/form.pdf` (text, a dropdown already holding `Alpha`, a checked box) and on a built fixture carrying every type the card claims (`docs/research/form-filler-fixture.pdf`, `f047-mk-fixture.ts`; text, multiline, maxlen 5, read-only, checkbox, radio group, filled dropdown, list, required text, push button), through `docs/research/form-filler-round.mjs` (six cases in one Chromium tab, every control written as a user event and read back out of the DOM before the save, pane **and** card screenshots kept) and judged by `docs/research/form-filler-round.py` (pypdf for `/V` — off the `/Parent` when there is one —, `/AS`, `/Ff`, `/AcroForm`, `/Annots`, `/Info`; MuPDF for what the page draws; page ink read with `annots=False`, because with widgets still in the file MuPDF paints their appearances and the result cannot tell baked ink from live). **Defect 1, the answer.** `core/forms` `applyValue` answered a dropdown/list with `select(value, true)`; `merge: true` concatenates onto `/V` and switches the control on to MultiSelect, so `Beta` typed over `Alpha` wrote **`/V [ (Alpha) <FEFF…Beta> ]`, `/Ff 131072 → 2228224`, and regenerated `/AP /N` from the *first* element — the stream drew `Alpha`**. The file therefore showed the value the user did not give, and the flattened page inked it: content stream (hex operands decoded) carried `Alpha` and no `Beta` after. After `select(wanted)`: `/V <FEFF…Beta>`, `/Ff 131072`, the appearance draws `Beta`, and the flattened page carries `Beta` with no `Alpha`. **Defect 2, the shape of a flattened file.** `flattenForm` deleted every field and left `<</AcroForm <</Fields []>>>>` in the catalog and `/Annots []` on the page — numbers a reader still acts on. Both go with the last field now: catalog `/AcroForm` **gone** and page `/Annots` **gone** on all three sheets, content-only ink 107 367 (source) → **108 753**, annots-on ink equal to content-only (nothing left to paint from an annotation). **Defect 3, a field with no row.** The tab-order list rendered `fields.filter(isFillable)`, so the push button counted in the header (`10 fields · 9 fillable`) and had no row, no name and no filter hit. `listedFields` returns every field: 10 rows, `action.button [button]`, found by the filter box. **Defect 4, the pane painted the file's old ink under its own answers.** pdf.js paints widget appearances by default, so `Alpha` showed through `Beta` on the canvas and a stale tick sat under the checkbox; `RenderOptions.annotations` is new and the pane passes `false` (`paintOverlayPage`), which is one option on a shared seam and named in the commit. **Defect 5, the radio group had one `name` for two copies.** Radios sharing a name are one native group, so only the copy rendered last could stay checked: clicking the overlay `Gold` left the list row ticked and the overlay ring empty, and the read-back said `none` where the file said `/V /0`. Distinct per variant, read-back `Gold` on both. **Defect 6, the card said nothing.** The pane unmounts the moment it sets an output, so the report (`Filled 9 fields; … 1 required field still empty (…)`) reached the log and no screen: the card showed a name and a size. It is written to the store's `warnings` now and painted under *What you should know* — `Filled 3 fields.` / `Filled 9 fields. 1 required field still empty (applicant.email). | `edit-metadata` | defect-found (fixed in dev `:5271`, not yet deployed; XMP half of the fix measured, the reader-display re-shot is open) | **The packet was rebuilt from the six fields the form shows, so saving a title deleted every XMP property it did not own.** Measured before, on the dev build with `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` and the produced bytes read by PyMuPDF (`/Info`) and lxml (packet): a fixture carrying `pdf:PDFVersion="1.7"`, `xmp:CreateDate`, `xmp:MetadataDate`, `dc:rights`, `dc:language` came back with all five gone (`xmplen` 723 → 856 but elements down to the six fields' own tags, `dc:rights`/`dc:language`/the three attributes absent) while `/Info`'s `/Trapped /Unknown`, `/Vendor`, `/AcmeJob` survived — a metadata editor deleting metadata. Also measured: each field wrote its own key with the exact string typed (`Report (final) v2 \\ draft — Ünïcode 日本語 🏷`, `A (subject) with \\ slash` round-tripped through UTF-16BE hex into both stores), blank fields kept the current value (key neither emptied nor removed: author/subject/keywords unchanged, `touchDates` off kept both `D:` dates byte-identical), sync off left the packet contradicting `/Info` on purpose (`/Title` `Contradiction on purpose` vs `dc:title` `XMP title`) exactly as the toggle's help says, and with no `/Title` in the file the sync wrote an **empty** `<rdf:li></rdf:li>` a reader prefers. **The card also promised dates it had no control for** — `creationDate`/`modDate` read `NO CONTROL` and the description said "and dates". Fix, the cause, in the shared module: `core/metadata.ts:syncXmp` **patches** the packet it finds (`patchProperty`, `descriptionFor`, `buildXmpPacket` for the absent-or-unparseable case) instead of `writeXmp`ing a fresh one, so unknown properties survive and a property whose `/Info` twin is absent is removed rather than left empty; the tool now has the two date fields the description promised, a typed value winning over the "set to now" toggle, and an unreadable date is refused in words (*«"not-a-date" is not a date. Use a PDF date such as D:20240131235959+00'00'…»*, screenshot `em-10-fail`, no file produced). After, same fixture: all five properties back and `dc:language` moved to `ja` with the typed `/Lang`, `/Trapped`/`Vendor`/`AcmeJob` intact, one `<dc:title>`; typed dates stored as `D:20260304130607+08'00'` / `D:20260305080000+08'00'` (= the typed instants, not the clock) and poppler `pdfinfo` displays `Title: Dated and authored`, `Author: Zoë Ångström`, both typed dates; all four sheets pixel- and text-identical to the source, 4 pages in and out. **Red without the fix: 5 failed / 1 passed** (`tests/pdf/metadata-xmp-edit.test.ts`; the green one in both states is the sync-off control). Pane: 11 controls in 3 groups, 3 notes, shot light (`em-8-pane`) and dark (`em-11-dark-pane`, tokens only). **Left open, measured:** the pane shows no current values (every box is a placeholder, so "blank keeps the current value" is only true in the notes); typed dates are normalised into the runtime's offset rather than kept verbatim, and a typed ISO date loses the `Z`; the packet is still replaced whole when it is not XML this parses (`meta.pdf` itself, an undeclared `rdf:` prefix) — property carry-over only exists on the parseable path; the Chrome PDF viewer painted blank over every *produced* file in this session (4314-byte grey capture, the same command renders the source), so the reader half here is poppler `pdfinfo`'s display and an earlier Chrome shot of the source's title, not a fresh Chrome shot of the edited one. | `src/lib/pdf/core/metadata.ts` (`syncXmp`, `patchProperty`, `descriptionFor`, `buildXmpPacket`, `XmpPatch`), `src/lib/pdf/tools/security.ts` (date fields, copy), `tests/pdf/metadata-xmp-edit.test.ts`, `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (`FIELDS_JSON`, the tool's label map) |

On a control built for the bystanders (`/PieceInfo` in the catalog **and** on page 1 with a private `/AcmeJob` dict, `/MarkInfo`, `/Lang en-GB`, `/PageMode`, `/ViewerPreferences/DisplayDocTitle`, a `/Perms` DocMDP record, an `/AcroForm` field, a text annot and a 2-entry outline; `/Info` 11 keys incl. `/Trapped`, `/AcmeJob`, `/AcmeOwner`) the same run claimed **12 properties** and deleted `/Lang` and `/Perms` with them. `/PieceInfo`, `/MarkInfo`, `/AcroForm` (12 field keys), the page content, the annot and the outline were already right on both fixtures — that half is now pinned. **Cause, in `core/metadata.ts:stripInfo`:** it deleted the dictionary's entries and never the trailer key, and deleted the catalog `/Lang` as a plus-one. It now removes the entries, then the trailer `/Info` **and the indirect object behind it**, and leaves `/Lang` alone; the count is therefore of keys really in `/Info`. `tools/security.ts` no longer takes `/Perms` (a signature's DocMDP record, not a reader setting) and says so in a note beside `/Lang` and `/PieceInfo`. After, same fixtures, same probe, same dpi: meta `/Info` **0 → absent**, `/Metadata` **absent**, card **`Removed 8 properties, XMP packet.`**, `/Lang (en)` kept, ink **253287 → 253287** and text **1792 → 1792** on 4 pages; control `/Info` **absent** over **11** counted keys, `/Lang en-GB`, both `/PieceInfo`s, `/MarkInfo`, `/Perms`, 12 form keys, ink **10048 → 10048**, text **98 → 98**, 1 annot, 2 outline entries, 2 pages — the drawn `/Title` (`Acme Confidential Title`) still on the sheet, only its dictionary entry gone. A file with no metadata at all (`scanned.pdf`, no `/Info`, no `/Metadata`) says **`Nothing was removed… the output is the same document`** and the output is a **clean rewrite** (105 987 → 105 572 B, no `/Info` invented, ink **99905 → 99905**, 3 pages). Report half, looked at: `view-metadata` over both stripped files reads `INFO DICTIONARY: absent`, every property and `/Trapped` `not set`, `XMP PACKET: absent`, `LANGUAGE: en-GB`, `PIECE INFO: declared catalogue (keys: AcmeJob); page 1 (keys: Acme)` — the pane and the file agree. **Red without the fix: 4 failed / 3 passed** (`tests/pdf/security-bytes.test.ts`, the whole-dictionary case, the bystander case, the `/Lang`/`/Perms` case and the message count; the three green in both states are the controls — a no-`/Info` file, a second run, the reader-settings-off flip). **Left open, measured:** `/PageMode` (a sidebar preference) is still billed as "page and zoom memory" while an `/OpenAction` GoTo destination — the actual last-page record — is left in place; `/Perms` is kept, so a certified file keeps its badge but nothing re-signs it after its `/Info` changes. | `src/lib/pdf/core/metadata.ts` (`stripInfo`), `src/lib/pdf/tools/security.ts` (the card's copy, `/Perms`), `tests/pdf/security-bytes.test.ts`, `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` (`SHOT_FULL`) |
| `validate-signature-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **A signature value that had been altered read "All 1 signature(s) verify", and neither a changed byte nor an unsigned tail was located.** Measured first on the dev build with `docs/research/validate-signature-probe.mjs` (upload, "Validate signatures", screenshot + report text), fixtures made by signing `/tmp/triage/colour.pdf` through `docs/research/verify-digital-sign-run.mjs` with the self-signed `CN=BrowserSnip Triage` (`/tmp/triage/in/signer-{cert,key}.pem`) and judged on the same files by `openssl cms -verify -binary -noverify` over the bytes `/ByteRange` names and by `pdfsig` (poppler 26.10). **The oracle table, before:** a valid 21 817 B file — app `All 1 signature(s) verify`, no byte-range row at all; openssl `CMS Verification successful`; pdfsig `Signature is Valid.` / `Total document signed`. Unsigned — app `No digital signature found`; pdfsig exit 2 `does not contain any signatures`. Byte 1000 changed — app `No — the file was modified after signing` with **no offset**; openssl exit 4; pdfsig `Digest Mismatch.` **One byte of the `encryptedDigest` changed inside `/Contents`, covered bytes untouched:** app `All 1 signature(s) verify`; openssl `RSA lib: …rsa_verify_directly… verification failure`; pdfsig `Signature is Invalid.` — a **false valid**, because only the stored message-digest attribute was compared and the RSA value over the signed attributes was never checked. **60 bytes appended after signing:** app `All 1 … the document has not changed since signing`; openssl OK (it only sees the named ranges); pdfsig `Signature is Valid.` + `Not total document signed` — partial coverage unflagged. **`/SubFilter /adbe.x509.rsa_sha1`** (a real raw-PKCS#1 signature `openssl dgst -sha256 -verify` answers `Verified OK`): app `Unparsed DER bytes remain after ASN.1 parsing`, SubFilter never named. **AES-256 re-encrypted signed file:** the same DER sentence, with no word "encrypted". The self-signed certificate was printed but not named as such. Already right: the unsigned sentence, and two signatures (fields `Signature1`/`Signature2`) were reported **per signature** (`1 of 2 verify`, first No, second Yes) — same verdicts as `pdfsig` (#1 `Digest Mismatch`, #2 `Valid`).<br>**Cause and fix, in `core/signature.ts`:** `verifyOne` now (1) verifies the RSA value over the DER of the authenticated attributes against the signer certificate's public key (`RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5`, the SET re-encoded as RFC 5652 signs it) and reports `signatureVerifies` — the false valid above becomes No; (2) keeps every signed range and computes the leading/trailing bytes outside them, the `/ByteRange`-vs-`/Contents` gap, and whether the ranges start at 0 and end at EOF; (3) reads `/SubFilter`, handles `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` through its `/Cert` (naming the missing-`/Cert` case instead of a DER parser message), and marks a filter it has no rule for; (4) flags subject == issuer as self-signed. The tool refuses to guess on an encrypted input (`Encrypted — the signature values cannot be checked until the file is unlocked`, screenshot), prints `Signed range` (e.g. `bytes 0–3863 and 20248–21816 of 21877 — 60 byte(s) after the last signed byte are not covered`), `Signature value`, and on a digest mismatch the exact spans (`No — … the change is inside 0–3863 and 20248–21816`), and warns on partial coverage, self-signed, expired and not-yet-valid certificates. **After, same fixtures, same probe:** valid — two Yes rows, `the whole document`, `/adbe.pkcs7.detached`, message unchanged in meaning plus the self-signed warning; tampered — `0 of 1 … 1 were broken`, row names `0–3863 and 20248–21816`; altered value — `0 of 1 … 1 had a signature value that does not match the certificate`, `Signature value: No`, `Byte range intact: Yes`; appended — `All 1 verify against the bytes they cover, but 1 does not cover the whole file`, `<60> byte(s) after the last signed byte are not covered`; raw PKCS#1 — `/adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` named with the missing `/Cert`; encrypted — the encrypted sentence; unsigned still `No digital signature found`; twice still per signature. Every screenshot looked at, one in dark mode (`docs/research/shots/validate-signature-{valid,tampered,forged-value,appended,unsigned,dark}.png`). **Red without the fix: 3 failed / 1 passed** with `core/signature.ts` and `tools/security.ts` stashed (the green one in both states is the already-correct per-signature case). Full suite 3 436 passed / 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0. **Left open, measured:** the certificate chain is still not built to a root and revocation is still not checked (named on the card), so "verifies" means signature-vs-certificate, not trust; a `/SubFilter` it has no rule for is named and its digest is still checked, but the value is only verified when it is PKCS#7/raw-RSA; a raw signature with a `/Cert` was not re-measured (the crafted fixture carries none), and a PKCS#7 with no authenticated attributes still reports "no message-digest attribute". | `src/lib/pdf/core/signature.ts` (`signatureVerifies`, `signedRanges`, coverage/gap, `/SubFilter`, `/Cert`, self-signed), `src/lib/pdf/tools/security.ts` (the encrypted refusal, the rows, warnings and copy), `tests/pdf/edge-security.test.ts` (4 pins), `docs/research/validate-signature-probe.mjs`, `docs/research/shots/validate-signature-*.png` |
| `timestamp-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **No timestamp had ever been produced in a browser, and three independent defects each hid the other two.** Measured first on the dev build through `docs/research/timestamp-round.mjs` (upload the file `digital-sign-pdf` had just written, write the authority, read it back out of the DOM, press `Add timestamp`, screenshot, bytes taken off the page's own blob) and judged by `docs/research/timestamp-read.py`, which reads `/ByteRange`, the signature value, the token and the pages out of the produced file. **Before, in this order:** the default authority `http://timestamp.digicert.com` failed with `Failed to fetch` — the tab's own console says why, `blocked by CORS policy: Response to preflight request doesn't pass access control check: No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' header` (curl: `501` on `OPTIONS`, no CORS header on the `POST` either; FreeTSA `403`, Sectigo / SwissSign / Entrust `200` and no header — only `rfc3161.ai.moda` answers both with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and the responder behind it is DigiCert's own, policy `2.16.840.1.114412.7.1`); pointed at that one, the run answered **`could not be reached (HTTP 400)`** — a *reachable* authority turning the request down, called unreachable — because **the `TimeStampReq` was not RFC 3161**: the digest sat beside the version with no `messageImprint` wrapper, so `openssl ts -query` stopped on `wrong tag … Type=X509_ALGOR`; and the body was handed to `fetch` as the binary string `forge` returns, which the browser encodes as **UTF-8**, so every DER octet above `0x7f` left as two bytes (`400` on the string body, `200` on the same bytes as a `Uint8Array`, measured against the live authority). The message was also printed twice over, the core's complete sentence wrapped inside the tool's, and the imprint was taken over the `messageDigest` attribute — the digest of the document — where a signature time-stamp imprints the **signature value** (ETSI EN 319 142-1 §6.3).<br>**After, same fixture, same probe, a token in hand** (`/tmp/r66/after-ts.pdf`, 21 818 B — the same length as its source, because only `/Contents` is patched): request accepted, token **5 998 B**, `openssl ts -reply -text` parses it (`Status: Granted`, `Time stamp: Nov 16 19:21:54 2026 GMT`, the card reading the same `2026-11-16T19:21:54.000Z`), `messageImprint` = **sha256 of the 128-byte signature value** (`a83fc395…`) and **not** the document digest, and `openssl ts -verify -token_in … -data <signature value> -CAfile <token chain + DigiCert Assured ID Root CA>` → **`Verification: OK`**, while the control over the covered bytes answers `message imprint mismatch` — the stamp is bound to this signature and not to the pages. **Nothing was replaced:** the token is an *unsigned* attribute (`1.2.840.113549.1.9.16.2.14`), `/ByteRange` is byte-identical to the source's `[0 3864 20248 1570]`, `bytes_outside_ranges` **0**, `covers_to_eof` true, `openssl cms -verify` over the covered bytes `CMS Verification successful`, `pdfsig` `Signature is Valid. / Total document signed`, page count 4, text 1 913 chars and ink `49084 / 49569 / 0 / 142563` **equal page for page** to the source. `validate-signature-pdf` on the timestamped file: `All 1 signature(s) verify`, `bytes 0–3863 and 20248–21817 of 21818 — the whole document`. With DigiCert blocked the refusal is one sentence naming one address and no file is written; an unsigned file is still refused with `This PDF has no digital signature to timestamp. Run Digital Signature on it first…` before the network is touched. Copy went by the same measure: the default is now an authority a page can read, and the help and notes say that CORS — not the command line — is the constraint.<br>**Red without the fix: 8 failed / 19 passed** over the two files with `signature.ts` and `security.ts` stashed (the request an `openssl ts -query` accepts, the byte body, the imprint target, the attested time read from `TSTInfo.genTime` instead of this machine's clock, the nonce echo, the HTTP-400 wording, the single-sentence failure, and the core case). Full suite 3 441 passed / 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `npm run typecheck:tests` 0, `npx vite build` 0. Screen half, looked at: `docs/research/shots/timestamp-1-result.png` (the result card over the drawn sheet, `21.3 KB → 21.3 KB · unchanged`, `PAGES 4 → 4`, `Timestamped at … by rfc3161.ai.moda.`), `…-2-dark.png` (dark mode, same numbers, tokens only, paper white), `…-3-digicert-cors.png` (the CORS refusal, one sentence) and `…-4-unsigned.png` (the refusal of an unsigned file); no console error in the successful runs. **Left open, measured:** (1) `validate-signature-pdf` reports on the signature and says **nothing** about the token, so a user cannot see the stamp from inside the app — a row there is a cross-tool change; (2) the `/Contents` placeholder must fit the token, and a TSA that returns a bigger one is refused by length with the signature untouched, not retried; (3) `timestamp.digicert.com`, `freetsa.org`, `timestamp.sectigo.com`, `tsa.swisssign.net` and `timestamp.entrust.net` all remain unusable from a tab until they send CORS headers — measured, not guessed — so a user who pastes one of them gets the `could not be reached` sentence, which is the truth for this page. | `src/lib/pdf/core/signature.ts` (`createMessageImprint`, the byte body, the imprint over the signature value, `TSTInfo` reading of `genTime`/nonce, the reachable-vs-refused wording), `src/lib/pdf/tools/security.ts` (`DEFAULT_TSA_URL` + its measured table, the notes and help, `timestampFailure`), `tests/pdf/{timestamp-chain,edge-signature-core}.test.ts` (8 pins), `docs/research/timestamp-round.mjs`, `docs/research/timestamp-read.py`, `docs/research/shots/timestamp-*` |
| `jpg-to-pdf` | fixed (dev :5271; not deployed) | **The route opened on A4 and had no way to say "just the picture", and one wrong file killed the batch.** A 420 × 300 JPEG landed 483 × 345 pt inside a 595.28 × 841.89 sheet (`rail-tool-bytes.mjs`, PyMuPDF), while the *card* printed `1 file(s) left out.` on one valid file. Cause: every raster preset took the shared `PAGE_FIELD` (default `a4`, no hug entry, 56 pt margin), and `decodeImageBatch`'s refusal collector was handed the `warnings` array that already began with `RASTER_LOSS`, so the count was never zero. After: page is `420.0 × 300.0` with the image filling it, message clean, the orientation control hidden under `fit` (`when`), and a PNG beside a JPEG is left out **by name while the JPEG converts**. Red without the fix: 2 failed / 1 passed; 3 green with it; 3 448 suite green, `tsc -b`/`typecheck:tests`/`vite build` 0. Evidence `docs/research/jpg-to-pdf-round.md`. Left open: a 20-file batch prints 20 warning lines; `orientation` is still passed as `'portrait'` under `fit` where nothing reads it. | `tools/convert-to.ts` (the preset factory, its field set, the collector) + `tests/pdf/convert-to-bytes.test.ts` |
| `png-to-pdf` | verified-good (measured at :5271; not deployed) | **The page was already right; the card never said the two things that matter for a transparent PNG.** Measured on the dev build with a 200 × 200 PNG, alpha 0 outside a red disc: the produced file carries an `/SMask` (mask **0** at the corners, **255** in the disc) and paints a full-sheet `1 1 1 rg … f` paper rectangle before the `Do`, so the see-through part renders **`(255,255,255)`** — the page's own white — and the disc **`(220,30,40)`** exact, over white and over a dark surround (`png-to-pdf-round.mjs`, PyMuPDF, screenshots looked at). Controls: the opaque PNG has **no** `/SMask` and its 200 × 200 RGB is **byte-identical** to the source; a 16-bit ramp decodes monotonic 0→128→255 (max deviation 1) and a palette PNG keeps every opaque pixel. A JPEG named `.png` is refused by name (`is a JPEG image, not a PNG image`), so is a PNG named `.jpg`, and a PNG beside a JPEG prints `1 PNG image(s) → 1 page(s). 1 file(s) left out.` with the JPEG named. What was wrong was the copy: notes said nothing about transparency or density, and the only `72 dpi` line sat in a field `help` the bar layout does not render — while a 300 dpi `pHYs` PNG produced a **300 × 150 pt** (4.17 × 2.08 in) page. After: both facts are notes **and** result-card warnings, page added at 72 dpi is stated, eyeballed light and dark, bytes unchanged (pages 1–5 pixel-identical). Red without the fix: 3/8 notes stash, 3/8 `background:'#ffffff'`, 1/8 `pack.ts` default `'transparent'`; 8 green with it. Left open: density is stated, not applied; the `SIZE` row counts refused bytes. Evidence `docs/research/png-to-pdf-round.md`. | `tools/convert-to.ts` (preset notes + result warnings) + `tests/pdf/png-to-pdf-alpha.test.ts`, `docs/research/png-to-pdf-round.mjs` |
| `bmp-to-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not yet deployed) | **The three reading traps were already right; a BMP cut short was converted anyway, with rows nobody had drawn.** Measured first on the dev build with `docs/research/bmp-to-pdf-round.mjs` (multi-file upload of hand-built BMPs, the whole `innerText` off the card, every created blob captured, screenshots looked at) and judged by PyMuPDF on the produced pages against the same fixture decoded by Pillow. **Before, the reader was sound where BMP usually lies:** a 24-bit **bottom-up** 243 × 160 file (white block top-left, red/green/blue bands) rendered **0 mismatched pixels** the right way up, its **negative-height top-down** twin the same **0**, an **8-bit palette** file kept its palette colours exactly (red 220,30,40 / green 30,160,60 / blue 30,60,200 — not greyscale) and a **1-bit** file is black and white; each page was `243.0 × 160.0 pt`, one point per pixel. **Before, the header was not checked at all:** the same file cut after 10 of its 160 rows embedded as a complete 243 × 160 page — the 10 real rows pixel-exact, **150 white rows Chrome invented** at the top — and the card said `5 BMP image(s) → 5 page(s)` with no warning (Pillow calls that file `truncated`). Refusals that were already right: `"actually-jpeg.bmp" is a JPEG image, not a BMP image — left out.` and the same for a PNG body, the rest of the batch still converting; a real BMP on `jpg-to-pdf` is refused the same way. **Cause:** `decodeImage` sent BMP straight to `createImageBitmap`/`<img>`; Blink paints the rows it has and pads the rest, and nothing compared `bfOffBits` + stride × rows against the file length, although the BMP header states that byte count. **After, same fixtures, same probe:** `bmpPixelArray` reads the header (INFO-family only; a header it does not model is left to the browser) and refuses a file that stops short, points its pixels past its own end, or points them inside its own header: `"cut.bmp" could not be decoded: its header describes 117174 byte(s) of pixels for a 243 x 160 picture but the file stops after 73254 — the 60 row(s) at its top would come out blank — left out.` The four complete files stay **0 mismatched pixels** at `243.0 × 160.0 pt`, the 10-row file is refused by name while the batch converts, and the controls are still accepted: `bfSize` 0, `biSizeImage` 0, 1 024 bytes appended, a 32-bit BI_BITFIELDS file (offset 66), a BITMAPCOREHEADER, and a BMP `encodeBmp` wrote itself. Refusals also printed **twice** on the card (the runner promotes a logged line containing "could not" into `warnings`, so the pushed sentence *and* `cut.bmp was left out: <the same sentence>` both rendered); the refusal paths no longer log, and each now appears once. Screen half: `bmp-to-pdf` result card and thumbnails match the file page for page, one in dark mode. **Red without the fix: 6 failed / 79 passed** with `core/images.ts` stashed (the note test 1/14 and the duplicate test 1/14 separately). Full suite 3 467 passed / 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0. **Left open, measured:** `image-to-pdf` still throws on the first undecodable file instead of collecting it, so a cut BMP in its batch ends the run (the sentence is right, the batch is not); a BMP whose *palette* index runs past the palette is still whatever Chrome invents; `bfSize` is never trusted, so a file that declares a smaller `bfSize` than its pixels is not flagged; `pHYs`-style density in a BMP header is still stated, not applied. | `src/lib/pdf/core/images.ts` (`bmpPixelArray`, `assertBmpComplete`, the `image/bmp` case), `src/lib/pdf/tools/convert-to.ts` (`BMP_TRUNCATED` note, the refusal log lines), `tests/pdf/bmp-to-pdf-header.test.ts` + `tests/pdf/convert-to-harness.ts` (`bmpBytes` is now a complete file, `bmpTruncate`, signed height), `docs/research/bmp-to-pdf-round.mjs` |
| `webp-to-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not deployed) | **The reader was right in every trap the round was ordered around; what it did not read was the file's own length, and a WebP cut short became a blank page.** Measured first on the dev build with `docs/research/webp-to-pdf-round.mjs` (six upload cases, the whole `innerText` off the card, every created blob captured, screenshots looked at) and judged by PyMuPDF on the produced pages against the same fixtures decoded by Pillow. **Before, the decode was sound:** a lossy and a lossless 400 x 240 WebP each embedded at `400.0 x 240.0 pt` with **0 mismatched pixels** (`>2` on any channel) against Pillow; a 200 x 200 lossless WebP with alpha 0 outside a red disc kept the alpha as an `/SMask` (mask **0** corners, **255** disc) over a full-sheet `1 1 1 rg … f` rectangle, rendering `(255,255,255)` there and `(220,30,40)` in the disc — **0 mismatched** against the source composited over white, the PNG round's shape exactly; a JPEG or a PNG body under a `.webp` name is refused by name once each while the real WebP converts. **Before, two things the card never said:** an **animated** 4-frame WebP gave one page (frame 0 exactly, 0 mismatched; frames 1-3 at 9 262 / 9 569 / 9 449) with the card at `1 WEBP image(s) -> 1 page(s)` and no note, against a promise reading “every WEBP you add becomes its own PDF page”; and a WebP **cut at 40 %** of its bytes converted as a complete 400 x 240 page that was **96 000 of 96 000 pixels white**, the card saying `2 WEBP image(s) -> 2 page(s)` (Pillow refuses that file, `could not create decoder object`). **Cause:** nothing read the RIFF `File Size` at byte 4 nor any chunk size — the two places the container states how long the file must be — and nothing counted `ANMF` frames, so `createImageBitmap` was asked about a file it answered with a bitmap of nothing. **After, same fixtures, same probe:** `webpShortfall` walks the declared length and the chunk sizes (only `RIFF`/`WEBP` files judged, only the file's own numbers used) in the `image/webp` case of `decodeImage`, beside the BMP guard — `"cut.webp" could not be decoded: its header says the file is 1428 byte(s) long, but the file stops after 571 — 857 byte(s) of the picture are missing — left out.`, page count down to the one good file (400 x 240, 96 000 inked px, pixel-identical to the lossy control), `1 file(s) left out.` on the card. `webpFrameCount` feeds a per-file warning — `"anim.webp" is an animated WebP with 4 frames — only the first one is on its page, and the PDF will not play the rest.` — and two notes replace the silence (header checked; first frame only), plus the transparency note the format now earns. **Controls that must still pass:** the same bytes complete convert at `400 x 240`; a chunk overrunning a rewritten header is refused by the chunk sentence alone (isolates the walk from the length check); **64 appended bytes are accepted** — `1 WEBP image(s) -> 1 page(s)`, all inked — because the spec lets a reader ignore trailing data; a still WebP gets no animation line; a single-frame animation none either; a real WebP on `jpg-to-pdf` is still refused by name. Screen half: the card and thumbnails match the file page for page, and the alpha page was rendered over a dark surround and looked at (white paper, red disc); one dark-mode screenshot. **Red without the fix: 6 failed / 7 passed** with `core/images.ts` and `tools/convert-to.ts` stashed; 13 green with them. Full suite green, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0. **Left open, measured:** `image-to-pdf` throws on the first undecodable file rather than collecting it and gets no animation line (it passes no `notices`); one page per frame would need `ImageDecoder` and the spec's canvas assembly (frame rects, blend, disposal) — this round states the first-frame behaviour instead; a `VP8X` canvas larger than its frames is not cross-checked. Evidence `docs/research/webp-to-pdf-round.md`. | `src/lib/pdf/core/images.ts` (`webpShortfall`, `assertWebpComplete`, `webpFrameCount`, the `image/webp` case), `src/lib/pdf/tools/convert-to.ts` (`WEBP_TRUNCATED` / `WEBP_FIRST_FRAME` notes, the `notices` collector, the transparency warning), `tests/pdf/webp-to-pdf-reader.test.ts`, `docs/research/webp-to-pdf-round.mjs` |
| `heic-to-pdf` | fixed (dev build `:5271`; not deployed — the refusal is the whole behaviour on this build) | **Nothing was ever invented here; the words at the point of refusal pointed the wrong way.** Measured first on the dev build with `docs/research/colour-rail.mjs` and `docs/research/heic-to-pdf-round-spec.json` (8 runs, one Chromium, records kept verbatim in `…-before.jsonl`), fixtures a real 5 235 B `ftypheic` 400 × 300 file and a 1 486 B one plus three renamed files: a real HEIC was refused by name (`"real.heic" could not be decoded: … this browser has no decoder for HEIC/HEIF … Convert the file to PNG or JPEG, or open it in Safari or Chrome 130+.`) — **0 blobs, no PDF, no console error**, so the bytes matched the surface; but the hint under it read `Re-save the file as HEIC, or use Images to PDF…`, which is what the file already is and leads back to the same refusal. A JPEG or PNG named `.heic` was refused as what it is, with the format printed **twice** (`not a HEIC/HEIF image or a HEIF image`); a real HEIC renamed `.jpg` is sniffed at byte 4 and refused on either route (`is a HEIC/HEIF image, not a JPEG image`); and on a HEIC + JPEG batch both are named and nothing converts — but the HEIC card painted the browser's **torn-image glyph** (an image card always has an object URL, so its own placeholder was unreachable) and the second refused card still read `READING` over a `counting pages…` no run was going to fill, because only the first name in the joined sentence got `setFileState`. **Four causes, fixed at each:** the version-named advice became `or open it in a browser whose platform can decode it`; a `decodeHint` (used once a decode has failed, raised sentence included) replaces the wrong-format hint; `advertisedFormats` drops a label contained in another; a failed thumbnail falls back to the dashed format placeholder and every blamed file is stopped. **After, same fixtures, same probe:** the decoder sentence names the platform, the hint is `Re-save the photo as PNG or JPEG, or open this page where the system can decode HEIC (Safari on macOS or iOS), then try again.`, the duplicate is gone (`not a HEIC/HEIF image.`), both batch cards are **STOPPED** with `pages unknown` and the HEIC card a `HEIC` placeholder while the JPEG keeps its picture — still **0 blobs** in both states and 0 console errors, with the counts (`2 IN THIS JOB`, `Nothing changed yet`) honest throughout. Controls: the good JPEG card keeps its `<img>` (`data-card-thumb="image"`), the sniffer still answers `image/jpeg` for JPEG bytes, and `jpg-to-pdf` over HEIC bytes still refuses with the HEIC's own name. Dark mode read, tokens only. **Red without the fix: 8 failed / 6 passed** with `src/` stashed; 14 green with it. Full suite 3 492 passed / 1 skipped, `tsc -b` 0, `typecheck:tests` 0, `vite build` 0. **Left open, measured:** `image-to-pdf` throws on the first undecodable file rather than collecting it (family-wide); any `ftyp` sniffs as HEIC/HEIF, so an `.mp4` is named that by both tools, and narrowing the brand list would make the no-brand fallback say `PNG` (written up, not guessed); a browser that actually decodes HEIC could not be measured here, so the promise is qualified rather than shown working; the `counting pages…` line on a *successful* image run is still there (the count is never set for image inputs). Evidence `docs/research/heic-to-pdf-round.md`. | `src/lib/pdf/tools/convert-to.ts` (`decodeHint`, `HEIC_DECODE_HINT`, `advertisedFormats`, the conditional note), `src/lib/pdf/core/images.ts` (the platform sentence), `src/components/tools/pdf/shared/FileCards.tsx` (broken thumbnail, stopped page count), `src/hooks/usePdfTool.ts` (`filesAtFault`), `tests/pdf/heic-to-pdf-claims.test.ts`, `tests/pdf/heic-to-pdf-refusal-screen.test.tsx`, `docs/research/heic-to-pdf-round.{md,json}`, `docs/research/heic-to-pdf-round-{before,after}.jsonl`, `docs/research/shots/heic-071-*` |
